5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तस्यास्तु tasyāstu → तस्याः + तु
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • नयनत्रितयं जज्ञे nayanatritayaṃ jajñe → नयनत्रितयम् + जज्ञे
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • नयनत्रितयम् nayanatritayam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: नयन + त्रितय · Vigraha: नयनानां त्रितयम् nayanānāṃ tritayam
    Literally “the triad of eyes”; in the verse: her three eyes; त्रितय is त्रि + the taddhita तयप् ‘a group of three’
  • पावकतेजसा pāvakatejasā — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: पावक + तेजस् · Vigraha: पावकस्य तेजः, तेन pāvakasya tejaḥ, tena
    Literally “by the radiance of the Purifier”; in the verse: from Agni’s share of the tejas

C. Morphology

  • जज्ञे jajñe — Perfect of √जन्, ātmanepada (ज-ज्ञ्-ए, with loss of the root vowel): ‘was born’. The singular agrees with the collective नयनत्रितयम्.
  • सम्भूताः sambhūtāḥ — Past participle of सम् + √भू used as a finite verb: ‘(were) produced’.
  • प्राजापत्येन prājāpatyena — Taddhita of relation from प्रजापति, ‘belonging to Prajāpati’. See Uncertain.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तस्याः तु दन्ताः प्राजापत्येन तेजसा सम्भूताः। तथा पावकतेजसा नयनत्रितयं जज्ञे।

tasyāḥ tu dantāḥ prājāpatyena tejasā sambhūtāḥ | tathā pāvakatejasā nayanatritayaṃ jajñe |

Two clauses, each with a subject, a verb (participle; perfect) and the source in the instrumental.

6Translation

LiteralHer teeth came into being by the radiance of Prajāpati; likewise the triad of eyes was born by the radiance of Fire.

ReadableHer teeth were formed from the radiance of Prajāpati, and her three eyes, likewise, from the radiance of Fire.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe teeth come from Prajāpati’s tejas and the three eyes from Agni’s. The Goddess has three eyes, like Śiva.

Traditional interpretationThree eyes are a mark the Goddess shares with Śiva, whose third eye is traditionally the eye of fire. Here all three of her eyes are formed from Agni’s tejas.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: प्राजापत्येन तेजसा: which Prajāpati is meant is not stated. Since Brahmā’s tejas has already given the feet (2.16), the Prajāpati here is probably another (one of the lords of creatures, such as Dakṣa) or the Prajāpatis as a class; it is translated simply as ‘Prajāpati’. The reading itself is not in doubt.

2.18Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

भ्रुवौ च संध्ययोस्तेजः श्रवणावनिलस्य च।
अन्येषां चैव देवानां सम्भवस्तेजसां शिवा ॥ १८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

भ्रुवौ | च | संध्ययोः | तेजः | श्रवणौ | अनिलस्य | चअन्येषाम् | च | एव | देवानाम् | सम्भवः | तेजसाम् | शिवा

3IAST

As recitedbhruvau ca saṃdhyayostejaḥ śravaṇāvanilasya ca |
anyeṣāṃ caiva devānāṃ sambhavastejasāṃ śivā ||

Separatedbhruvau | ca | saṃdhyayoḥ | tejaḥ | śravaṇau | anilasya | ca
anyeṣām | ca | eva | devānām | sambhavaḥ | tejasām | śivā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
भ्रुवौbhruvauthe two eyebrowsf. nom. dual of भ्रू (ū-stem)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
संध्ययोःsaṃdhyayoḥof the two twilightsf. gen. dual of संध्या (dawn and dusk, personified); depends on तेजः
तेजःtejaḥ(were) the radiancen. nom. sg. of तेजस्; predicate nominative
श्रवणौśravaṇauthe two earsm. nom. dual of श्रवण
अनिलस्यanilasyaof Anila (the Wind)m. gen. sg. of अनिल; with तेजः understood
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
अन्येषाम्anyeṣāmof the otherpronominal adjective अन्य, m. gen. pl.; qualifies देवानाम्
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
एवevaalso, indeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
देवानाम्devānāmgodsm. gen. pl. of देव; depends on तेजसाम्
सम्भवःsambhavaḥthe coming-into-being, the issuem. nom. sg. of सम्भव (सम् + √भू + अप्); predicate of शिवा
तेजसाम्tejasāmof the radiancesn. gen. pl. of तेजस्; depends on सम्भवः
शिवाśivāŚivā, the auspicious one (the Goddess)f. nom. sg. of शिवा; subject

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • संध्ययोस्तेजः saṃdhyayostejaḥ → संध्ययोः + तेजः
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s; likewise सम्भवस्तेजसाम्.
  • श्रवणावनिलस्य śravaṇāvanilasya → श्रवणौ + अनिलस्य
    vowel sandhi (āv) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    Final au before a vowel becomes āv. Here the v is kept (the optional loss of y/v, 8.3.19, applies to the ay/av from e/o, not to āv).
  • चैव caiva → च + एव
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + e → ai.
  • तेजसां शिवा tejasāṃ śivā → तेजसाम् + शिवा
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

C. Morphology

  • भ्रुवौ bhruvau — Nominative dual of the feminine root-noun भ्रू ‘brow’: before vowel endings ū becomes uv (भ्रुव्-औ).
  • तेजः tejaḥ — The construction changes in this verse: instead of an instrumental of source (तेजसा) the radiance stands in the nominative as a predicate, ‘the brows (were) the twilights’ radiance’.
  • सम्भवः sambhavaḥ — Action noun from सम् + √भू, ‘coming together, origin’. Predicated of शिवा: ‘Śivā was the issue of the radiances’. The masculine noun and the feminine subject do not need to agree: they are subject and predicate.

D. Anvaya — prose order

भ्रुवौ च संध्ययोः तेजः, श्रवणौ च अनिलस्य (तेजः)। अन्येषां च एव देवानां तेजसां सम्भवः शिवा (अभूत्)।

bhruvau ca saṃdhyayoḥ tejaḥ, śravaṇau ca anilasya (tejaḥ) | anyeṣāṃ ca eva devānāṃ tejasāṃ sambhavaḥ śivā (abhūt) |

Two nominal sentences of the form ‘X (was) Y’s radiance’, then a summary sentence with शिवा as subject and सम्भवः as predicate.

6Translation

LiteralAnd the brows (were) the radiance of the two Twilights, and the ears (that) of the Wind; and Śivā was the issue of the radiances of the other gods as well.

ReadableHer brows were the radiance of the two Twilights, her ears that of the Wind; and so the auspicious Goddess arose from the radiance of all the other gods as well.

tr renders सम्भवः … शिवा as ‘the auspicious Goddess arose from’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe description ends with a summary: the rest of the gods contributed too, and the being who arose from all their radiance is called Śivā, ‘the auspicious’. The two Twilights (संध्ये, dawn and dusk) are counted among the givers.

Traditional interpretationŚivā is the feminine of śiva, ‘auspicious, benign’, and a name of the Goddess as Śiva’s consort. This is the first time the text uses it: the being formed from anger-born radiance is named ‘the auspicious one’.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the same name, शिवा (name 53), and श्रीशिवा (name 998). The name is common to many Śākta texts; the two works are distinct.

2.19Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ततः समस्तदेवानां तेजोराशिसमुद्भवाम्।
तां विलोक्य मुदं प्रापुरमरा महिषार्दिताः ॥ १९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ततः | समस्तदेवानाम् | तेजोराशिसमुद्भवाम्ताम् | विलोक्य | मुदम् | प्रापुः | अमराः | महिषार्दिताः

3IAST

As recitedtataḥ samastadevānāṃ tejorāśisamudbhavām |
tāṃ vilokya mudaṃ prāpuramarā mahiṣārditāḥ ||

Separatedtataḥ | samastadevānām | tejorāśisamudbhavām
tām | vilokya | mudam | prāpuḥ | amarāḥ | mahiṣārditāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
समस्तदेवानाम्samastadevānāmof all the godsm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on तेजोराशि- inside the next compound
तेजोराशिसमुद्भवाम्tejorāśisamudbhavāmher who had arisen from the mass of radiancef. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ताम्
ताम्tāmherpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; object of विलोक्य
विलोक्यvilokyahaving seenवि + √लोक् (curādi, 10 ‘to look’) + ल्यप् (absolutive with a prefix)
मुदम्mudamjoyf. acc. sg. of मुद् (consonant stem); object of प्रापुः
प्रापुःprāpuḥattained, feltप्र + √आप् (svādi, 5P ‘to obtain’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
अमराःamarāḥthe immortals, the godsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
महिषार्दिताःmahiṣārditāḥoppressed by Mahiṣam. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अमराः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तेजोराशि tejorāśi → तेजस् + राशि
    visarga sandhi inside a compound · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    The s of तेजस् becomes रु and then u before the voiced r; a + u → o.
  • प्रापुरमरा महिषार्दिताः prāpuramarā mahiṣārditāḥ → प्रापुः + अमराः + महिषार्दिताः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    After u the visarga is sounded r before the vowel a. -āḥ before the voiced m loses its visarga (via y, which is dropped before a consonant).
  • महिषार्दिताः mahiṣārditāḥ → महिष + अर्दिताः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • समस्तदेवानाम् samastadevānām — karmadhāraya
    Members: समस्त + देव · Vigraha: समस्ताः देवाः, तेषाम् samastāḥ devāḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of the all-gods”; in the verse: of all the gods together
  • तेजोराशिसमुद्भवाम् tejorāśisamudbhavām — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (तेजोराशि: तेजसां राशिः)
    Members: तेजोराशि + समुद्भव · Vigraha: तेजोराशेः समुद्भवः यस्याः सा, ताम् tejorāśeḥ samudbhavaḥ yasyāḥ sā, tām
    Literally “she whose origin is the heap of radiance”; in the verse: born of the gods’ gathered tejas
    Alternative: Also possible as a pañcamī tatpuruṣa with an adjectival समुद्भवा ‘arisen from the mass of radiance’; the bahuvrīhi is the regular way such a compound qualifies a feminine.
  • अमराः amarāḥ — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ (न) + मर · Vigraha: न मराः na marāḥ
    Literally “not mortal”; in the verse: the gods
  • महिषार्दिताः mahiṣārditāḥ — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: महिष + अर्दित · Vigraha: महिषेण अर्दिताः mahiṣeṇa arditāḥ
    Literally “afflicted by Mahiṣa”; in the verse: the gods whom Mahiṣa had oppressed

C. Morphology

  • प्रापुः prāpuḥ — Perfect of प्र + √आप्: the reduplicating a merges with the root’s ā, so the perfect stem is आप्- (3rd pl. आपुः, with the prefix प्रापुः), ‘they obtained’. मुदं प्राप् is the idiom ‘to feel joy’.
  • समस्तदेवानाम् samastadevānām — A genitive outside the compound that qualifies one of its members (तेजोराशि ‘the mass of radiance of all the gods’). Strict grammar frowns on this, but Purāṇic and epic verse uses it freely.
  • विलोक्य vilokya — Absolutive of वि + √लोक्, ‘having beheld’; its agent is अमराः, the subject of the main verb.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ततः महिषार्दिताः अमराः समस्तदेवानां तेजोराशिसमुद्भवां तां विलोक्य मुदं प्रापुः।

tataḥ mahiṣārditāḥ amarāḥ samastadevānāṃ tejorāśisamudbhavāṃ tāṃ vilokya mudaṃ prāpuḥ |

Subject (महिषार्दिताः अमराः), an absolutive phrase with its object (तां … विलोक्य), and the main verb with its object (मुदं प्रापुः).

6Translation

LiteralThen, having beheld her, who had arisen from the mass of radiance of all the gods, the immortals, oppressed by Mahiṣa, attained joy.

ReadableThen the gods, so long oppressed by Mahiṣa, looked on her who had arisen from the gathered radiance of them all, and they were filled with joy.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods see the woman formed from their pooled tejas and rejoice. Their joy closes the account of her birth and opens the giving of gifts (2.20–31).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the gods rejoice at something greater than any of them, which is also their own power given back in a new form.

2.20Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शूलं शूलाद्विनिष्कृष्य ददौ तस्यै पिनाकधृक्।
चक्रं च दत्तवान् कृष्णः समुत्पाद्य स्वचक्रतः ॥ २० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शूलम् | शूलात् | विनिष्कृष्य | ददौ | तस्यै | पिनाकधृक्चक्रम् | च | दत्तवान् | कृष्णः | समुत्पाद्य | स्वचक्रतः

3IAST

As recitedśūlaṃ śūlādviniṣkṛṣya dadau tasyai pinākadhṛk |
cakraṃ ca dattavān kṛṣṇaḥ samutpādya svacakrataḥ ||

Separatedśūlam | śūlāt | viniṣkṛṣya | dadau | tasyai | pinākadhṛk
cakram | ca | dattavān | kṛṣṇaḥ | samutpādya | svacakrataḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
शूलम्śūlama tridentn. acc. sg. of शूल; object of विनिष्कृष्य and ददौ
शूलात्śūlātfrom (his own) tridentn. abl. sg. of शूल; ablative of source
विनिष्कृष्यviniṣkṛṣyahaving drawn outवि + निस् + √कृष् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to drag’) + ल्यप् (absolutive with prefixes)
ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3P ‘to give’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.; indirect object
पिनाकधृक्pinākadhṛkthe bearer of Pināka (Śiva)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of ददौ
चक्रम्cakrama discusn. acc. sg. of चक्र; object of दत्तवान्
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
दत्तवान्dattavāngave√दा + क्तवतु (past active participle), m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
कृष्णःkṛṣṇaḥKṛṣṇa (Viṣṇu)m. nom. sg. of कृष्ण; subject of दत्तवान्
समुत्पाद्यsamutpādyahaving producedसम् + उद् + √पद् (causative stem पादि- ‘to bring forth’) + ल्यप्
स्वचक्रतःsvacakrataḥfrom his own discusindeclinable; compound स्वचक्र (see Samāsa) + taddhita तसिल् with ablative sense

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शूलं शूलाद्विनिष्कृष्य śūlaṃ śūlādviniṣkṛṣya → शूलम् + शूलात् + विनिष्कृष्य
    anusvāra; consonant sandhi (voicing) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; final t becomes d before the voiced v.
  • विनिष्कृष्य viniṣkṛṣya → वि + निस् + कृष्य
    consonant sandhi at the prefix · a rule of internal sandhi (the s of निस् becomes ṣ before k after i)
    The prefix निस् before the k of कृष् is written निष्; compare निश्चक्राम (2.10), where it becomes निश् before c.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पिनाकधृक् pinākadhṛk — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: पिनाक + धृक् · Vigraha: पिनाकं धरति इति pinākaṃ dharati iti
    Literally “he who holds Pināka”; in the verse: Śiva, whose bow is called Pināka; -धृक् ‘holding’ is an agent noun used at the end of compounds
  • स्वचक्र svacakra — karmadhāraya
    Members: स्व + चक्र · Vigraha: स्वं चक्रम् svaṃ cakram
    Literally “own discus”; in the verse: Viṣṇu’s own discus, Sudarśana

C. Morphology

  • दत्तवान् dattavān — Past active participle (क्तवतु) of √दा, used as a finite past verb: ‘(he) gave’. The text alternates it with the perfect ददौ through the list of gifts.
  • समुत्पाद्य samutpādya — Absolutive of the causative of सम् + उद् + √पद्, ‘having caused to arise’: each god produces a copy of his own weapon from it and gives the copy.
  • पिनाकधृक् pinākadhṛk — Nominative singular of a consonant-stem agent noun (-धृक्), the same in pause; see Uncertain for its derivation.

D. Anvaya — prose order

पिनाकधृक् शूलात् शूलं विनिष्कृष्य तस्यै ददौ। कृष्णः च स्वचक्रतः चक्रं समुत्पाद्य (तस्यै) दत्तवान्।

pinākadhṛk śūlāt śūlaṃ viniṣkṛṣya tasyai dadau | kṛṣṇaḥ ca svacakrataḥ cakraṃ samutpādya (tasyai) dattavān |

Two parallel sentences: giver, an absolutive phrase (how the weapon was made), the gift, and the verb.

6Translation

LiteralDrawing out a trident from (his own) trident, the bearer of Pināka gave it to her; and Kṛṣṇa, producing a discus from his own discus, gave it.

ReadableŚiva, bearer of the Pināka bow, drew a trident out of his own trident and gave it to her; and Viṣṇu brought forth a discus from his own discus and gave it to her.

tr names the givers Śiva and Viṣṇu for पिनाकधृक् and कृष्ण, and repeats ‘to her’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gifts begin. Each weapon is drawn out of the god’s own weapon: the gods do not give theirs away but make a second one from it. Śiva gives the trident and Viṣṇu the discus.

Traditional interpretationThe dhyāna of the middle carita in the Gita Press pāṭha (recited before 2.1) pictures Mahālakṣmī holding these gifts: trident, discus (सुदर्शन), conch, spear, bow, thunderbolt, bell, staff, noose, rosary, water-pot, sword and shield among them. The chapter supplies the story behind that iconography.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: Kṛṣṇa here is a name of Viṣṇu (the discus-bearer of 2.10), not specifically the avatāra. पिनाकधृक्: the final member -धृक् ‘holding’ is common at the end of compounds; its derivation (from √धृ, or by analogy with forms of √धृष्) is not certain. GRETIL’s Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa carries additional verse material at this point (recension note in corrections.json); the Gita Press text is followed.

2.21Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शङ्खं च वरुणः शक्तिं ददौ तस्यै हुताशनः।
मारुतो दत्तवांश्चापं बाणपूर्णे तथेषुधी ॥ २१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शङ्खम् | च | वरुणः | शक्तिम् | ददौ | तस्यै | हुताशनःमारुतः | दत्तवान् | चापम् | बाणपूर्णे | तथा | इषुधी

3IAST

As recitedśaṅkhaṃ ca varuṇaḥ śaktiṃ dadau tasyai hutāśanaḥ |
māruto dattavāṃścāpaṃ bāṇapūrṇe tatheṣudhī ||

Separatedśaṅkham | ca | varuṇaḥ | śaktim | dadau | tasyai | hutāśanaḥ
mārutaḥ | dattavān | cāpam | bāṇapūrṇe | tathā | iṣudhī

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
शङ्खम्śaṅkhama conchm. acc. sg. of शङ्ख; object (ददौ understood with वरुणः)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
वरुणःvaruṇaḥVaruṇam. nom. sg. of वरुण, lord of the waters; subject
शक्तिम्śaktima spear (śakti)f. acc. sg. of शक्ति; object of ददौ
ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.
हुताशनःhutāśanaḥHutāśana, the eater of oblations (Agni)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of ददौ
मारुतःmārutaḥMāruta, the Windm. nom. sg. of मारुत; subject of दत्तवान्
दत्तवान्dattavāngave√दा + क्तवतु, m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
चापम्cāpama bowm./n. acc. sg. of चाप; object
बाणपूर्णेbāṇapūrṇefull of arrowsf. acc. dual; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies इषुधी
तथाtathāand alsoindeclinable (adverb, here conjunctive)
इषुधीiṣudhītwo quiversf. acc. dual of इषुधि; object of दत्तवान्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मारुतो दत्तवांश्चापं māruto dattavāṃścāpaṃ → मारुतः + दत्तवान् + चापम्
    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (n + c) · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o. Final n before c becomes anusvāra + ś: दत्तवान् चापम् → दत्तवांश्चापम्.
  • तथेषुधी tatheṣudhī → तथा + इषुधी
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + i → e.
  • शङ्खं च śaṅkhaṃ ca → शङ्खम् + च
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; likewise शक्तिं ददौ.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • हुताशनः hutāśanaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: हुत + अशन · Vigraha: हुतम् अशनं यस्य सः hutam aśanaṃ yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose food is the oblation”; in the verse: Agni, the fire that consumes what is offered
    Alternative: Also analysed as an upapada-type tatpuruṣa, हुतम् अश्नाति इति ‘he who eats the oblation’ (अशन as an agent noun); the sense is the same.
  • बाणपूर्णे bāṇapūrṇe — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: बाण + पूर्ण · Vigraha: बाणैः पूर्णे bāṇaiḥ pūrṇe
    Literally “filled with arrows”; in the verse: fully stocked

C. Morphology

  • इषुधी iṣudhī — Accusative dual of इषुधि ‘quiver’ (‘that in which arrows are placed’). The noun is masculine or feminine; the feminine adjective बाणपूर्णे shows it is feminine here. Two quivers, one for each side.
  • बाणपूर्णे bāṇapūrṇe — Feminine accusative dual of the a-stem adjective (-ā stem in the feminine, dual -ए), agreeing with इषुधी.

D. Anvaya — prose order

वरुणः च शङ्खं (तस्यै ददौ), हुताशनः तस्यै शक्तिं ददौ। मारुतः चापं तथा बाणपूर्णे इषुधी दत्तवान्।

varuṇaḥ ca śaṅkhaṃ (tasyai dadau), hutāśanaḥ tasyai śaktiṃ dadau | mārutaḥ cāpaṃ tathā bāṇapūrṇe iṣudhī dattavān |

Three gifts: ददौ is stated once for Varuṇa’s and Agni’s gifts; Māruta’s bow and quivers take दत्तवान्.

6Translation

LiteralAnd Varuṇa (gave) a conch; Hutāśana gave her a spear; Māruta gave a bow and also two quivers full of arrows.

ReadableVaruṇa gave her a conch, and Fire a spear; the Wind gave her a bow and two quivers full of arrows.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThree more gifts: the conch from Varuṇa, lord of the waters (the conch is a sea creature), the spear (śakti) from Agni, and from the Wind a bow and two full quivers.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: each gift fits its giver. The sea gives the conch, fire the spear, and the swift wind the bow and arrows that fly through it.

2.22Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

वज्रमिन्द्रः समुत्पाद्य कुलिशादमराधिपः।
ददौ तस्यै सहस्राक्षो घण्टामैरावताद् गजात् ॥ २२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

वज्रम् | इन्द्रः | समुत्पाद्य | कुलिशात् | अमराधिपःददौ | तस्यै | सहस्राक्षः | घण्टाम् | ऐरावतात् | गजात्

3IAST

As recitedvajramindraḥ samutpādya kuliśādamarādhipaḥ |
dadau tasyai sahasrākṣo ghaṇṭāmairāvatād gajāt ||

Separatedvajram | indraḥ | samutpādya | kuliśāt | amarādhipaḥ
dadau | tasyai | sahasrākṣaḥ | ghaṇṭām | airāvatāt | gajāt

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
वज्रम्vajrama thunderbolt (vajra)m./n. acc. sg. of वज्र; object of समुत्पाद्य and ददौ
इन्द्रःindraḥIndram. nom. sg. of इन्द्र; subject of ददौ
समुत्पाद्यsamutpādyahaving producedसम् + उद् + √पद् (causative) + ल्यप्
कुलिशात्kuliśātfrom (his own) thunderboltm./n. abl. sg. of कुलिश; ablative of source
अमराधिपःamarādhipaḥlord of the immortalsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to इन्द्रः
ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.
सहस्राक्षःsahasrākṣaḥthe thousand-eyed (Indra)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to इन्द्रः
घण्टाम्ghaṇṭāma bellf. acc. sg. of घण्टा; second object of ददौ
ऐरावतात्airāvatātfrom Airāvatam. abl. sg. of ऐरावत, Indra’s elephant; in apposition to गजात्
गजात्gajāt(his) elephantm. abl. sg. of गज; ablative of source

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कुलिशादमराधिपः kuliśādamarādhipaḥ → कुलिशात् + अमराधिपः
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t is voiced to d before a vowel; likewise ऐरावताद् गजात् before the voiced g. Inside the compound, अमर + अधिप → अमराधिप (अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः, 6.1.101).
  • सहस्राक्षो घण्टाम् sahasrākṣo ghaṇṭām → सहस्राक्षः + घण्टाम्
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced gh becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अमराधिपः amarādhipaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: अमर + अधिप · Vigraha: अमराणाम् अधिपः amarāṇām adhipaḥ
    Literally “overlord of the immortals”; in the verse: Indra, king of the gods (the title Mahiṣa had usurped, 2.3)
  • सहस्राक्षः sahasrākṣaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: सहस्र + अक्षि · Vigraha: सहस्रम् अक्षीणि यस्य सः sahasram akṣīṇi yasya saḥ
    Literally “he who has a thousand eyes”; in the verse: Indra; अक्षि becomes अक्ष at the end of a bahuvrīhi (a samāsānta affix)

C. Morphology

  • समुत्पाद्य samutpādya — As in 2.20: absolutive of the causative of सम् + उद् + √पद्. Indra makes a vajra from his own kuliśa (both words mean ‘thunderbolt’).
  • गजात् gajāt — Ablative of source: the bell is taken from Airāvata, the elephant, who wore it. ऐरावतात् and गजात् stand in apposition.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अमराधिपः सहस्राक्षः इन्द्रः कुलिशात् वज्रं समुत्पाद्य तस्यै ददौ, ऐरावतात् गजात् घण्टां (च ददौ)।

amarādhipaḥ sahasrākṣaḥ indraḥ kuliśāt vajraṃ samutpādya tasyai dadau, airāvatāt gajāt ghaṇṭāṃ (ca dadau) |

One subject with two epithets, an absolutive phrase, the verb ददौ, and a second object (घण्टाम्) with its source.

6Translation

LiteralIndra, lord of the immortals, producing a thunderbolt from (his own) thunderbolt, gave it to her, the thousand-eyed one, and a bell from the elephant Airāvata.

ReadableIndra, the thousand-eyed lord of the gods, brought forth a thunderbolt from his own and gave it to her, together with a bell from his elephant Airāvata.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningIndra gives a thunderbolt made from his own and the bell of his elephant Airāvata. The god whose throne Mahiṣa took is named by his full titles: lord of the immortals, the thousand-eyed.

Traditional interpretationThe bell will be used in the battle: its sound stupefies the asuras (2.56, घण्टास्वनविमोहितान्), and the gods later pray that it protect them (11.27).

2.23Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कालदण्डाद्यमो दण्डं पाशं चाम्बुपतिर्ददौ।
प्रजापतिश्चाक्षमालां ददौ ब्रह्मा कमण्डलुम् ॥ २३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

कालदण्डात् | यमः | दण्डम् | पाशम् | च | अम्बुपतिः | ददौप्रजापतिः | च | अक्षमालाम् | ददौ | ब्रह्मा | कमण्डलुम्

3IAST

As recitedkāladaṇḍādyamo daṇḍaṃ pāśaṃ cāmbupatirdadau |
prajāpatiścākṣamālāṃ dadau brahmā kamaṇḍalum ||

Separatedkāladaṇḍāt | yamaḥ | daṇḍam | pāśam | ca | ambupatiḥ | dadau
prajāpatiḥ | ca | akṣamālām | dadau | brahmā | kamaṇḍalum

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
कालदण्डात्kāladaṇḍātfrom (his) staff of deathm. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); ablative of source
यमःyamaḥYamam. nom. sg. of यम, lord of death; subject (ददौ understood)
दण्डम्daṇḍama staffm. acc. sg. of दण्ड; object
पाशम्pāśama noosem. acc. sg. of पाश; object of ददौ
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
अम्बुपतिःambupatiḥthe lord of the waters (Varuṇa)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of ददौ
ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
प्रजापतिःprajāpatiḥPrajāpatim. nom. sg.; compound (प्रजानां पतिः, as in 2.4); subject of ददौ
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
अक्षमालाम्akṣamālāma rosary (of rudrākṣa beads)f. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of ददौ
ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
ब्रह्माbrahmāBrahmām. nom. sg. of ब्रह्मन्; subject of the second ददौ
कमण्डलुम्kamaṇḍaluma water-potm. acc. sg. of कमण्डलु (u-stem); object

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कालदण्डाद्यमो दण्डं kāladaṇḍādyamo daṇḍaṃ → कालदण्डात् + यमः + दण्डम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing); visarga sandhi · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced y; -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.
  • चाम्बुपतिर्ददौ cāmbupatirdadau → च + अम्बुपतिः + ददौ
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    a + a → ā; after i the visarga is sounded r before the voiced d.
  • प्रजापतिश्चाक्षमालां prajāpatiścākṣamālāṃ → प्रजापतिः + च + अक्षमालाम्
    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Visarga before c becomes ś; च + अक्षमालाम्: a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • कालदण्डात् kāladaṇḍāt — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: काल + दण्ड · Vigraha: कालस्य दण्डः, तस्मात् kālasya daṇḍaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally “from the staff of Kāla (Time, Death)”; in the verse: from Yama’s own staff of death
    Alternative: Also a karmadhāraya, कालः एव दण्डः ‘the staff that is death’; either way it is Yama’s own weapon.
  • अम्बुपतिः ambupatiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: अम्बु + पति · Vigraha: अम्बूनां पतिः ambūnāṃ patiḥ
    Literally “lord of the waters”; in the verse: Varuṇa
  • अक्षमालाम् akṣamālām — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: अक्ष + माला · Vigraha: अक्षाणां माला, ताम् akṣāṇāṃ mālā, tām
    Literally “a garland of akṣa beads”; in the verse: a rosary for counting recitations

C. Morphology

  • ब्रह्मा brahmā — Nominative singular of the masculine n-stem ब्रह्मन् (the god Brahmā), as distinct from the neuter ब्रह्म (the Absolute).
  • कमण्डलुम् kamaṇḍalum — Accusative of the u-stem कमण्डलु, the ascetic’s water-pot, Brahmā’s emblem.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यमः कालदण्डात् दण्डं (तस्यै ददौ), अम्बुपतिः च पाशं ददौ। प्रजापतिः च अक्षमालां ददौ, ब्रह्मा कमण्डलुं (ददौ)।

yamaḥ kāladaṇḍāt daṇḍaṃ (tasyai dadau), ambupatiḥ ca pāśaṃ dadau | prajāpatiḥ ca akṣamālāṃ dadau, brahmā kamaṇḍaluṃ (dadau) |

Four gifts in two lines; ददौ is stated twice and understood with the other two givers.

6Translation

LiteralYama (gave) a staff from (his) staff of death, and the lord of the waters gave a noose; and Prajāpati gave a rosary; Brahmā a water-pot.

ReadableYama gave her a staff drawn from his own staff of death, and Varuṇa, lord of the waters, a noose; Prajāpati gave her a rosary, and Brahmā a water-pot.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningYama gives a staff, Varuṇa a noose (his second gift, after the conch in 2.21), Prajāpati a rosary and Brahmā a water-pot. The last two are not weapons but the emblems of an ascetic and of the creator.

Traditional interpretationThe noose (पाश) is Varuṇa’s ancient emblem, the bond with which he binds wrongdoers. In the battle the Goddess will use it to bind asuras and drag them along (2.57, पाशेन बद्ध्वा).

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: प्रजापतिश्चाक्षमालां ददौ ब्रह्मा कमण्डलुम्: with ददौ stated twice, the verse is taken as naming two givers, Prajāpati (as in 2.17, distinct from Brahmā) and Brahmā. It can also be read as one giver, ‘Brahmā the Prajāpati gave a rosary and a water-pot’, since Brahmā is called प्रजापति in 2.4. The reading itself is not in doubt.

2.24Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

समस्तरोमकूपेषु निजरश्मीन् दिवाकरः।
कालश्च दत्तवान् खड्गं तस्याश्चर्म च निर्मलम् ॥ २४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

समस्तरोमकूपेषु | निजरश्मीन् | दिवाकरःकालः | च | दत्तवान् | खड्गम् | तस्याः | चर्म | च | निर्मलम्

3IAST

As recitedsamastaromakūpeṣu nijaraśmīn divākaraḥ |
kālaśca dattavān khaḍgaṃ tasyāścarma ca nirmalam ||

Separatedsamastaromakūpeṣu | nijaraśmīn | divākaraḥ
kālaḥ | ca | dattavān | khaḍgam | tasyāḥ | carma | ca | nirmalam

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
समस्तरोमकूपेषुsamastaromakūpeṣuinto all the pores of her skinm. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); locative of the place where the rays were set
निजरश्मीन्nijaraśmīnhis own raysm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object (ददौ understood)
दिवाकरःdivākaraḥthe Day-maker, the Sunm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
कालःkālaḥKāla (Time)m. nom. sg. of काल; subject of दत्तवान्

खड्गम्khaḍgama swordm. acc. sg. of खड्ग; object
तस्याःtasyāḥto herpronoun तद्, f. gen. sg.; genitive used for the dative of the recipient

चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कालश्च kālaśca → कालः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c becomes ś; likewise तस्याश्चर्म.
  • खड्गं तस्याश्चर्म khaḍgaṃ tasyāścarma → खड्गम् + तस्याः + चर्म
    anusvāra; visarga sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Final m before t becomes anusvāra; the visarga of तस्याः before c becomes ś.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • समस्तरोमकूपेषु samastaromakūpeṣu — karmadhāraya containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (रोमकूप: रोम्णां कूपाः)
    Members: समस्त + रोमकूप · Vigraha: समस्ताः रोमकूपाः, तेषु samastāḥ romakūpāḥ, teṣu
    Literally “in all the hair-wells”; in the verse: in every pore of her body
  • निजरश्मीन् nijaraśmīn — karmadhāraya
    Members: निज + रश्मि · Vigraha: निजाः रश्मयः, तान् nijāḥ raśmayaḥ, tān
    Literally “own rays”; in the verse: the Sun’s own light
  • दिवाकरः divākaraḥ — upapada tatpuruṣa (कर from √कृ + ट)
    Members: दिवा + कर · Vigraha: दिवा करोति इति divā karoti iti
    Literally “he who makes the day”; in the verse: the Sun
  • निर्मलम् nirmalam — bahuvrīhi
    Members: निर् + मल · Vigraha: निर्गतं मलं यस्मात् तत् nirgataṃ malaṃ yasmāt tat
    Literally “that from which stain has gone”; in the verse: flawless, gleaming

C. Morphology

  • तस्याः tasyāḥ — Genitive of the recipient: with verbs of giving, epic and Purāṇic Sanskrit often uses the genitive where the classical language prefers the dative (contrast तस्यै in 2.20–22).
  • चर्म carma — Accusative (= nominative) singular of the neuter n-stem चर्मन्: the final n is lost (चर्म).

D. Anvaya — prose order

दिवाकरः समस्तरोमकूपेषु निजरश्मीन् (ददौ)। कालः च खड्गं निर्मलं चर्म च तस्याः दत्तवान्।

divākaraḥ samastaromakūpeṣu nijaraśmīn (dadau) | kālaḥ ca khaḍgaṃ nirmalaṃ carma ca tasyāḥ dattavān |

The first line has no verb of its own (ददौ from 2.23 is understood); the second is a full sentence with two objects.

6Translation

LiteralInto all the pores (of her skin) the Day-maker (set) his own rays; and Kāla gave her a sword and a spotless shield.

ReadableThe Sun filled every pore of her body with his own rays, and Time gave her a sword and a gleaming shield.

‘Filled’ is supplied for the missing verb of the first line.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Sun’s gift is not a weapon but his light, set into every pore of her skin: the Goddess shines from within. Kāla, Time, gives the sword and shield.

Traditional interpretationKāla here is Time personified, a power that ends all things, and so a fitting giver of the sword. The sword and shield are among the weapons named in the dhyāna of the middle carita (असिं च चर्म).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) calls the Goddess सर्वायुधधरा, ‘bearer of all weapons’ (name 530). In the Devī Māhātmya the same idea is told as a story: every weapon she holds was given to her by a god.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The first line has no finite verb; ददौ (or a verb of placing) is understood. Kāla is taken as Time personified, distinct from Yama (2.23), who is named separately.

2.25Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

क्षीरोदश्चामलं हारमजरे च तथाम्बरे।
चूडामणिं तथा दिव्यं कुण्डले कटकानि च ॥ २५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

क्षीरोदः | च | अमलम् | हारम् | अजरे | च | तथा | अम्बरेचूडामणिम् | तथा | दिव्यम् | कुण्डले | कटकानि | च

3IAST

As recitedkṣīrodaścāmalaṃ hāramajare ca tathāmbare |
cūḍāmaṇiṃ tathā divyaṃ kuṇḍale kaṭakāni ca ||

Separatedkṣīrodaḥ | ca | amalam | hāram | ajare | ca | tathā | ambare
cūḍāmaṇim | tathā | divyam | kuṇḍale | kaṭakāni | ca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
क्षीरोदःkṣīrodaḥthe Ocean of Milkm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of a ददौ supplied from the surrounding verses
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); adds the Ocean to the givers of 2.20–24
अमलम्amalamspotless, purem. acc. sg. of अमल (‘without stain’); qualifies हारम्
हारम्hārama necklacem. acc. sg. of हार (a-stem); object of the supplied ददौ
अजरेajareunaging, that never wear outn. acc. du. of अजर (‘free from decay’); qualifies अम्बरे
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
तथाtathāalso, likewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner, used as a connective)
अम्बरेambaretwo garmentsn. acc. du. of अम्बर (lit. ‘garment’; also ‘sky’); object
चूडामणिम्cūḍāmaṇima crest-jewelm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object
तथाtathāalsoindeclinable (connective)
दिव्यम्divyamdivine, heavenlym. acc. sg. of दिव्य (taddhita from दिव् ‘heaven’); qualifies चूडामणिम्
कुण्डलेkuṇḍalea pair of earringsn. acc. du. of कुण्डल; object
कटकानिkaṭakānibraceletsn. acc. pl. of कटक (m./n., ‘ring, bracelet’); object
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • क्षीरोदश्चामलं kṣīrodaścāmalaṃ → क्षीरोदः + च + अमलम्
    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś (क्षीरोदश्च); then a + a → ā (चामलं). The final m of अमलम् is anusvāra before the h of हारम् (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • हारमजरे hāramajare → हारम् + अजरे
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m stays m before a vowel and is written together with it.
  • तथाम्बरे tathāmbare → तथा + अम्बरे
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • क्षीरोद kṣīroda → क्षीर + उद
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • क्षीरोदः kṣīrodaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: क्षीर + उद (= उदक) · Vigraha: क्षीरम् उदकं यस्य सः kṣīram udakaṃ yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose water is milk”; in the verse: the Ocean of Milk, personified as a giver
    Alternative: Also analysed as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, क्षीरस्य उदः ‘the water of milk’, i.e. the milk-sea; the bahuvrīhi suits a personified giver. उद stands for उदक as the final member of such compounds.
  • चूडामणिम् cūḍāmaṇim — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: चूडा + मणि · Vigraha: चूडायाः मणिः (तम्) cūḍāyāḥ maṇiḥ (tam)
    Literally “the jewel of the crest”; in the verse: a jewel worn on the crown of the head
    Alternative: Also saptamī tatpuruṣa, चूडायां मणिः ‘the jewel (worn) on the crest’; the sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • अम्बरे ambare — Neuter dual of an a-stem (nom./acc. -e): ‘two garments’, the upper and the lower cloth. With अजरे it is a pair of everlasting robes.
  • कुण्डले kuṇḍale — Neuter dual again: earrings come as a pair, so the dual is the natural number. A dual in -e is pragṛhya, but here both duals stand before consonants, so no hiatus arises.

D. Anvaya — prose order

क्षीरोदः च अमलं हारम्, अजरे अम्बरे च, तथा दिव्यं चूडामणिम्, कुण्डले कटकानि च (ददौ)।

kṣīrodaḥ ca amalaṃ hāram, ajare ambare ca, tathā divyaṃ cūḍāmaṇim, kuṇḍale kaṭakāni ca (dadau) |

The verse has a subject and a string of objects but no verb of its own; ददौ, the verb of the whole catalogue (2.20–31), is supplied. The list of ornaments runs on into 2.26 and 2.27a.

6Translation

LiteralAnd the Milk-Ocean (gave) a spotless necklace and likewise two unaging garments, likewise a divine crest-jewel, two earrings and bracelets,

ReadableThe Ocean of Milk gave her a spotless necklace, a pair of garments that never wear out, a heavenly crest-jewel, earrings and bracelets,

‘Gave her’ is supplied from the catalogue’s verb ददौ; the sentence continues into 2.26–27.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningAfter the weapons come the ornaments. The Ocean of Milk, the store of treasures in Purāṇic myth, dresses the newly formed Goddess: necklace, a pair of imperishable garments, crest-jewel, earrings and bracelets. The verse names a giver but no verb; the list continues for a verse and a half.

Traditional interpretationWhere the list of the Ocean’s gifts ends is not marked. The usual reading lets it run to the finger-rings of 2.27a, so that Viśvakarman’s gift begins with the axe; others give the jewellery after the garments to the craftsman Viśvakarman, whose verb ददौ follows in 2.27. The Sanskrit allows both.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The verse has no verb, and the boundary between the Ocean’s gifts and Viśvakarman’s (2.27) is not marked in the text; the translation follows the common reading (the Ocean gives everything to the rings of 2.27a).

2.26Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अर्धचन्द्रं तथा शुभ्रं केयूरान् सर्वबाहुषु।
नूपुरौ विमलौ तद्वद् ग्रैवेयकमनुत्तमम् ॥ २६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अर्धचन्द्रम् | तथा | शुभ्रम् | केयूरान् | सर्वबाहुषुनूपुरौ | विमलौ | तद्वत् | ग्रैवेयकम् | अनुत्तमम्

3IAST

As recitedardhacandraṃ tathā śubhraṃ keyūrān sarvabāhuṣu |
nūpurau vimalau tadvad graiveyakamanuttamam ||

Separatedardhacandram | tathā | śubhram | keyūrān | sarvabāhuṣu
nūpurau | vimalau | tadvat | graiveyakam | anuttamam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अर्धचन्द्रम्ardhacandrama crescent (ornament)m. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of the supplied ददौ
तथाtathāalsoindeclinable (connective)
शुभ्रम्śubhrambright, gleaming whitem. acc. sg. of शुभ्र; qualifies अर्धचन्द्रम्
केयूरान्keyūrānarmletsm. acc. pl. of केयूर; object
सर्वबाहुषुsarvabāhuṣuon all (her) armsm. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
नूपुरौnūpuraua pair of ankletsm. acc. du. of नूपुर; object
विमलौvimalauspotless, shiningm. acc. du. of विमल; qualifies नूपुरौ
तद्वत्tadvatlikewise, in the same wayindeclinable (तद् + taddhita -vat ‘like that’)
ग्रैवेयकम्graiveyakama neck-ornament, a collarn. acc. sg. of ग्रैवेयक (taddhita from ग्रीवा ‘neck’: ‘what belongs to the neck’); object
अनुत्तमम्anuttamamunsurpassed, the finestn. acc. sg. of अनुत्तम; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ग्रैवेयकम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • अर्धचन्द्रं तथा ardhacandraṃ tathā → अर्धचन्द्रम् + तथा
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; likewise शुभ्रं before k.
  • तद्वद् ग्रैवेयकम् tadvad graiveyakam → तद्वत् + ग्रैवेयकम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced g. The Gita Press writes the d with a virāma and a space.
  • ग्रैवेयकमनुत्तमम् graiveyakamanuttamam → ग्रैवेयकम् + अनुत्तमम्
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m is simply written together with the following vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अर्धचन्द्रम् ardhacandram — tatpuruṣa (ekadeśin type: the part named first, ‘half of the moon’)
    Members: अर्ध + चन्द्र · Vigraha: अर्धं चन्द्रस्य (तम्) ardhaṃ candrasya (tam)
    Literally “the half-moon”; in the verse: a crescent-shaped ornament, worn on the head or brow
    Alternative: Also taken as a karmadhāraya, अर्धः चासौ चन्द्रः च ‘a half moon’; the meaning is the same.
  • सर्वबाहुषु sarvabāhuṣu — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + बाहु · Vigraha: सर्वे च ते बाहवः च, तेषु sarve ca te bāhavaḥ ca, teṣu
    Literally “on all the arms”; in the verse: on every one of her many arms
  • अनुत्तमम् anuttamam — bahuvrīhi (with the negative particle)
    Members: न + उत्तम · Vigraha: न विद्यते उत्तमः यस्मात् तत् na vidyate uttamaḥ yasmāt tat
    Literally “that than which there is no higher”; in the verse: peerless, of the highest kind

C. Morphology

  • तद्वत् tadvat — The taddhita -vat of comparison added to तद्: ‘like that, likewise’. It is an indeclinable and must not be confused with the possessive -vat of हिमवत् (2.29).
  • नूपुरौ nūpurau — Masculine dual (-au): anklets, like earrings, are named as a pair.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तथा शुभ्रम् अर्धचन्द्रम्, सर्वबाहुषु केयूरान्, तद्वत् विमलौ नूपुरौ, अनुत्तमं ग्रैवेयकं (च ददौ)।

tathā śubhram ardhacandram, sarvabāhuṣu keyūrān, tadvat vimalau nūpurau, anuttamaṃ graiveyakaṃ (ca dadau) |

A continuation of the list begun in 2.25, all accusatives of the supplied verb; सर्वबाहुषु says where the armlets are worn.

6Translation

Literallikewise a bright half-moon, armlets on all the arms, likewise two spotless anklets, an unsurpassed neck-ornament,

Readablea gleaming crescent, armlets for all her arms, a pair of shining anklets and a peerless collar,

The sentence continues from 2.25 and into 2.27.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe list of ornaments continues from head to foot: a crescent for the head, armlets for ‘all her arms’, anklets and a collar. The plural arms anticipate 2.39, where she fills the directions with a thousand arms.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāThe same ornament vocabulary describes the Goddess’s form in the Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa): 31, कनकाङ्गदकेयूरकमनीयभुजान्विता (arms lovely with golden aṅgadas and keyūras), and 32, रत्नग्रैवेयचिन्ताकलोलमुक्ताफलान्विता (with a jewelled graiveya and pearls). This is a shared iconographic vocabulary, not a borrowing between the two texts.

2.27Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अङ्गुलीयकरत्नानि समस्तास्वङ्गुलीषु च।
विश्वकर्मा ददौ तस्यै परशुं चातिनिर्मलम् ॥ २७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अङ्गुलीयकरत्नानि | समस्तासु | अङ्गुलीषु | चविश्वकर्मा | ददौ | तस्यै | परशुम् | च | अतिनिर्मलम्

3IAST

As recitedaṅgulīyakaratnāni samastāsvaṅgulīṣu ca |
viśvakarmā dadau tasyai paraśuṃ cātinirmalam ||

Separatedaṅgulīyakaratnāni | samastāsu | aṅgulīṣu | ca
viśvakarmā | dadau | tasyai | paraśum | ca | atinirmalam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अङ्गुलीयकरत्नानिaṅgulīyakaratnānithe finest rings, jewelled ringsn. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of the supplied ददौ
समस्तासुsamastāsuon allf. loc. pl. of समस्त (सम् + √अस् + kta, ‘put together, entire’); qualifies अङ्गुलीषु
अङ्गुलीषुaṅgulīṣuon the fingersf. loc. pl. of अङ्गुली (ī-stem)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); closes the list begun in 2.25
विश्वकर्माviśvakarmāViśvakarman, the craftsman of the godsm. nom. sg. of विश्वकर्मन् (n-stem); compound (see Samāsa); subject of ददौ
ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to give’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.; indirect object
परशुम्paraśuman axem. acc. sg. of परशु (u-stem); object of ददौ
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); links परशुम् to the further gifts of 2.28a
अतिनिर्मलम्atinirmalamexceedingly bright, flawlessm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies परशुम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • समस्तास्वङ्गुलीषु samastāsvaṅgulīṣu → समस्तासु + अङ्गुलीषु
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final u before a dissimilar vowel becomes v: -āsu a- → -āsva-.
  • चातिनिर्मलम् cātinirmalam → च + अतिनिर्मलम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā. The m of परशुम् before c is anusvāra (परशुं, 8.3.23).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अङ्गुलीयकरत्नानि aṅgulīyakaratnāni — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: अङ्गुलीयक + रत्न · Vigraha: अङ्गुलीयकानां रत्नानि aṅgulīyakānāṃ ratnāni
    Literally “the jewels among finger-rings”; in the verse: the finest rings (रत्न at the end of a compound means ‘the best of its kind’)
    Alternative: Also read as ‘gem-rings’, रत्नमयानि अङ्गुलीयकानि, a madhyamapadalopin karmadhāraya with the members reversed; but the head of the compound is रत्न, which favours ‘the jewels among rings’.
  • विश्वकर्मा viśvakarmā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: विश्व + कर्मन् · Vigraha: विश्वं कर्म यस्य सः viśvaṃ karma yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose work is everything”; in the verse: the maker of all things, the divine artisan
  • अतिनिर्मलम् atinirmalam — tatpuruṣa (prādi); निर्मल is itself a bahuvrīhi, निर्गतः मलः यस्मात् तत्
    Members: अति + निर्मल · Vigraha: अतिशयेन निर्मलम् atiśayena nirmalam
    Literally “beyond stainless”; in the verse: gleaming, without a flaw

C. Morphology

  • ददौ dadau — Perfect of √दा: reduplicated दद- with the ending -au that roots in -ā take in the 1st and 3rd singular perfect (ददौ ‘he gave’). The catalogue’s refrain verb.
  • विश्वकर्मा viśvakarmā — Nominative singular of an n-stem: the final n is dropped and the vowel lengthened (कर्मन् → कर्मा), as in राजा.

D. Anvaya — prose order

समस्तासु अङ्गुलीषु अङ्गुलीयकरत्नानि च (क्षीरोदः ददौ)। विश्वकर्मा तस्यै अतिनिर्मलं परशुं च (अनेकरूपाणि अस्त्राणि अभेद्यं दंशनं च) ददौ।

samastāsu aṅgulīṣu aṅgulīyakaratnāni ca (kṣīrodaḥ dadau) | viśvakarmā tasyai atinirmalaṃ paraśuṃ ca (anekarūpāṇi astrāṇi abhedyaṃ daṃśanaṃ ca) dadau |

The first line ends the Ocean’s list (2.25–26); the second begins a new giver, Viśvakarman, whose objects continue into the first line of 2.28.

6Translation

Literaland jewel-rings on all the fingers. Viśvakarman gave to her an exceedingly spotless axe and

Readableand the finest rings for every finger. Viśvakarman gave her a gleaming axe,

The first line is taken as the last of the Ocean’s gifts (see 2.25); the second line runs on into 2.28.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningRings for every finger complete the jewellery. Viśvakarman, the craftsman of the gods, gives the axe and, in the next line, missiles and armour.

Traditional interpretationThe axe (परशु) appears in the madhyama-carita dhyāna (Gita Press pāṭha), where Mahālakṣmī holds अक्षस्रक्परशुं … — rosary and axe are the first two objects named. The dhyāna gathers the gifts of this chapter into eighteen hands.

2.28Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अस्त्राण्यनेकरूपाणि तथाभेद्यं च दंशनम्।
अम्लानपङ्कजां मालां शिरस्युरसि चापराम् ॥ २८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अस्त्राणि | अनेकरूपाणि | तथा | अभेद्यम् | च | दंशनम्अम्लानपङ्कजाम् | मालाम् | शिरसि | उरसि | च | अपराम्

3IAST

As recitedastrāṇyanekarūpāṇi tathābhedyaṃ ca daṃśanam |
amlānapaṅkajāṃ mālāṃ śirasyurasi cāparām ||

Separatedastrāṇi | anekarūpāṇi | tathā | abhedyam | ca | daṃśanam
amlānapaṅkajām | mālām | śirasi | urasi | ca | aparām

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अस्त्राणिastrāṇimissilesn. acc. pl. of अस्त्र (√अस् ‘to throw’ + ṣṭran: a weapon hurled or discharged); object of ददौ (2.27)
अनेकरूपाणिanekarūpāṇiof many formsn. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अस्त्राणि
तथाtathāand alsoindeclinable (connective)
अभेद्यम्abhedyamimpenetrablen. acc. sg. of अभेद्य (negative न + √भिद् ‘to split’ + gerundive ṇyat: ‘not to be pierced’); qualifies दंशनम्
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
दंशनम्daṃśanamarmour, a coat of mailn. acc. sg. of दंशन (√दंश् + lyuṭ); object of ददौ (2.27)
अम्लानपङ्कजाम्amlānapaṅkajāmof unfading lotusesf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मालाम्
मालाम्mālāma garlandf. acc. sg. of माला; object of अददत् (2.29)
शिरसिśirasifor the head, on the headn. loc. sg. of शिरस् (s-stem)
उरसिurasifor the breast, on the breastn. loc. sg. of उरस् (s-stem)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
अपराम्aparāmanother (garland)f. acc. sg. of the pronominal adjective अपर; माला is understood

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • अस्त्राण्यनेकरूपाणि astrāṇyanekarūpāṇi → अस्त्राणि + अनेकरूपाणि
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final i before a dissimilar vowel becomes y.
  • तथाभेद्यं tathābhedyaṃ → तथा + अभेद्यम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • शिरस्युरसि śirasyurasi → शिरसि + उरसि
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i + u → yu.
  • चापराम् cāparām → च + अपराम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अनेकरूपाणि anekarūpāṇi — bahuvrīhi (अनेक itself a nañ tatpuruṣa, न एकम्)
    Members: अनेक + रूप · Vigraha: अनेकानि रूपाणि येषां तानि anekāni rūpāṇi yeṣāṃ tāni
    Literally “having not-one forms”; in the verse: of many kinds
  • अम्लानपङ्कजाम् amlānapaṅkajām — bahuvrīhi; अम्लान is a nañ tatpuruṣa (न म्लानम्, √म्लै + kta) and पङ्कज an upapada tatpuruṣa (पङ्के जायते इति)
    Members: अम्लान + पङ्कज · Vigraha: अम्लानानि पङ्कजानि यस्याः सा, ताम् amlānāni paṅkajāni yasyāḥ sā, tām
    Literally “whose mud-born (flowers) are unwithered”; in the verse: a garland of lotuses that never fade

C. Morphology

  • अभेद्यम् abhedyam — Gerundive (ṇyat) of √भिद् with the negative prefix: ‘not to be split’ — the armour cannot be pierced.
  • शिरसि śirasi — Locative of the s-stem शिरस्, like उरसि from उरस्: the locative tells where each garland is to be worn.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(विश्वकर्मा) अनेकरूपाणि अस्त्राणि तथा अभेद्यं दंशनं च (ददौ)। (जलधिः) शिरसि अम्लानपङ्कजां मालाम्, उरसि च अपरां (मालाम्, अददत्)।

(viśvakarmā) anekarūpāṇi astrāṇi tathā abhedyaṃ daṃśanaṃ ca (dadau) | (jaladhiḥ) śirasi amlānapaṅkajāṃ mālām, urasi ca aparāṃ (mālām, adadat) |

The first line completes Viśvakarman’s gift (verb ददौ in 2.27); the second line lists objects of अददत्, whose subject जलधिः stands in 2.29.

6Translation

Literalmissiles of many forms, and likewise an impenetrable armour; a garland of unfading lotuses for the head, and another for the breast —

Readablemissiles of many kinds and armour that nothing can pierce. A garland of unfading lotuses for her head and another for her breast

The second line is the object of ‘the Ocean gave’ in 2.29; the translation leaves the verb to that verse.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningViśvakarman’s gifts end with missiles and impenetrable armour. The garlands — one for the head, one for the breast — belong to the next giver, the Ocean, named in 2.29.

Traditional interpretationUnfading lotuses (अम्लान) are a stock sign of the divine: what the gods wear does not wither. The lotus also stands in the madhyama-carita dhyāna (पद्मं), and she is pictured there as seated on a lotus (सरोजस्थिताम्).

2.29Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अददज्जलधिस्तस्यै पङ्कजं चातिशोभनम्।
हिमवान् वाहनं सिंहं रत्नानि विविधानि च ॥ २९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अददत् | जलधिः | तस्यै | पङ्कजम् | च | अतिशोभनम्हिमवान् | वाहनम् | सिंहम् | रत्नानि | विविधानि | च

3IAST

As recitedadadajjaladhistasyai paṅkajaṃ cātiśobhanam |
himavān vāhanaṃ siṃhaṃ ratnāni vividhāni ca ||

Separatedadadat | jaladhiḥ | tasyai | paṅkajam | ca | atiśobhanam
himavān | vāhanam | siṃham | ratnāni | vividhāni | ca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अददत्adadatgave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to give’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; an epic thematic form for classical अददात्
जलधिःjaladhiḥthe Oceanm. nom. sg. of जलधि; compound (see Samāsa); subject of अददत्
तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.
पङ्कजम्paṅkajama lotusn. acc. sg. of पङ्कज (‘mud-born’); object
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); adds the lotus to the garlands of 2.28
अतिशोभनम्atiśobhanamexceedingly beautifuln. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies पङ्कजम्
हिमवान्himavānHimavat, the Himālayam. nom. sg. of हिमवत् (हिम + possessive matup, ‘having snow’); subject of a supplied ददौ / अददत्
वाहनम्vāhanamas a mount, a vehiclen. acc. sg. of वाहन (√वह् causative + lyuṭ, ‘that which carries’); predicative to सिंहम्
सिंहम्siṃhama lionm. acc. sg. of सिंह; object
रत्नानिratnānijewelsn. acc. pl. of रत्न; object
विविधानिvividhāniof various kindsn. acc. pl. of विविध; qualifies रत्नानि
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • अददज्जलधिस्तस्यै adadajjaladhistasyai → अददत् + जलधिः + तस्यै
    consonant sandhi (palatalisation) and visarga sandhi · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40) with झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Final t before j becomes j (t voiced to d, and the dental made palatal next to the palatal j): अददज्जलधिः. Then the visarga before t becomes s: जलधिस्तस्यै.
  • चातिशोभनम् cātiśobhanam → च + अतिशोभनम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • हिमवान् वाहनं himavān vāhanaṃ → हिमवान् + वाहनम्
    no change · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) for वाहनं; final n before v is unchanged
    Final n before v stays n; only the m of वाहनम् becomes anusvāra before s (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • जलधिः jaladhiḥ — upapada tatpuruṣa (√धा + ki)
    Members: जल + धि · Vigraha: जलानि धीयन्ते अस्मिन् इति jalāni dhīyante asmin iti
    Literally “where the waters are held”; in the verse: the ocean
  • अतिशोभनम् atiśobhanam — tatpuruṣa (prādi)
    Members: अति + शोभन · Vigraha: अतिशयेन शोभनम् atiśayena śobhanam
    Literally “beyond beautiful”; in the verse: exceedingly lovely

C. Morphology

  • अददत् adadat — Imperfect of √दा. Classical grammar has अददात् (reduplicated stem ददा-); the epics often inflect √दा on a thematic stem दद-, giving अददत्. The metre (eight syllables, अ-द-दज्-ज-ल-धिस्-तस्-यै) is unaffected either way, so this is a matter of usage, not of metre.
  • हिमवान् himavān — Nominative singular of the possessive -vat stem हिमवत्: the vowel is lengthened and n inserted (-वान्), as in भगवान्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

जलधिः तस्यै (शिरसि अम्लानपङ्कजां मालाम्, उरसि च अपराम्,) अतिशोभनं पङ्कजं च अददत्। हिमवान् वाहनं सिंहं विविधानि रत्नानि च (ददौ)।

jaladhiḥ tasyai (śirasi amlānapaṅkajāṃ mālām, urasi ca aparām,) atiśobhanaṃ paṅkajaṃ ca adadat | himavān vāhanaṃ siṃhaṃ vividhāni ratnāni ca (dadau) |

अददत् governs the garlands of 2.28b and the lotus; Himavat’s verb is supplied. वाहनम् is predicative: ‘a lion as her mount’.

6Translation

Literalthe Ocean gave to her, and an exceedingly beautiful lotus. Himavat (gave) a lion as mount, and various jewels.

Readablethe Ocean gave her, with a lotus of surpassing beauty. Himavat gave her a lion to ride, and jewels of every kind.

‘Gave’ is supplied for Himavat.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Ocean (जलधि, distinct from the Ocean of Milk of 2.25) gives garlands and a lotus. Himavat gives the lion that will carry her and fight beside her (2.51; it battles Cāmara in 3.14–16).

Traditional interpretationHimavat is, in Purāṇic tradition, the father of Pārvatī; here the mountain simply gives what belongs to the mountains — the lion and its jewels. The lion-mount gives her the name सिंहवाहिनी in 2.34.

2.30Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ददावशून्यं सुरया पानपात्रं धनाधिपः।
शेषश्च सर्वनागेशो महामणिविभूषितम् ॥ ३० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ददौ | अशून्यम् | सुरया | पानपात्रम् | धनाधिपःशेषः | च | सर्वनागेशः | महामणिविभूषितम्

3IAST

As reciteddadāvaśūnyaṃ surayā pānapātraṃ dhanādhipaḥ |
śeṣaśca sarvanāgeśo mahāmaṇivibhūṣitam ||

Separateddadau | aśūnyam | surayā | pānapātram | dhanādhipaḥ
śeṣaḥ | ca | sarvanāgeśaḥ | mahāmaṇivibhūṣitam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
अशून्यम्aśūnyamnever emptyn. acc. sg. of अशून्य (negative न + शून्य); qualifies पानपात्रम्
सुरयाsurayāof wine, with winef. instr. sg. of सुरा; construed with अशून्यम् (‘not empty of wine’)
पानपात्रम्pānapātrama drinking cupn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of ददौ
धनाधिपःdhanādhipaḥthe Lord of Wealth (Kubera)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of ददौ
शेषःśeṣaḥŚeṣa, the cosmic serpentm. nom. sg. of शेष; subject of ददौ in 2.31
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
सर्वनागेशःsarvanāgeśaḥlord of all serpentsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to शेषः
महामणिविभूषितम्mahāmaṇivibhūṣitamadorned with great jewelsm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies नागहारम् in 2.31

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ददावशून्यं dadāvaśūnyaṃ → ददौ + अशून्यम्
    vowel sandhi (ayādi) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    Final au before a vowel becomes āv: ददौ + अ- → ददाव-.
  • धनाधिपः dhanādhipaḥ → धन + अधिपः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • शेषश्च śeṣaśca → शेषः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c → s → ś.
  • सर्वनागेशो महामणि sarvanāgeśo mahāmaṇi → सर्वनागेशः + महामणि-
    visarga sandhi; and guṇa inside the compound · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced m becomes -o. Inside the compound नाग + ईश → नागेश (a + ī → e).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पानपात्रम् pānapātram — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: पान + पात्र · Vigraha: पानस्य पात्रम् pānasya pātram
    Literally “vessel of drinking”; in the verse: a drinking cup
    Alternative: Also caturthī tatpuruṣa, पानाय पात्रम् ‘a vessel for drinking’; the sense is the same.
  • धनाधिपः dhanādhipaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: धन + अधिप · Vigraha: धनानाम् अधिपः dhanānām adhipaḥ
    Literally “overlord of riches”; in the verse: Kubera, the god of wealth (compare कौबेरेण in 2.16)
  • सर्वनागेशः sarvanāgeśaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (सर्वनाग a karmadhāraya, सर्वे च ते नागाः च)
    Members: सर्वनाग + ईश · Vigraha: सर्वेषां नागानाम् ईशः sarveṣāṃ nāgānām īśaḥ
    Literally “lord of all the serpents”; in the verse: Śeṣa as king of the Nāgas
  • महामणिविभूषितम् mahāmaṇivibhūṣitam — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (महामणि a karmadhāraya, महान् चासौ मणिः च)
    Members: महामणि + विभूषित · Vigraha: महामणिभिः विभूषितम् mahāmaṇibhiḥ vibhūṣitam
    Literally “adorned with great gems”; in the verse: jewel-studded

C. Morphology

  • अशून्यम् aśūnyam — A litotes: ‘not empty of wine’ means ‘always full’. The thing it is not empty of is in the instrumental (सुरया).
  • महामणिविभूषितम् mahāmaṇivibhūṣitam — Accusative, though it stands beside the nominatives शेषः … सर्वनागेशः: it anticipates नागहारम्, the object in the next verse.

D. Anvaya — prose order

धनाधिपः सुरया अशून्यं पानपात्रं ददौ। सर्वनागेशः शेषः च महामणिविभूषितं (नागहारं तस्यै ददौ)।

dhanādhipaḥ surayā aśūnyaṃ pānapātraṃ dadau | sarvanāgeśaḥ śeṣaḥ ca mahāmaṇivibhūṣitaṃ (nāgahāraṃ tasyai dadau) |

The first line is complete. The second line begins a sentence finished in 2.31: Śeṣa is its subject, and the accusative adjective waits for its noun नागहारम्.

6Translation

LiteralThe Lord of Wealth gave a drinking-cup never empty of wine. And Śeṣa, the lord of all serpents, (gave) a … adorned with great jewels

ReadableKubera, Lord of Wealth, gave her a drinking-cup never empty of wine; and Śeṣa, king of all serpents, a jewel-studded

The second line runs into 2.31, where the noun (नागहारम्) and the verb stand.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningKubera, keeper of the gods’ treasure, gives a cup that is never empty of wine. The cup returns in battle: before slaying Mahiṣa she drinks and says ‘Roar, roar for a moment, fool, while I drink the honey-wine’ (3.38).

Traditional interpretationThe wine-vessel is among the eighteen objects of the madhyama-carita dhyāna (सुराभाजनम्, Gita Press pāṭha). In the Śākta tradition the cup is commonly read as the Goddess’s exhilaration in battle, not as an ordinary indulgence; the text itself says only what is given.

2.31Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

नागहारं ददौ तस्यै धत्ते यः पृथिवीमिमाम्।
अन्यैरपि सुरैर्देवी भूषणैरायुधैस्तथा ॥ ३१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

नागहारम् | ददौ | तस्यै | धत्ते | यः | पृथिवीम् | इमाम्अन्यैः | अपि | सुरैः | देवी | भूषणैः | आयुधैः | तथा

3IAST

As recitednāgahāraṃ dadau tasyai dhatte yaḥ pṛthivīmimām |
anyairapi surairdevī bhūṣaṇairāyudhaistathā ||

Separatednāgahāram | dadau | tasyai | dhatte | yaḥ | pṛthivīm | imām
anyaiḥ | api | suraiḥ | devī | bhūṣaṇaiḥ | āyudhaiḥ | tathā

ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U), liṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; subject शेषः (2.30)

पृथिवीम्pṛthivīmthe earthf. acc. sg. of पृथिवी; object of धत्ते

भूषणैःbhūṣaṇaiḥwith ornamentsn. instr. pl. of भूषण; instrument
