5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पृथिवीमिमाम् pṛthivīmimām → पृथिवीम् + इमाम्
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m is written together with the following vowel.
  • अन्यैरपि anyairapi → अन्यैः + अपि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after a vowel other than a/ā, before a vowel or voiced consonant, appears as r: अन्यैरपि, सुरैर्देवी, भूषणैरायुधैः.
  • आयुधैस्तथा āyudhaistathā → आयुधैः + तथा
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • नागहारम् nāgahāram — karmadhāraya (of identification: the serpent is the necklace)
    Members: नाग + हार · Vigraha: नागः एव हारः (तम्) nāgaḥ eva hāraḥ (tam)
    Literally “the serpent-necklace”; in the verse: a necklace that is a serpent, given by the serpent-king
    Alternative: Also ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, नागानां हारः ‘the necklace of the Nāgas’ — a jewelled necklace from their treasury. महामणिविभूषितम् (2.30) fits either: Nāgas are known for the jewels on their hoods.

C. Morphology

  • धत्ते dhatte — Present ātmanepada of √धा: reduplicated दध्- + ते, with the aspiration moved forward to the first syllable (दध् + ते → धत्ते). The present tense describes Śeṣa’s permanent office: he who bears this earth.
  • देवी devī — The nominative here is the subject of the next verse’s सम्मानिता … ननाद; the instrumentals सुरैः, भूषणैः, आयुधैः are the agent and the means of that participle.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यः इमां पृथिवीं धत्ते, (सः शेषः) तस्यै नागहारं ददौ। अन्यैः अपि सुरैः भूषणैः तथा आयुधैः देवी (सम्मानिता)।

yaḥ imāṃ pṛthivīṃ dhatte, (saḥ śeṣaḥ) tasyai nāgahāraṃ dadau | anyaiḥ api suraiḥ bhūṣaṇaiḥ tathā āyudhaiḥ devī (sammānitā) |

The first line closes the sentence begun in 2.30 with a relative clause on Śeṣa. The second line opens a new sentence whose participle and verb (सम्मानिता … ननाद) stand in 2.32.

6Translation

Literalserpent-necklace he gave to her — he who bears this earth. By other gods too, with ornaments and with weapons likewise, the Goddess —

Readableserpent-necklace — Śeṣa, who bears this earth. And the Goddess, honoured by the other gods as well with ornaments and weapons,

The second line is completed by सम्मानिता in 2.32; ‘honoured’ is brought forward for English order.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningŚeṣa, the serpent who holds up the earth, gives a jewelled serpent-necklace. Then the list is closed with a summary: the other gods too honoured her with ornaments and weapons.

Traditional interpretationŚākta readers see in the catalogue of gifts (2.20–31) the gods handing back to the Goddess powers that are hers: several weapons are drawn out of the gods’ own weapons (2.20–23), as her body was drawn out of their light (2.13–18).

2.32Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सम्मानिता ननादोच्चैः साट्टहासं मुहुर्मुहुः।
तस्या नादेन घोरेण कृत्स्नमापूरितं नभः ॥ ३२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सम्मानिता | ननाद | उच्चैः | साट्टहासम् | मुहुः | मुहुःतस्याः | नादेन | घोरेण | कृत्स्नम् | आपूरितम् | नभः

3IAST

As recitedsammānitā nanādoccaiḥ sāṭṭahāsaṃ muhurmuhuḥ |
tasyā nādena ghoreṇa kṛtsnamāpūritaṃ nabhaḥ ||

Separatedsammānitā | nanāda | uccaiḥ | sāṭṭahāsam | muhuḥ | muhuḥ
tasyāḥ | nādena | ghoreṇa | kṛtsnam | āpūritam | nabhaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सम्मानिताsammānitāhonouredf. nom. sg. of सम्मानित (सम् + √मन्, causative मानय- ‘to honour’, + kta); agrees with देवी (2.31)
ननादnanādaroared√नद् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to sound, roar’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
उच्चैःuccaiḥloudlyindeclinable (adverb, ‘aloud, high’)
साट्टहासम्sāṭṭahāsamwith loud laughterindeclinable (adverbial n. acc. sg.); compound (see Samāsa)
मुहुःmuhuḥagainindeclinable (adverb)
मुहुःmuhuḥand againindeclinable; repeated (āmreḍita) for frequency: ‘again and again’
तस्याःtasyāḥherpronoun तद्, f. gen. sg.
नादेनnādenaby the roarm. instr. sg. of नाद; agent of आपूरितम्
घोरेणghoreṇaterriblem. instr. sg. of घोर; qualifies नादेन
कृत्स्नम्kṛtsnamthe wholen. nom. sg. of कृत्स्न; qualifies नभः
आपूरितम्āpūritamwas filledn. nom. sg. of आपूरित (आ + √पूर्, causative/curādi पूरय- ‘to fill’, + kta); predicate (भूतम् supplied)
नभःnabhaḥthe skyn. nom. sg. of नभस् (s-stem); subject

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ननादोच्चैः nanādoccaiḥ → ननाद + उच्चैः
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • मुहुर्मुहुः muhurmuhuḥ → मुहुः + मुहुः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after u before the voiced m appears as r.
  • तस्या नादेन tasyā nādena → तस्याः + नादेन
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced sound: the visarga becomes y, which is then dropped before a consonant, leaving -ā.
  • कृत्स्नमापूरितं kṛtsnamāpūritaṃ → कृत्स्नम् + आपूरितम्
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m joins the following vowel; the m of आपूरितम् becomes anusvāra before n.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • साट्टहासम् sāṭṭahāsam — bahuvrīhi (with सह, which becomes स-), used adverbially in the neuter accusative; अट्टहास is a karmadhāraya, अट्टः (‘excessive’) हासः
    Members: सह + अट्टहास · Vigraha: अट्टहासेन सह वर्तते यथा तथा aṭṭahāsena saha vartate yathā tathā
    Literally “accompanied by loud laughter”; in the verse: with peals of laughter
    Alternative: Also explained as an avyayībhāva with सह (‘together with laughter’); either way the form is adverbial and the sense the same.

C. Morphology

  • ननाद nanāda — Perfect of √नद् with reduplication न-नाद (the root vowel lengthened in the 3rd singular). The same root gives नाद ‘roar’ in the next line.
  • आपूरितम् āpūritam — Past participle of the causative stem पूरय- (‘to fill’) with आ ‘completely’. It is a passive participle used as a finite verb: ‘(the sky) was filled’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(अन्यैः अपि सुरैः भूषणैः आयुधैः तथा) सम्मानिता (देवी) साट्टहासं मुहुः मुहुः उच्चैः ननाद। तस्याः घोरेण नादेन कृत्स्नं नभः आपूरितम्।

(anyaiḥ api suraiḥ bhūṣaṇaiḥ āyudhaiḥ tathā) sammānitā (devī) sāṭṭahāsaṃ muhuḥ muhuḥ uccaiḥ nanāda | tasyāḥ ghoreṇa nādena kṛtsnaṃ nabhaḥ āpūritam |

Two sentences: the first finishes 2.31b (देवी … सम्मानिता ननाद); the second is passive, with the roar as agent.

6Translation

Literalhonoured, roared loudly with loud laughter again and again. By her terrible roar the whole sky was filled.

Readableroared aloud again and again, with peals of laughter. The whole sky was filled with her terrible roar.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningArmed and adorned, the Goddess’s first act is a roar mixed with laughter. The roar fills the sky; the next two verses follow its effect through the worlds.

Traditional interpretationHer laughter in the face of battle is a recurring mark of this carita: she fights without effort and is unwearied (अनायस्तानना, 2.50). Laughter here shows sovereignty, not mockery.

2.33Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अमायतातिमहता प्रतिशब्दो महानभूत् ।
चुक्षुभुः सकला लोकाः समुद्राश्च चकम्पिरे ॥ ३३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अमायता | अतिमहता | प्रतिशब्दः | महान् | अभूत्चुक्षुभुः | सकलाः | लोकाः | समुद्राः | च | चकम्पिरे

3IAST

As recitedamāyatātimahatā pratiśabdo mahānabhūt |
cukṣubhuḥ sakalā lokāḥ samudrāśca cakampire ||

Separatedamāyatā | atimahatā | pratiśabdaḥ | mahān | abhūt
cukṣubhuḥ | sakalāḥ | lokāḥ | samudrāḥ | ca | cakampire

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अमायताamāyatāuncontainable, that no space could holdm. instr. sg. of अमायत्, the negative (न) of मायत्, present participle of √मा ‘to be measured, to be contained’ (on the stem माय-); qualifies the roar (नादेन, 2.32) understood
अतिमहताatimahatāby the exceedingly great (roar)m. instr. sg. of अतिमहत् (prādi compound, ‘very great’); instrument of cause
प्रतिशब्दःpratiśabdaḥan echom. nom. sg. of प्रतिशब्द (प्रति + शब्द, ‘counter-sound’); subject of अभूत्
महान्mahāngreat, mightym. nom. sg. of महत्; qualifies प्रतिशब्दः
अभूत्abhūtarose, there was√भू, luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
चुक्षुभुःcukṣubhuḥwere shaken√क्षुभ् (divādi, 4P ‘to be agitated’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
सकलाःsakalāḥallm. nom. pl. of सकल; qualifies लोकाः
लोकाःlokāḥthe worldsm. nom. pl. of लोक; subject of चुक्षुभुः
समुद्राःsamudrāḥthe oceansm. nom. pl. of समुद्र; subject of चकम्पिरे
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
चकम्पिरेcakampiretrembled√कम्प् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to tremble’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, ātmanepada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • अमायतातिमहता amāyatātimahatā → अमायता + अतिमहता
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. The same spelling also results from अमायत + अतिमहता (see Flags).
  • प्रतिशब्दो महानभूत् pratiśabdo mahānabhūt → प्रतिशब्दः + महान् + अभूत्
    visarga sandhi; final n before a vowel · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced m → -o. The n of महान् is not doubled before अभूत् because the vowel before it is long (ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम्, 8.3.32, needs a short vowel).
  • सकला लोकाः sakalā lokāḥ → सकलाः + लोकाः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant loses its visarga.
  • समुद्राश्च samudrāśca → समुद्राः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c → ś.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अतिमहता atimahatā — tatpuruṣa (prādi)
    Members: अति + महत् · Vigraha: अतिशयेन महत्, तेन atiśayena mahat, tena
    Literally “beyond great”; in the verse: immense

C. Morphology

  • अमायता amāyatā — Instrumental of a negated present participle: ‘(by the roar) not being contained’, i.e. a sound that no space could hold. It and अतिमहता qualify the roar of 2.32, which is not repeated.
  • चुक्षुभुः cukṣubhuḥ — Perfect 3rd plural of √क्षुभ्: reduplication चु- (k → c in the reduplicating syllable) + क्षुभ् + -उः.
  • चकम्पिरे cakampire — Perfect 3rd plural ātmanepada (-इरे) of √कम्प्; contrast parasmaipada चुक्षुभुः beside it.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अमायता अतिमहता (नादेन) महान् प्रतिशब्दः अभूत्। सकलाः लोकाः चुक्षुभुः, समुद्राः च चकम्पिरे।

amāyatā atimahatā (nādena) mahān pratiśabdaḥ abhūt | sakalāḥ lokāḥ cukṣubhuḥ, samudrāḥ ca cakampire |

Three clauses: the echo caused by the roar (instrumental of cause), the worlds shaken, the seas trembling.

6Translation

LiteralBy the uncontainable, exceedingly great (roar) a mighty echo arose. All the worlds were shaken, and the oceans trembled.

ReadableFrom that boundless, immense roar a mighty echo rang out; all the worlds were shaken and the oceans trembled.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe roar is too great to be held anywhere; its echo sets the worlds and oceans shaking. The effects are listed outward: sky (2.32), worlds and seas (2.33), earth and mountains (2.34).

Traditional interpretationThe roar is the first sign the asuras receive of her: it is this sound that Mahiṣa rushes towards in 2.37 (अभ्यधावत तं शब्दम्).

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: अमायता is taken as the instrumental of a negated present participle (‘uncontainable’). The same letters can be divided अमायत अतिमहता, with अमायत an imperfect ātmanepada of √मा (‘it was measured / contained’); but that leaves the clause without a negative and without a clear subject, so the participle reading is followed.

2.34Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

चचाल वसुधा चेलुः सकलाश्च महीधराः।
जयेति देवाश्च मुदा तामूचुः सिंहवाहिनीम् ॥ ३४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

चचाल | वसुधा | चेलुः | सकलाः | च | महीधराःजय | इति | देवाः | च | मुदा | ताम् | ऊचुः | सिंहवाहिनीम्

3IAST

As recitedcacāla vasudhā celuḥ sakalāśca mahīdharāḥ |
jayeti devāśca mudā tāmūcuḥ siṃhavāhinīm ||

Separatedcacāla | vasudhā | celuḥ | sakalāḥ | ca | mahīdharāḥ
jaya | iti | devāḥ | ca | mudā | tām | ūcuḥ | siṃhavāhinīm

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
चचालcacālashook√चल् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to move, shake’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
वसुधाvasudhāthe earthf. nom. sg. of वसुधा; compound (see Samāsa); subject of चचाल
चेलुःceluḥquaked√चल्, liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
सकलाःsakalāḥallm. nom. pl. of सकल; qualifies महीधराः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
महीधराःmahīdharāḥthe mountainsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of चेलुः
जयjayabe victorious! victory!√जि (bhvādi, 1P ‘to conquer’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
इतिitithus (closing the quotation)indeclinable (particle of quotation)
देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of ऊचुः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
मुदाmudāwith joyf. instr. sg. of मुद् (consonant stem); instrument of manner
ताम्tāmto herpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; the person addressed (√वच् takes two accusatives)
ऊचुःūcuḥsaid, cried√वच् (adādi, 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
सिंहवाहिनीम्siṃhavāhinīmher who rides the lionf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to ताम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सकलाश्च sakalāśca → सकलाः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c → ś; the same in देवाश्च.
  • जयेति jayeti → जय + इति
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + i → e.
  • तामूचुः tāmūcuḥ → ताम् + ऊचुः
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m joins the following vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • वसुधा vasudhā — upapada tatpuruṣa (√धा + kvip-type affix)
    Members: वसु + धा · Vigraha: वसूनि दधाति इति vasūni dadhāti iti
    Literally “she who holds wealth”; in the verse: the earth
  • महीधराः mahīdharāḥ — upapada tatpuruṣa (√धृ + ac)
    Members: मही + धर · Vigraha: महीं धरन्ति इति mahīṃ dharanti iti
    Literally “earth-holders”; in the verse: the mountains, which hold the earth firm
  • सिंहवाहिनीम् siṃhavāhinīm — upapada tatpuruṣa (√वह् + ṇini, feminine in -ī)
    Members: सिंह + वाहिनी · Vigraha: सिंहेन वहति इति सिंहवाहिनी, ताम् siṃhena vahati iti siṃhavāhinī, tām
    Literally “she who travels by a lion”; in the verse: the lion-rider — the lion just given by Himavat (2.29)
    Alternative: Often glossed as सिंहः वाहनं यस्याः सा, a bahuvrīhi ‘she whose mount is a lion’; strictly that analysis gives सिंहवाहना, and the -इनी ending marks the upapada formation.

C. Morphology

  • चेलुः celuḥ — Perfect 3rd plural of √चल् without reduplication: a root of the shape C-a-C with a single consonant on each side substitutes e for a and drops the reduplication in the weak forms (चेलुः, not *चचलुः). Contrast the singular चचाल at the start of the line.
  • ऊचुः ūcuḥ — Perfect 3rd plural of √वच् with samprasāraṇa: व → उ, and the reduplicated उ + उ gives ऊ (ऊचुः ‘they said’); compare उवाच.
  • जय jaya — Imperative 2nd singular of √जि on the stem जय-: the acclamation ‘Be victorious!’, quoted with इति.

D. Anvaya — prose order

वसुधा चचाल, सकलाः महीधराः च चेलुः। देवाः च मुदा तां सिंहवाहिनीं ‘जय’ इति ऊचुः।

vasudhā cacāla, sakalāḥ mahīdharāḥ ca celuḥ | devāḥ ca mudā tāṃ siṃhavāhinīṃ ‘jaya’ iti ūcuḥ |

Two clauses of upheaval, then the gods’ acclamation: ‘जय’ इति is the quoted word, ताम् the person addressed.

6Translation

LiteralThe earth shook, and all the mountains quaked. And the gods with joy said ‘Be victorious!’ to her, the lion-rider.

ReadableThe earth shook and all the mountains quaked, and the gods cried out in joy to the lion-rider, ‘Victory to you!’

‘Victory to you’ renders the imperative जय (‘be victorious’).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe roar reaches the earth and the mountains, and the gods answer it with one word: जय. सिंहवाहिनी is the first epithet the gods give her after her arming.

Traditional interpretationजय, ‘be victorious’, is at once an acclamation and a prayer. The same imperative is the refrain of the Argalā Stotra recited before the text in the Gita Press pāṭha (जय त्वं देवि चामुण्डे …), a separate liturgical hymn.

2.35Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तुष्टुवुर्मुनयश्चैनां भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयः।
दृष्ट्वा समस्तं संक्षुब्धं त्रैलोक्यममरारयः ॥ ३५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तुष्टुवुः | मुनयः | च | एनाम् | भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयःदृष्ट्वा | समस्तम् | संक्षुब्धम् | त्रैलोक्यम् | अमरारयः

3IAST

As recitedtuṣṭuvurmunayaścaināṃ bhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥ |
dṛṣṭvā samastaṃ saṃkṣubdhaṃ trailokyamamarārayaḥ ||

Separatedtuṣṭuvuḥ | munayaḥ | ca | enām | bhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥ
dṛṣṭvā | samastam | saṃkṣubdham | trailokyam | amarārayaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तुष्टुवुःtuṣṭuvuḥpraised√स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
मुनयःmunayaḥthe sagesm. nom. pl. of मुनि (i-stem); subject of तुष्टुवुः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); joins the sages to the gods of 2.34
एनाम्enāmherpronoun एनद् (the unemphatic form of एतद्/इदम् used for something already mentioned), f. acc. sg.; object
भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयःbhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥtheir bodies bowed in devotionm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मुनयः
दृष्ट्वाdṛṣṭvāhaving seenabsolutive (ktvā) of √दृश् ‘to see’; its agent is अमरारयः
समस्तम्samastamentire, the wholen. acc. sg. of समस्त; qualifies त्रैलोक्यम्
संक्षुब्धम्saṃkṣubdhamin turmoil, shakenn. acc. sg. of संक्षुब्ध (सम् + √क्षुभ् + kta); predicative with त्रैलोक्यम्
त्रैलोक्यम्trailokyamthe three worldsn. acc. sg. of त्रैलोक्य (taddhita from त्रिलोक, ‘the triad of worlds’); object of दृष्ट्वा
अमरारयःamarārayaḥthe enemies of the immortals (the asuras)m. nom. pl. of अमरारि (i-stem); compound (see Samāsa); subject of समुत्तस्थुः (2.36)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तुष्टुवुर्मुनयश्चैनां tuṣṭuvurmunayaścaināṃ → तुष्टुवुः + मुनयः + च + एनाम्
    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    Visarga after u before the voiced m appears as r (तुष्टुवुर्); visarga before c → ś (मुनयश्च); a + e → ai (चैनां).
  • भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयः bhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥ → भक्तिनम्र + आत्ममूर्तयः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • त्रैलोक्यममरारयः trailokyamamarārayaḥ → त्रैलोक्यम् + अमरारयः
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m joins the following vowel; inside the compound अमर + अरि → अमरारि (a + a → ā, 6.1.101).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयः bhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥ — bahuvrīhi, containing a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (भक्तिनम्र = भक्त्या नम्रः) and a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (आत्ममूर्ति = आत्मनः मूर्तिः)
    Members: भक्तिनम्र + आत्ममूर्ति · Vigraha: भक्त्या नम्राः आत्ममूर्तयः येषां ते bhaktyā namrāḥ ātmamūrtayaḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “they whose own bodies were bent by devotion”; in the verse: the sages, bowing low in devotion
  • अमरारयः amarārayaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (अमर itself न म्रियते इति, ‘undying’)
    Members: अमर + अरि · Vigraha: अमराणाम् अरयः amarāṇām arayaḥ
    Literally “the foes of the immortals”; in the verse: the asuras

C. Morphology

  • तुष्टुवुः tuṣṭuvuḥ — Perfect 3rd plural of √स्तु: reduplication तु- with the root’s s made ṣ after the reduplicating u (तु-ष्टु-), and u → uv before the ending -उः.
  • एनाम् enām — The enclitic pronoun एन- is used only for a person or thing already named (anvādeśa): ‘her’, the Goddess of 2.34.
  • दृष्ट्वा dṛṣṭvā — Absolutive of √दृश् (ś + tvā → ṣṭvā). Its subject is the subject of the main verb: the asuras saw, and then rose (2.36).

D. Anvaya — prose order

भक्तिनम्रात्ममूर्तयः मुनयः च एनां तुष्टुवुः। अमरारयः समस्तं त्रैलोक्यं संक्षुब्धं दृष्ट्वा (2.36: सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याः समुत्तस्थुः)।

bhaktinamrātmamūrtayaḥ munayaḥ ca enāṃ tuṣṭuvuḥ | amarārayaḥ samastaṃ trailokyaṃ saṃkṣubdhaṃ dṛṣṭvā (2.36: sannaddhākhilasainyāḥ samuttasthuḥ) |

The first line closes the scene of praise. The second line starts a new sentence: the absolutive clause with its subject अमरारयः, whose main verb comes in 2.36.

6Translation

LiteralAnd the sages, their own bodies bowed with devotion, praised her. Having seen the whole three worlds agitated, the enemies of the immortals —

ReadableThe sages, bowing low in devotion, sang her praise. But the enemies of the gods, seeing the three worlds in turmoil,

‘But’ marks the change of scene, which the Sanskrit makes by a new subject; the sentence continues into 2.36.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningTwo answers to the roar. The sages praise her with bowed bodies; the asuras, seeing the worlds in upheaval, prepare for war (2.36). The same event is heard as grace by the one side and as threat by the other.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the verse sets reverence and alarm side by side as responses to one and the same power. What a person sees in an upheaval says something about where they stand.

2.36Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्यास्ते समुत्तस्थुरुदायुधाः।
आः किमेतदिति क्रोधादाभाष्य महिषासुरः ॥ ३६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याः | ते | समुत्तस्थुः | उदायुधाःआः | किम् | एतत् | इति | क्रोधात् | आभाष्य | महिषासुरः

3IAST

As recitedsannaddhākhilasainyāste samuttasthurudāyudhāḥ |
āḥ kimetaditi krodhādābhāṣya mahiṣāsuraḥ ||

Separatedsannaddhākhilasainyāḥ | te | samuttasthuḥ | udāyudhāḥ
āḥ | kim | etat | iti | krodhāt | ābhāṣya | mahiṣāsuraḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याःsannaddhākhilasainyāḥwith all their armies armed and readym. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ते
तेtetheypronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; resumes अमरारयः (2.35)
समुत्तस्थुःsamuttasthuḥrose upसम् + उद् + √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
उदायुधाःudāyudhāḥwith weapons raisedm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ते
आःāḥAh! (in anger)indeclinable (interjection of anger or surprise)
किम्kimwhat?interrogative pronoun किम्, n. nom. sg.
एतत्etat(is) thispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.
इतिitithus (closing the quotation)indeclinable (particle of quotation)
क्रोधात्krodhātin anger, out of angerm. abl. sg. of क्रोध; ablative of cause
आभाष्यābhāṣyahaving exclaimedabsolutive (lyap) of आ + √भाष् ‘to speak, address’
महिषासुरःmahiṣāsuraḥMahiṣāsuram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of आभाष्य and of अभ्यधावत (2.37)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्यास्ते sannaddhākhilasainyāste → सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याः + ते
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s. Inside the compound सन्नद्ध + अखिल → सन्नद्धाखिल (a + a → ā, 6.1.101).
  • समुत्तस्थुरुदायुधाः samuttasthurudāyudhāḥ → समुत्तस्थुः + उदायुधाः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after u before a vowel appears as r.
  • किमेतदिति kimetaditi → किम् + एतत् + इति
    final m before a vowel; consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final m joins the vowel; final t becomes d before the vowel of इति.
  • क्रोधादाभाष्य krodhādābhāṣya → क्रोधात् + आभाष्य
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t → d before a vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याः sannaddhākhilasainyāḥ — bahuvrīhi (अखिल itself a nañ bahuvrīhi, न विद्यते खिलम् यस्य, ‘without remainder’)
    Members: सन्नद्ध + अखिल + सैन्य · Vigraha: सन्नद्धानि अखिलानि सैन्यानि येषां ते sannaddhāni akhilāni sainyāni yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “they whose entire armies were girded”; in the verse: with their whole forces armed and in array
  • उदायुधाः udāyudhāḥ — bahuvrīhi (prādi type: the prefix उद् stands for उद्यत ‘raised’)
    Members: उद् + आयुध · Vigraha: उद्यतानि आयुधानि येषां ते udyatāni āyudhāni yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “they whose weapons were up”; in the verse: brandishing their weapons
  • महिषासुरः mahiṣāsuraḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: महिष + असुर · Vigraha: महिषः च असौ असुरः च mahiṣaḥ ca asau asuraḥ ca
    Literally “the buffalo-asura”; in the verse: the asura who has the form of a buffalo
    Alternative: Also read as a madhyamapadalopin karmadhāraya, महिषरूपः असुरः ‘the asura in buffalo form’; the sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • समुत्तस्थुः samuttasthuḥ — Perfect of √स्था (reduplicated त-स्थ्-, the s dropped in the reduplicating syllable) with सम् + उद्. उद् + स्था loses its s (उत्था-), so the d of उद् appears as t: सम्-उत्-तस्थुः.
  • आभाष्य ābhāṣya — Absolutive with a prefix takes -य (lyap), not -त्वा: आ + √भाष् → आभाष्य ‘having called out’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(अमरारयः) ते सन्नद्धाखिलसैन्याः उदायुधाः समुत्तस्थुः। महिषासुरः ‘आः, किम् एतत्’ इति क्रोधात् आभाष्य (2.37: … अभ्यधावत)।

(amarārayaḥ) te sannaddhākhilasainyāḥ udāyudhāḥ samuttasthuḥ | mahiṣāsuraḥ ‘āḥ, kim etat’ iti krodhāt ābhāṣya (2.37: … abhyadhāvata) |

The first line completes 2.35b. The second gives Mahiṣāsura’s cry, quoted with इति, as an absolutive clause; his main verb is in 2.37.

6Translation

Literalthey, their entire armies armed, rose up with weapons raised. Having exclaimed in anger, ‘Ah! What is this?’, Mahiṣāsura —

Readablerose up, weapons raised, their whole army in arms. ‘Ha! What is this?’ cried Mahiṣāsura in fury,

The absolutive आभाष्य (‘having exclaimed’) is rendered as a finite verb; the sentence runs on into 2.37.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe asuras arm themselves, and Mahiṣāsura appears in the narrative for the first time in person, with an angry question: ‘What is this?’ He does not yet know what the sound is.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: Mahiṣa meets the Goddess first as a disturbance to be put down — ‘What is this?’ — not as a power to be understood. The question is asked in anger, and anger does not wait for the answer.

2.37Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अभ्यधावत तं शब्दमशेषैरसुरैर्वृतः।
स ददर्श ततो देवीं व्याप्तलोकत्रयां त्विषा ॥ ३७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अभ्यधावत | तम् | शब्दम् | अशेषैः | असुरैः | वृतःसः | ददर्श | ततः | देवीम् | व्याप्तलोकत्रयाम् | त्विषा

3IAST

As recitedabhyadhāvata taṃ śabdamaśeṣairasurairvṛtaḥ |
sa dadarśa tato devīṃ vyāptalokatrayāṃ tviṣā ||

Separatedabhyadhāvata | tam | śabdam | aśeṣaiḥ | asuraiḥ | vṛtaḥ
saḥ | dadarśa | tataḥ | devīm | vyāptalokatrayām | tviṣā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अभ्यधावतabhyadhāvatarushed towardsअभि + √धाव् (bhvādi, 1U ‘to run’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
तम्tamthatpronoun तद्, m. acc. sg.; qualifies शब्दम्
शब्दम्śabdamsoundm. acc. sg. of शब्द; goal of अभ्यधावत
अशेषैःaśeṣaiḥby all, by the whole host ofm. instr. pl. of अशेष (‘without remainder’); qualifies असुरैः
असुरैःasuraiḥby the asurasm. instr. pl. of असुर; agent of वृतः
वृतःvṛtaḥsurroundedpast participle (kta) of √वृ ‘to cover, surround’, m. nom. sg.; qualifies महिषासुरः (2.36)
सःsaḥhepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; subject of ददर्श
ददर्शdadarśasaw√दृश् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to see’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
देवीम्devīmthe Goddessf. acc. sg. of देवी; object of ददर्श
व्याप्तलोकत्रयाम्vyāptalokatrayāmwho pervaded the three worldsf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवीम्
त्विषाtviṣāwith her radiancef. instr. sg. of त्विष् (consonant stem ‘light, splendour’)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शब्दमशेषैरसुरैर्वृतः śabdamaśeṣairasurairvṛtaḥ → शब्दम् + अशेषैः + असुरैः + वृतः
    final m before a vowel; visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Final m joins the vowel; visarga after ai before a vowel or a voiced consonant appears as r (अशेषैरसुरैर्वृतः).
  • स ददर्श sa dadarśa → सः + ददर्श
    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    The visarga of सः is dropped before a consonant.
  • ततो देवीं tato devīṃ → ततः + देवीम्
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • व्याप्तलोकत्रयाम् vyāptalokatrayām — bahuvrīhi (लोकत्रय a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, लोकानां त्रयम्)
    Members: व्याप्त + लोकत्रय · Vigraha: व्याप्तं लोकत्रयं यया सा, ताम् vyāptaṃ lokatrayaṃ yayā sā, tām
    Literally “she by whom the triad of worlds is pervaded”; in the verse: filling the three worlds (with her light)

C. Morphology

  • अभ्यधावत abhyadhāvata — Imperfect ātmanepada of √धाव् with अभि (augment a- after the prefix: अभि + अ + धावत → अभ्यधावत). With a goal in the accusative: ‘ran at, charged towards’.
  • ददर्श dadarśa — Perfect of √दृश् with guṇa of the root vowel (दर्श्) and reduplication द-: ‘he saw’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(महिषासुरः) अशेषैः असुरैः वृतः तं शब्दम् अभ्यधावत। ततः सः त्विषा व्याप्तलोकत्रयां देवीं ददर्श।

(mahiṣāsuraḥ) aśeṣaiḥ asuraiḥ vṛtaḥ taṃ śabdam abhyadhāvata | tataḥ saḥ tviṣā vyāptalokatrayāṃ devīṃ dadarśa |

The first line gives Mahiṣāsura’s main verb (subject in 2.36); the second line is a new sentence whose object is described through 2.38–39a.

6Translation

Literalrushed towards that sound, surrounded by all the asuras. Then he saw the Goddess, who pervaded the three worlds with her radiance,

Readableand charged towards the sound with all his asuras around him. Then he saw the Goddess, her radiance filling the three worlds,

The second sentence continues through 2.38 into 2.39a, all describing the Goddess as he sees her.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMahiṣa runs towards the sound and only then sees its source. The description of her begins with the phrase used for the gods’ gathered light in 2.13 (व्याप्तलोकत्रयं त्विषा): the light that became her body still fills the worlds. The vision runs on through 2.38–39a.

2.38Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

पादाक्रान्त्या नतभुवं किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम्।
क्षोभिताशेषपातालां धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन ताम् ॥ ३८ ॥

पादाक्रान्त्या | नतभुवम् | किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम्क्षोभिताशेषपातालाम् | धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन | ताम्

As recitedpādākrāntyā natabhuvaṃ kirīṭollikhitāmbarām |
kṣobhitāśeṣapātālāṃ dhanurjyāniḥsvanena tām ||

Separatedpādākrāntyā | natabhuvam | kirīṭollikhitāmbarām
kṣobhitāśeṣapātālām | dhanurjyāniḥsvanena | tām

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
पादाक्रान्त्याpādākrāntyāby the tread of her feetf. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); construed with नतभुवम्
नतभुवम्natabhuvamby whom the earth was bowed downf. acc. sg. of नतभू; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ताम् / देवीम् (2.37)
किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम्kirīṭollikhitāmbarāmwhose crown grazed the skyf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ताम्
क्षोभिताशेषपातालाम्kṣobhitāśeṣapātālāmwho shook all the netherworldsf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ताम्
धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेनdhanurjyāniḥsvanenawith the twang of her bowstringm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); construed with क्षोभिताशेषपातालाम्
ताम्tāmherpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; resumes देवीम् (2.37)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पादाक्रान्त्या pādākrāntyā → पाद + आक्रान्त्या
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम् kirīṭollikhitāmbarām → किरीट + उल्लिखित + अम्बराम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa and savarṇa-dīrgha); consonant sandhi inside the prefix · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); तोर्लि (8.4.60)
    a + u → o (किरीटोल्लिखित); a + a → ā (-ताम्बराम्). In उल्लिखित the d of उद् has become l before l (उद् + लिखित).
  • क्षोभिताशेषपातालां kṣobhitāśeṣapātālāṃ → क्षोभित + अशेषपातालाम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha); anusvāra · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    a + a → ā; the final m becomes anusvāra before the dh of the next word.
  • धनुर्ज्या dhanurjyā → धनुस् + ज्या
    visarga sandhi inside a compound · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The s of धनुस् becomes r before the voiced j.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पादाक्रान्त्या pādākrāntyā — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: पाद + आक्रान्ति · Vigraha: पादयोः आक्रान्तिः, तया pādayoḥ ākrāntiḥ, tayā
    Literally “by the stepping-on of the feet”; in the verse: by the weight of her tread
  • नतभुवम् natabhuvam — bahuvrīhi
    Members: नत + भू · Vigraha: नता भूः यया सा, ताम् natā bhūḥ yayā sā, tām
    Literally “she by whom the earth is bowed”; in the verse: the earth sinking under her step
  • किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम् kirīṭollikhitāmbarām — bahuvrīhi (किरीटोल्लिखित a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, किरीटेन उल्लिखितम्)
    Members: किरीटोल्लिखित + अम्बर · Vigraha: किरीटेन उल्लिखितम् अम्बरं यया सा, ताम् kirīṭena ullikhitam ambaraṃ yayā sā, tām
    Literally “she by whom the sky was scraped with the crown”; in the verse: so tall that her diadem touched the sky
  • क्षोभिताशेषपातालाम् kṣobhitāśeṣapātālām — bahuvrīhi (अशेषपाताल a karmadhāraya, अशेषाणि पातालानि)
    Members: क्षोभित + अशेषपाताल · Vigraha: क्षोभितानि अशेषाणि पातालानि यया सा, ताम् kṣobhitāni aśeṣāṇi pātālāni yayā sā, tām
    Literally “she by whom all the netherworlds were stirred”; in the verse: shaking the underworlds to their depths
  • धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन dhanurjyāniḥsvanena — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (धनुर्ज्या also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, धनुषः ज्या)
    Members: धनुर्ज्या + निःस्वन · Vigraha: धनुर्ज्यायाः निःस्वनः, तेन dhanurjyāyāḥ niḥsvanaḥ, tena
    Literally “by the sound of the string of the bow”; in the verse: by the twang of her bowstring

C. Morphology

  • नतभुवम् natabhuvam — The stem भू ‘earth’ keeps its form at the end of a bahuvrīhi; before a vowel ending ū becomes uv (भुवम्), as in the simple noun (भुवम्, भुवि).
  • पादाक्रान्त्या pādākrāntyā — आक्रान्ति is an action noun (ktin) from आ + √क्रम् ‘to step upon’; its instrumental gives the cause of the earth’s bowing.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सः) पादाक्रान्त्या नतभुवं, किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बरां, धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन क्षोभिताशेषपातालां तां (देवीं ददर्श)।

(saḥ) pādākrāntyā natabhuvaṃ, kirīṭollikhitāmbarāṃ, dhanurjyāniḥsvanena kṣobhitāśeṣapātālāṃ tāṃ (devīṃ dadarśa) |

All accusatives in apposition to देवीम् (2.37); each bahuvrīhi has its own instrumental of cause beside it.

6Translation

Literalher by whose tread the earth was bowed, by whose crown the sky was scraped, by the twang of whose bowstring all the netherworlds were shaken —

Readablethe earth bending beneath her tread, her crown grazing the sky, the twang of her bowstring shaking the underworlds to their depths,

The verse continues the object of ददर्श (2.37); the next verse completes it.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningWhat Mahiṣa sees is the Goddess as large as the cosmos: her foot presses the earth, her crown touches the sky, and the sound of her bow reaches the netherworlds. The three levels of the world are each touched by her.

Traditional interpretationPurāṇic cosmology divides the world into the heavens above, the earth, and the pātālas below. The verse touches all three, so that before a blow is struck the asura sees that the one he means to fight is as wide as the world.

2.39Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

दिशो भुजसहस्रेण समन्ताद् व्याप्य संस्थिताम्।
ततः प्रववृते युद्धं तया देव्या सुरद्विषाम् ॥ ३९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

दिशः | भुजसहस्रेण | समन्तात् | व्याप्य | संस्थिताम्ततः | प्रववृते | युद्धम् | तया | देव्या | सुरद्विषाम्

3IAST

As reciteddiśo bhujasahasreṇa samantād vyāpya saṃsthitām |
tataḥ pravavṛte yuddhaṃ tayā devyā suradviṣām ||

Separateddiśaḥ | bhujasahasreṇa | samantāt | vyāpya | saṃsthitām
tataḥ | pravavṛte | yuddham | tayā | devyā | suradviṣām

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
दिशःdiśaḥthe directionsf. acc. pl. of दिश् (consonant stem); object of व्याप्य
भुजसहस्रेणbhujasahasreṇawith a thousand armsn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of व्याप्य
समन्तात्samantāton every sideindeclinable (ablative form used as an adverb)
व्याप्यvyāpyahaving pervadedabsolutive (lyap) of वि + √आप् ‘to reach, pervade’
संस्थिताम्saṃsthitāmstandingf. acc. sg. of संस्थित (सम् + √स्था + kta); qualifies ताम् (2.38) / देवीम् (2.37)
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
प्रववृतेpravavṛtebegan, broke outप्र + √वृत् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to turn, happen’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
युद्धम्yuddhamthe battlen. nom. sg. of युद्ध; subject of प्रववृते
तयाtayāwith thatpronoun तद्, f. instr. sg.; qualifies देव्या
देव्याdevyāwith the Goddessf. instr. sg. of देवी; instrumental of the opponent (‘battle with’)
सुरद्विषाम्suradviṣāmof the enemies of the godsm. gen. pl. of सुरद्विष्; compound (see Samāsa); depends on युद्धम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • दिशो भुजसहस्रेण diśo bhujasahasreṇa → दिशः + भुजसहस्रेण
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced bh becomes -o.
  • समन्ताद् व्याप्य samantād vyāpya → समन्तात् + व्याप्य
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t → d before the voiced v; written with a virāma and a space.
  • युद्धं तया yuddhaṃ tayā → युद्धम् + तया
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • भुजसहस्रेण bhujasahasreṇa — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: भुज + सहस्र · Vigraha: भुजानां सहस्रम्, तेन bhujānāṃ sahasram, tena
    Literally “with a thousand of arms”; in the verse: with her thousand arms
  • सुरद्विषाम् suradviṣām — upapada tatpuruṣa (√द्विष् + kvip)
    Members: सुर + द्विष् · Vigraha: सुरान् द्विषन्ति इति सुरद्विषः, तेषाम् surān dviṣanti iti suradviṣaḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of the god-haters”; in the verse: of the asuras

C. Morphology

  • प्रववृते pravavṛte — Perfect ātmanepada of √वृत् with प्र: reduplicated व-वृत्-, ending -ए. ‘(The battle) began, set in’.
  • व्याप्य vyāpya — वि + √आप् (वि → व्य् before आ by yaṇ) with -य: ‘having filled, having spread over’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सः) भुजसहस्रेण समन्तात् दिशः व्याप्य संस्थितां (तां देवीं ददर्श)। ततः तया देव्या (सह) सुरद्विषां युद्धं प्रववृते।

(saḥ) bhujasahasreṇa samantāt diśaḥ vyāpya saṃsthitāṃ (tāṃ devīṃ dadarśa) | tataḥ tayā devyā (saha) suradviṣāṃ yuddhaṃ pravavṛte |

The first line ends the description of the Goddess (object of ददर्श, 2.37). The second line is a new sentence; तया देव्या is the instrumental of the one fought against (सह is understood).

6Translation

Literalstanding, having pervaded the directions on every side with a thousand arms. Then the battle of the enemies of the gods with that Goddess began,

Readablestanding with her thousand arms spread through every quarter. Then the battle began between the Goddess and the enemies of the gods,

The second line continues into 2.40, which describes the battle.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe vision is completed by her arms, a thousand of them, filling the directions; then the battle begins. ‘A thousand’ here means beyond counting, completing the vertical vastness of 2.38 with the horizontal.

Traditional interpretationThe madhyama-carita dhyāna of the Gita Press pāṭha pictures Mahālakṣmī with eighteen objects in her hands; the text’s own vision here is of a thousand arms. The two do not compete: the dhyāna is a form for meditation, the verse a vision of her cosmic extent.

2.40Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शस्त्रास्त्रैर्बहुधा मुक्तैरादीपितदिगन्तरम्।
महिषासुरसेनानीश्चिक्षुराख्यो महासुरः ॥ ४० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शस्त्रास्त्रैः | बहुधा | मुक्तैः | आदीपितदिगन्तरम्महिषासुरसेनानीः | चिक्षुराख्यः | महासुरः

3IAST

As recitedśastrāstrairbahudhā muktairādīpitadigantaram |
mahiṣāsurasenānīścikṣurākhyo mahāsuraḥ ||

Separatedśastrāstraiḥ | bahudhā | muktaiḥ | ādīpitadigantaram
mahiṣāsurasenānīḥ | cikṣurākhyaḥ | mahāsuraḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
शस्त्रास्त्रैःśastrāstraiḥby weapons and missilesn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of the participle in आदीपित-
बहुधाbahudhāin many ways, in profusionindeclinable (बहु + taddhita -धा ‘-fold’)
मुक्तैःmuktaiḥdischarged, let flyn. instr. pl. of मुक्त (past participle, kta, of √मुच् ‘to release’); qualifies शस्त्रास्त्रैः
आदीपितदिगन्तरम्ādīpitadigantaramin which the spaces between the quarters were set ablazen. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies युद्धम् (2.39)
महिषासुरसेनानीःmahiṣāsurasenānīḥMahiṣāsura’s generalm. nom. sg. of the ī-stem सेनानी at the end of a compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to चिक्षुराख्यः
चिक्षुराख्यःcikṣurākhyaḥnamed Cikṣuram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of युयुधे (2.41)
महासुरःmahāsuraḥthe great asuram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शस्त्रास्त्रैर्बहुधा śastrāstrairbahudhā → शस्त्रास्त्रैः + बहुधा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after ai before a voiced consonant appears as r; likewise मुक्तैरादीपित- before a vowel.
  • दिगन्तरम् digantaram → दिक् + अन्तरम्
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    दिश् ends in k at the end of a member (दिक्), and k becomes g before the vowel of अन्तर.
  • सेनानीश्चिक्षुराख्यो महासुरः senānīścikṣurākhyo mahāsuraḥ → सेनानीः + चिक्षुराख्यः + महासुरः
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Visarga before c → ś (सेनानीश्चि-); -aḥ before the voiced m → -o (चिक्षुराख्यो).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शस्त्रास्त्रैः śastrāstraiḥ — dvandva (itaretara)
    Members: शस्त्र + अस्त्र · Vigraha: शस्त्राणि च अस्त्राणि च, तैः śastrāṇi ca astrāṇi ca, taiḥ
    Literally “by weapons and missiles”; in the verse: hand-weapons and weapons hurled
  • आदीपितदिगन्तरम् ādīpitadigantaram — bahuvrīhi (दिगन्तर a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, दिशाम् अन्तरम्)
    Members: आदीपित + दिगन्तर · Vigraha: आदीपितानि दिगन्तराणि यस्मिन् तत् ādīpitāni digantarāṇi yasmin tat
    Literally “that in which the intervals of the directions were kindled”; in the verse: a battle whose weapons lit up the whole horizon
  • महिषासुरसेनानीः mahiṣāsurasenānīḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: महिषासुर + सेनानी · Vigraha: महिषासुरस्य सेनानीः (सेनानी = सेनां नयति इति, upapada tatpuruṣa with √नी + kvip) mahiṣāsurasya senānīḥ (senānī = senāṃ nayati iti, upapada tatpuruṣa with √nī + kvip)
    Literally “army-leader of Mahiṣāsura”; in the verse: Mahiṣāsura’s commander-in-chief
  • चिक्षुराख्यः cikṣurākhyaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: चिक्षुर + आख्या · Vigraha: चिक्षुरः आख्या यस्य सः cikṣuraḥ ākhyā yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose name is Cikṣura”; in the verse: Cikṣura by name
  • महासुरः mahāsuraḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुर · Vigraha: महान् चासौ असुरः च mahān cāsau asuraḥ ca
    Literally “a great asura”; in the verse: a mighty asura (महत् becomes महा- as the first member of a karmadhāraya)

C. Morphology

  • महिषासुरसेनानीः mahiṣāsurasenānīḥ — सेनानी is a root-noun in -ī (from √नी ‘to lead’); its nominative singular keeps the visarga: सेनानीः. Chapter 3 calls Cikṣura सेनानीः again (3.2) and, after his death, महिषस्य चमूपतिः (3.11).
  • आदीपितदिगन्तरम् ādīpitadigantaram — Neuter, to agree with युद्धम् of 2.39: the battle is described before its first fighter is named. आदीपित is the past participle of the causative of आ + √दीप् ‘set ablaze’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(तत् युद्धं) बहुधा मुक्तैः शस्त्रास्त्रैः आदीपितदिगन्तरम् (आसीत्)। महिषासुरसेनानीः चिक्षुराख्यः महासुरः (2.41: युयुधे)।

(tat yuddhaṃ) bahudhā muktaiḥ śastrāstraiḥ ādīpitadigantaram (āsīt) | mahiṣāsurasenānīḥ cikṣurākhyaḥ mahāsuraḥ (2.41: yuyudhe) |

The first line qualifies युद्धम् (2.39). The second introduces Cikṣura, the subject of युयुधे at the head of 2.41.

6Translation

Literal(a battle) in which the spaces between the quarters were lit up by weapons and missiles discharged in many ways. Mahiṣāsura’s general, the great asura named Cikṣura,

Readablea battle in which the weapons and missiles they hurled set the whole horizon ablaze. Cikṣura, the mighty asura, Mahiṣāsura’s commander,

The sentence of the second line is completed by युयुधे in 2.41.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe battle is first seen as a whole — the horizon lit by weapons — and then its leaders are named one by one, beginning with Cikṣura, Mahiṣa’s commander. He is the first to attack the Goddess in chapter 3 (3.2).

Traditional interpretationFrom here to 2.47 the text gives a muster-roll of Mahiṣa’s generals with the size of their forces. Most of them are killed by name in chapter 3: Cikṣura (3.2ff.), Cāmara (3.11–16), Udagra (3.17), Bāṣkala (3.18), Mahāhanu (3.19), Biḍāla (3.20).

2.41Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

युयुधे चामरश्चान्यैश्चतुरङ्गबलान्वितः।
रथानामयुतैः षड्भिरुदग्राख्यो महासुरः ॥ ४१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

युयुधे | चामरः | च | अन्यैः | चतुरङ्गबलान्वितःरथानाम् | अयुतैः | षड्भिः | उदग्राख्यः | महासुरः

rathānāmayutaiḥ ṣaḍbhirudagrākhyo mahāsuraḥ ||

rathānām | ayutaiḥ | ṣaḍbhiḥ | udagrākhyaḥ | mahāsuraḥ

चामरःcāmaraḥCāmaram. nom. sg. of चामर, proper name (as a common noun ‘yak-tail fly-whisk’); subject
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
अन्यैःanyaiḥwith otherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. instr. pl.; instrumental of accompaniment
चतुरङ्गबलान्वितःcaturaṅgabalānvitaḥaccompanied by an army of four divisionsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies चामरः
रथानाम्rathānāmof chariotsm. gen. pl. of रथ; depends on अयुतैः
अयुतैःayutaiḥwith ten-thousandsn. instr. pl. of अयुत (‘ten thousand’); instrumental of accompaniment
षड्भिःṣaḍbhiḥsixnumeral षष्, instr. pl.; qualifies अयुतैः
उदग्राख्यःudagrākhyaḥnamed Udagram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of अयुध्यत (2.42)
महासुरःmahāsuraḥthe great asuram. nom. sg.; compound (see 2.40); in apposition

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • चामरश्चान्यैश्चतुरङ्ग cāmaraścānyaiścaturaṅga → चामरः + च + अन्यैः + चतुरङ्ग-
    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Visarga before c → ś (चामरश्च, अन्यैश्च-); a + a → ā (चान्यैः).
  • रथानामयुतैः rathānāmayutaiḥ → रथानाम् + अयुतैः
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m joins the following vowel.
  • षड्भिरुदग्राख्यो महासुरः ṣaḍbhirudagrākhyo mahāsuraḥ → षड्भिः + उदग्राख्यः + महासुरः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Visarga after i before a vowel appears as r; -aḥ before m becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • चतुरङ्गबलान्वितः caturaṅgabalānvitaḥ — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (चतुरङ्गबल a karmadhāraya, चतुरङ्गं बलम्; चतुरङ्ग a bahuvrīhi, चत्वारि अङ्गानि यस्य तत्)
    Members: चतुरङ्गबल + अन्वित · Vigraha: चतुरङ्गबलेन अन्वितः caturaṅgabalena anvitaḥ
    Literally “joined with a four-limbed army”; in the verse: with a full army of elephants, chariots, cavalry and foot
  • उदग्राख्यः udagrākhyaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: उदग्र + आख्या · Vigraha: उदग्रः आख्या यस्य सः udagraḥ ākhyā yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose name is Udagra (‘towering’)”; in the verse: Udagra by name

C. Morphology

  • युयुधे yuyudhe — Perfect of the ātmanepada root √युध्: reduplication यु- + युध् + -ए. Its subject is Cikṣura (2.40), and it serves Cāmara too.
  • षड्भिः ṣaḍbhiḥ — Instrumental plural of षष् ‘six’: before -भिः the ṣ appears as ḍ (षड्भिः). Six ayutas = sixty thousand.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(चिक्षुराख्यः महासुरः) युयुधे; चामरः च चतुरङ्गबलान्वितः अन्यैः (सह युयुधे)। उदग्राख्यः महासुरः रथानां षड्भिः अयुतैः (2.42: अयुध्यत)।

(cikṣurākhyaḥ mahāsuraḥ) yuyudhe; cāmaraḥ ca caturaṅgabalānvitaḥ anyaiḥ (saha yuyudhe) | udagrākhyaḥ mahāsuraḥ rathānāṃ ṣaḍbhiḥ ayutaiḥ (2.42: ayudhyata) |

युयुधे closes the sentence of 2.40b and is understood again with Cāmara. The second line names Udagra, whose verb is अयुध्यत at the head of 2.42.

6Translation

Literalfought; and Cāmara, with others, accompanied by an army of four divisions. The great asura named Udagra, with six ten-thousands of chariots,

Readablejoined battle; so did Cāmara, with others and an army of all four arms. Udagra, the mighty asura, with sixty thousand chariots,

‘Sixty thousand’ renders षड्भिः अयुतैः (six ayutas). The second line continues into 2.42.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe first three generals: Cikṣura, Cāmara with a full army, and Udagra with sixty thousand chariots. The fourfold army (चतुरङ्ग) is the classical Indian army of elephants, chariots, horse and foot.

Traditional interpretationThe numbers grow as the roll goes on — tens of thousands, then millions, then crores — to show the scale of what one Goddess faces. The point is the disproportion, not the arithmetic.

2.42Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अयुध्यतायुतानां च सहस्रेण महाहनुः।
पञ्चाशद्भिश्च नियुतैरसिलोमा महासुरः ॥ ४२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अयुध्यत | अयुतानाम् | च | सहस्रेण | महाहनुःपञ्चाशद्भिः | च | नियुतैः | असिलोमा | महासुरः

3IAST

As recitedayudhyatāyutānāṃ ca sahasreṇa mahāhanuḥ |
pañcāśadbhiśca niyutairasilomā mahāsuraḥ ||

Separatedayudhyata | ayutānām | ca | sahasreṇa | mahāhanuḥ
pañcāśadbhiḥ | ca | niyutaiḥ | asilomā | mahāsuraḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अयुध्यतayudhyatafought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada; subject Udagra (2.41), and understood again with Mahāhanu
अयुतानाम्ayutānāmof ten-thousandsn. gen. pl. of अयुत; depends on सहस्रेण
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
सहस्रेणsahasreṇawith a thousandn. instr. sg. of सहस्र; instrumental of accompaniment
महाहनुःmahāhanuḥMahāhanu (‘Great-Jaw’)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa), used as a proper name; subject
पञ्चाशद्भिःpañcāśadbhiḥwith fiftynumeral पञ्चाशत् (f.), instr. pl.; qualifies नियुतैः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
नियुतैःniyutaiḥwith niyutas (millions)n. instr. pl. of नियुत (a large number, usually a million); instrumental of accompaniment
असिलोमाasilomāAsiloman (‘Sword-Hair’)m. nom. sg. of असिलोमन् (n-stem); compound (see Samāsa), used as a proper name; subject of a supplied verb
महासुरःmahāsuraḥthe great asuram. nom. sg.; compound (see 2.40); in apposition

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • अयुध्यतायुतानां ayudhyatāyutānāṃ → अयुध्यत + अयुतानाम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • पञ्चाशद्भिश्च pañcāśadbhiśca → पञ्चाशद्भिः + च
    visarga sandhi; voicing inside the word · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c → ś. In पञ्चाशद्भिः the t of पञ्चाशत् is voiced to d before the ending -भिः.
  • नियुतैरसिलोमा niyutairasilomā → नियुतैः + असिलोमा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after ai before a vowel appears as r.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महाहनुः mahāhanuḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: महत् + हनु · Vigraha: महती हनुः यस्य सः mahatī hanuḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose jaw is great”; in the verse: ‘Great-Jaw’, a name
  • असिलोमा asilomā — bahuvrīhi (with an implied comparison)
    Members: असि + लोमन् · Vigraha: असयः इव लोमानि यस्य सः asayaḥ iva lomāni yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose hairs are (like) swords”; in the verse: ‘Sword-Hair’, a name

C. Morphology

  • अयुध्यत ayudhyata — Imperfect of √युध् on the divādi stem युध्य- with augment a-: ‘(he) fought’. Its use beside the perfect युयुधे shows the two tenses alternating freely in narrative.
  • असिलोमा asilomā — Nominative of an n-stem (लोमन् ‘hair’) at the end of a bahuvrīhi: -मन् → -मा, as in विश्वकर्मा (2.27).

D. Anvaya — prose order

(उदग्राख्यः महासुरः रथानां षड्भिः अयुतैः) अयुध्यत; महाहनुः च अयुतानां सहस्रेण (अयुध्यत)। असिलोमा महासुरः च पञ्चाशद्भिः नियुतैः (अयुध्यत)।

(udagrākhyaḥ mahāsuraḥ rathānāṃ ṣaḍbhiḥ ayutaiḥ) ayudhyata; mahāhanuḥ ca ayutānāṃ sahasreṇa (ayudhyata) | asilomā mahāsuraḥ ca pañcāśadbhiḥ niyutaiḥ (ayudhyata) |

अयुध्यत completes 2.41b and is understood for Mahāhanu and Asiloman. Each general is given with the instrumental of the force that accompanies him.

6Translation

Literalfought; and Mahāhanu with a thousand of ten-thousands; and with fifty niyutas the great asura Asiloman,

Readablefought on, and Mahāhanu with ten million; and the mighty Asiloman with fifty million,

A thousand ayutas is ten million; fifty niyutas is rendered ‘fifty million’, taking a niyuta as a million (see Flags).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningUdagra fights; Mahāhanu (‘Great-Jaw’) comes with a thousand ayutas, ten million; Asiloman (‘Sword-Hair’) with fifty niyutas. The names describe monstrous bodies.

Traditional interpretationMahāhanu is killed by the Goddess’s trident in 3.19. Asiloman is not named again in the Gita Press text of chapter 3.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The value of नियुत varies in the lexical tradition (a million in most lists, a hundred thousand in some); fifty niyutas is therefore fifty million or five million.

2.43Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अयुतानां शतैः षड्भिर्बाष्कलो युयुधे रणे।
गजवाजिसहस्रौघैरनेकैः परिवारितः ॥ ४३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अयुतानाम् | शतैः | षड्भिः | बाष्कलः | युयुधे | रणेगजवाजिसहस्रौघैः | अनेकैः | परिवारितः

3IAST

As recitedayutānāṃ śataiḥ ṣaḍbhirbāṣkalo yuyudhe raṇe |
gajavājisahasraughairanekaiḥ parivāritaḥ ||

Separatedayutānām | śataiḥ | ṣaḍbhiḥ | bāṣkalaḥ | yuyudhe | raṇe
gajavājisahasraughaiḥ | anekaiḥ | parivāritaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अयुतानाम्ayutānāmof ten-thousandsn. gen. pl. of अयुत; depends on शतैः
शतैःśataiḥwith hundredsn. instr. pl. of शत; instrumental of accompaniment
षड्भिःṣaḍbhiḥsixnumeral षष्, instr. pl.; qualifies शतैः
बाष्कलःbāṣkalaḥBāṣkalam. nom. sg., proper name; subject of युयुधे
युयुधेyuyudhefought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
रणेraṇein the battlem. loc. sg. of रण
गजवाजिसहस्रौघैःgajavājisahasraughaiḥby hosts of thousands of elephants and horsesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of परिवारितः
अनेकैःanekaiḥmanym. instr. pl. of अनेक; qualifies -ओघैः
परिवारितःparivāritaḥParivārita (‘the Surrounded’); lit. ‘surrounded’m. nom. sg. of परिवारित (परि + √वृ, causative वारय-, + kta); taken here as a proper name, subject of अयुध्यत (2.44); alternatively the participle ‘surrounded’ (see Morph)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शतैः षड्भिर्बाष्कलो युयुधे śataiḥ ṣaḍbhirbāṣkalo yuyudhe → शतैः + षड्भिः + बाष्कलः + युयुधे
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Visarga before ṣ may stay (शतैः षड्भिः); after i before the voiced b it appears as r (षड्भिर्बाष्कलो); -aḥ before y becomes -o (बाष्कलो युयुधे).
  • गजवाजिसहस्रौघैरनेकैः gajavājisahasraughairanekaiḥ → गजवाजिसहस्र + ओघैः + अनेकैः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (vṛddhi); visarga sandhi · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    a + o → au (सहस्रौघ); visarga after ai before a vowel appears as r.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • गजवाजिसहस्रौघैः gajavājisahasraughaiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa; inside it गजवाजिसहस्र is a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (गजवाजिनां सहस्राणि) and गजवाजि an itaretara dvandva (गजाः च वाजिनः च)
    Members: गजवाजिसहस्र + ओघ · Vigraha: गजवाजिसहस्राणाम् ओघाः, तैः gajavājisahasrāṇām oghāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “by floods of thousands of elephants and horses”; in the verse: with wave on wave of elephants and cavalry

C. Morphology

  • परिवारितः parivāritaḥ — Past participle of the causative of परि + √वृ: ‘made surrounded, encircled’. It can be read as a name (Parivārita), the subject of अयुध्यत in 2.44, which otherwise has none; or as the plain participle ‘surrounded’, describing Bāṣkala, with 2.44a then repeating his fighting. The name reading is followed here; in 2.45 the same word is plainly the participle.
  • षड्भिः ṣaḍbhiḥ — As in 2.41: six hundreds of ayutas = six million.

D. Anvaya — prose order

बाष्कलः अयुतानां षड्भिः शतैः रणे युयुधे। अनेकैः गजवाजिसहस्रौघैः परिवारितः (2.44: रथानां कोट्या च वृतः तस्मिन् युद्धे अयुध्यत)।

bāṣkalaḥ ayutānāṃ ṣaḍbhiḥ śataiḥ raṇe yuyudhe | anekaiḥ gajavājisahasraughaiḥ parivāritaḥ (2.44: rathānāṃ koṭyā ca vṛtaḥ tasmin yuddhe ayudhyata) |

The first line is complete. The second introduces परिवारितः, taken as the name of the next general, whose verb is अयुध्यत in 2.44.

6Translation

LiteralWith six hundreds of ten-thousands Bāṣkala fought in the battle. Parivārita, with many hosts of thousands of elephants and horses,

ReadableBāṣkala fought in the battle with six million. Parivārita, with many thousands of elephants and horses in their hosts,

परिवारितः is rendered as a name (see Morph and Flags); ‘six million’ renders six hundred ayutas.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningBāṣkala comes with six hundred ayutas — six million. He is struck down with a bhindipāla in 3.18, where the Gita Press prints the name as वाष्कल (b and v are interchangeable in many manuscripts).

Traditional interpretationTranslators divide the next lines differently. Taking परिवारितः as a name gives each verb of 2.43–45 its own general (Bāṣkala, Parivārita, Biḍāla); taking it as ‘surrounded’ leaves Bāṣkala, or Biḍāla, as the subject of two verbs.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: परिवारितः may be a proper name (Parivārita) or the participle ‘surrounded’; the name reading is followed because अयुध्यत in 2.44a has no other subject. No general of that name appears among those slain in chapter 3.

2.44Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

वृतो रथानां कोट्या च युद्धे तस्मिन्नयुध्यत।
बिडालाख्योऽयुतानां च पञ्चाशद्भिरथायुतैः ॥ ४४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

वृतः | रथानाम् | कोट्या | च | युद्धे | तस्मिन् | अयुध्यतबिडालाख्यः | अयुतानाम् | च | पञ्चाशद्भिः | अथ | अयुतैः

3IAST

As recitedvṛto rathānāṃ koṭyā ca yuddhe tasminnayudhyata |
biḍālākhyo'yutānāṃ ca pañcāśadbhirathāyutaiḥ ||

Separatedvṛtaḥ | rathānām | koṭyā | ca | yuddhe | tasmin | ayudhyata
biḍālākhyaḥ | ayutānām | ca | pañcāśadbhiḥ | atha | ayutaiḥ
