5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • वृतो रथानां vṛto rathānāṃ → वृतः + रथानाम्
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced r becomes -o.
  • तस्मिन्नयुध्यत tasminnayudhyata → तस्मिन् + अयुध्यत
    consonant sandhi (doubling of n) · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32)
    Final n after a short vowel is doubled before a vowel: तस्मिन्न-.
  • बिडालाख्योऽयुतानां biḍālākhyo'yutānāṃ → बिडालाख्यः + अयुतानाम्
    visarga sandhi (with pūrvarūpa) · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a- becomes -o, and the initial a is absorbed (avagraha).
  • पञ्चाशद्भिरथायुतैः pañcāśadbhirathāyutaiḥ → पञ्चाशद्भिः + अथ + अयुतैः
    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Visarga after i before a vowel appears as r; a + a → ā. The division पञ्चाशद्भिः + रथायुतैः is not possible: -iḥ before r would give पञ्चाशद्भी रथा- (रो रि, 8.3.14; ढ्रलोपे पूर्वस्य दीर्घोऽणः, 6.3.111).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • बिडालाख्यः biḍālākhyaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: बिडाल + आख्या · Vigraha: बिडालः आख्या यस्य सः biḍālaḥ ākhyā yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose name is Biḍāla (‘Cat’)”; in the verse: Biḍāla by name

C. Morphology

  • अयुतैः ayutaiḥ — The phrase अयुतानां च पञ्चाशद्भिः अथ अयुतैः names the ayuta twice. Taken with रथानाम् in 2.45 it is read as ‘with fifty ayutas (of chariots)’, the second अयुतैः resuming the first after the connective अथ; read strictly it could be ‘fifty ayutas of ayutas’, a hyperbole of the kind found in 2.46. The first reading is followed.
  • तस्मिन् tasmin — Locative singular of the pronoun तद् (-स्मिन्, the pronominal locative), agreeing with the neuter युद्धे.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(परिवारितः) रथानां कोट्या च वृतः तस्मिन् युद्धे अयुध्यत। बिडालाख्यः च अयुतानां पञ्चाशद्भिः अथ अयुतैः (रथानां परिवारितः, 2.45: युयुधे)।

(parivāritaḥ) rathānāṃ koṭyā ca vṛtaḥ tasmin yuddhe ayudhyata | biḍālākhyaḥ ca ayutānāṃ pañcāśadbhiḥ atha ayutaiḥ (rathānāṃ parivāritaḥ, 2.45: yuyudhe) |

The first line completes 2.43b (subject परिवारितः). The second introduces Biḍāla; his verb and the genitive रथानाम् stand in 2.45.

6Translation

Literaland surrounded by a crore of chariots, fought in that battle. And the one named Biḍāla, with fifty of ten-thousands, and with ten-thousands —

Readableand a crore of chariots about him, fought in that battle. Biḍāla, with fifty ayutas

The doubled ‘ayuta’ is rendered once (see Morph); ‘a crore’ is ten million. The sentence continues into 2.45.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe general of 2.43b fights encircled by elephants, horses and a crore of chariots; then Biḍāla (‘Cat’) is named, with fifty ayutas — half a million — of chariots.

Traditional interpretationBiḍāla is beheaded by the Goddess’s sword in 3.20 (बिडालस्यासिना कायात्पातयामास वै शिरः).

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: अयुतानां … पञ्चाशद्भिः अथ अयुतैः repeats अयुत; the sense ‘fifty ayutas’ is adopted, but ‘fifty ayutas of ayutas’ is grammatically possible. The sandhi rules out a split into पञ्चाशद्भिः रथायुतैः.

2.45Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

युयुधे संयुगे तत्र रथानां परिवारितः।
अन्ये च तत्रायुतशो रथनागहयैर्वृताः ॥ ४५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

युयुधे | संयुगे | तत्र | रथानाम् | परिवारितःअन्ये | च | तत्र | अयुतशः | रथनागहयैः | वृताः

3IAST

As recitedyuyudhe saṃyuge tatra rathānāṃ parivāritaḥ |
anye ca tatrāyutaśo rathanāgahayairvṛtāḥ ||

Separatedyuyudhe | saṃyuge | tatra | rathānām | parivāritaḥ
anye | ca | tatra | ayutaśaḥ | rathanāgahayaiḥ | vṛtāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
युयुधेyuyudhefought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada; subject बिडालाख्यः (2.44)
संयुगेsaṃyugein the battlen. loc. sg. of संयुग (सम् + √युज्, ‘a coming together’, i.e. combat)
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
रथानाम्rathānāmof chariotsm. gen. pl. of रथ; depends on the numbers of 2.44b
परिवारितःparivāritaḥsurroundedpast participle of परि + √वृ (causative वारय-, + kta), m. nom. sg.; qualifies बिडालाख्यः
अन्येanyeotherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. pl.; subject of युयुधुः (2.46)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
अयुतशःayutaśaḥby tens of thousandsindeclinable (distributive adverb, अयुत + taddhita -शस्)
रथनागहयैःrathanāgahayaiḥby chariots, elephants and horsesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of वृताः
वृताःvṛtāḥsurroundedpast participle (kta) of √वृ, m. nom. pl.; qualifies अन्ये

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • रथानां परिवारितः rathānāṃ parivāritaḥ → रथानाम् + परिवारितः
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • तत्रायुतशो रथ tatrāyutaśo ratha → तत्र + अयुतशः + रथ-
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + a → ā; -aḥ before the voiced r becomes -o.
  • रथनागहयैर्वृताः rathanāgahayairvṛtāḥ → रथनागहयैः + वृताः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after ai before a voiced consonant appears as r.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • रथनागहयैः rathanāgahayaiḥ — dvandva (itaretara)
    Members: रथ + नाग + हय · Vigraha: रथाः च नागाः च हयाः च, तैः rathāḥ ca nāgāḥ ca hayāḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally “by chariots and elephants and horses”; in the verse: by chariots, elephants and cavalry

C. Morphology

  • अयुतशः ayutaśaḥ — The taddhita -शस् makes distributive adverbs from numbers: अयुतशः ‘by ten-thousands, in tens of thousands’ (compare शतशः ‘by hundreds’, 2.56).
  • परिवारितः parivāritaḥ — Here clearly the participle ‘surrounded’, construed with the genitive रथानाम् and the instrumentals of 2.44b. Compare its use in 2.43.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(बिडालाख्यः, अयुतानां पञ्चाशद्भिः अयुतैः) रथानां परिवारितः तत्र संयुगे युयुधे। अन्ये च तत्र अयुतशः रथनागहयैः वृताः (2.46: महासुराः युयुधुः)।

(biḍālākhyaḥ, ayutānāṃ pañcāśadbhiḥ ayutaiḥ) rathānāṃ parivāritaḥ tatra saṃyuge yuyudhe | anye ca tatra ayutaśaḥ rathanāgahayaiḥ vṛtāḥ (2.46: mahāsurāḥ yuyudhuḥ) |

The first line finishes Biḍāla’s sentence. The second introduces ‘others’, whose verb युयुधुः and noun महासुराः stand in 2.46.

6Translation

Literalof chariots, surrounded, fought there in the battle. And others there, surrounded by chariots, elephants and horses by tens of thousands,

Readableof chariots around him, fought in that battle. And other great asuras, each with chariots, elephants and horses by the ten thousand,

‘Great asuras’ is brought forward from 2.46, where the subject is named.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningBiḍāla’s entry is completed, and the roll turns to the many unnamed generals, each with his tens of thousands of chariots, elephants and horses.

Traditional interpretationThe muster-roll is longer in the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa as preserved in GRETIL, which carries verses here that the Gita Press text does not; the recited Saptaśatī of seven hundred mantras is followed in this edition, and nothing is added to it.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: Recension note (corrections.json): GRETIL’s Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa has additional verses at this point (in 2.45) not found in the Gita Press text; they are not part of the 700 and are not analysed.

2.46Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

युयुधुः संयुगे देव्या सह तत्र महासुराः।
कोटिकोटिसहस्रैस्तु रथानां दन्तिनां तथा ॥ ४६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

युयुधुः | संयुगे | देव्या | सह | तत्र | महासुराःकोटिकोटिसहस्रैः | तु | रथानाम् | दन्तिनाम् | तथा

3IAST

As recitedyuyudhuḥ saṃyuge devyā saha tatra mahāsurāḥ |
koṭikoṭisahasraistu rathānāṃ dantināṃ tathā ||

Separatedyuyudhuḥ | saṃyuge | devyā | saha | tatra | mahāsurāḥ
koṭikoṭisahasraiḥ | tu | rathānām | dantinām | tathā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
युयुधुःyuyudhuḥfought√युध्, liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada (an epic parasmaipada form of an ātmanepada root; classical युयुधिरे)
संयुगेsaṃyugein the battlen. loc. sg. of संयुग
देव्याdevyāthe Goddessf. instr. sg. of देवी; governed by सह
सहsahawith, againstindeclinable (postposition with the instrumental)
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
महासुराःmahāsurāḥgreat asurasm. nom. pl.; compound (see 2.40); subject of युयुधुः, resuming अन्ये (2.45)
कोटिकोटिसहस्रैःkoṭikoṭisahasraiḥby thousands of crores of croresn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of वृतः (2.47)
तुtuand (now)indeclinable (particle marking a turn to a new subject)
रथानाम्rathānāmof chariotsm. gen. pl. of रथ; depends on कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः
दन्तिनाम्dantināmof tuskers, of elephantsm. gen. pl. of दन्तिन् (दन्त + possessive -इन्, ‘having tusks’)
तथाtathāand alsoindeclinable (connective)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • युयुधुः संयुगे yuyudhuḥ saṃyuge → युयुधुः + संयुगे
    visarga sandhi (optional retention) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34) is optional before a sibilant
    Before s the visarga may stay or become s; the Gita Press keeps the visarga.
  • कोटिकोटिसहस्रैस्तु koṭikoṭisahasraistu → कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः + तु
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • रथानां दन्तिनां rathānāṃ dantināṃ → रथानाम् + दन्तिनाम्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः koṭikoṭisahasraiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (कोटिकोटि also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, कोटीनां कोटयः)
    Members: कोटिकोटि + सहस्र · Vigraha: कोटिकोटीनां सहस्राणि, तैः koṭikoṭīnāṃ sahasrāṇi, taiḥ
    Literally “by thousands of crores of crores”; in the verse: by numbers beyond counting

C. Morphology

  • युयुधुः yuyudhuḥ — √युध् is ātmanepada (युध्यते, युयुधे in 2.41/2.43/2.45). The 3rd plural perfect युयुधुः has the parasmaipada ending -उः: an epic form (ārṣa) for classical युयुधिरे. The text uses it regularly (2.48, 2.53, 2.63).
  • दन्तिनाम् dantinām — Genitive plural of an in-stem: दन्तिन् + आम् → दन्तिनाम्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(अन्ये च) महासुराः तत्र संयुगे देव्या सह युयुधुः। (महिषासुरः) तु रथानां दन्तिनां तथा (हयानां च) कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः (2.47: वृतः)।

(anye ca) mahāsurāḥ tatra saṃyuge devyā saha yuyudhuḥ | (mahiṣāsuraḥ) tu rathānāṃ dantināṃ tathā (hayānāṃ ca) koṭikoṭisahasraiḥ (2.47: vṛtaḥ) |

The first line closes the sentence of 2.45b. With तु the second turns to Mahiṣāsura himself; the instrumental and genitives belong with वृतः in 2.47.

6Translation

Literalgreat asuras fought there in the battle with the Goddess. And, by thousands of crores of crores of chariots and likewise of elephants

Readablefought there in battle against the Goddess. And with thousands upon thousands of crores of chariots and elephants

The second line runs into 2.47, where Mahiṣāsura is named.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningAll the generals together fight the Goddess. Then तु turns to the king himself: Mahiṣāsura’s own forces are counted in ‘thousands of crores of crores’ — a number meant to be beyond counting.

Traditional interpretationThe army grows from tens of thousands to crores of crores while the Goddess remains one. The disproportion is the point of the roll, and it prepares 2.49–50, where she cuts down their weapons ‘as if in play’ (लीलयैव).

2.47Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

हयानां च वृतो युद्धे तत्राभून्महिषासुरः।
तोमरैर्भिन्दिपालैश्च शक्तिभिर्मुसलैस्तथा ॥ ४७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

हयानाम् | च | वृतः | युद्धे | तत्र | अभूत् | महिषासुरःतोमरैः | भिन्दिपालैः | च | शक्तिभिः | मुसलैः | तथा

3IAST

As recitedhayānāṃ ca vṛto yuddhe tatrābhūnmahiṣāsuraḥ |
tomarairbhindipālaiśca śaktibhirmusalaistathā ||

Separatedhayānām | ca | vṛtaḥ | yuddhe | tatra | abhūt | mahiṣāsuraḥ
tomaraiḥ | bhindipālaiḥ | ca | śaktibhiḥ | musalaiḥ | tathā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
हयानाम्hayānāmof horsesm. gen. pl. of हय; depends on कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः (2.46)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
वृतःvṛtaḥsurroundedpast participle (kta) of √वृ, m. nom. sg.; qualifies महिषासुरः
युद्धेyuddhein the battlen. loc. sg. of युद्ध
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
अभूत्abhūtwas, stood√भू, luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
महिषासुरःmahiṣāsuraḥMahiṣāsuram. nom. sg.; compound (see 2.36); subject of अभूत्
तोमरैःtomaraiḥwith javelins (tomaras)m. instr. pl. of तोमर (a lance or javelin); instrument of युयुधुः (2.48)
भिन्दिपालैःbhindipālaiḥwith bhindipālas (short throwing-spears)m. instr. pl. of भिन्दिपाल; instrument
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
शक्तिभिःśaktibhiḥwith spears (śaktis)f. instr. pl. of शक्ति (here the weapon, a spear or dart); instrument
मुसलैःmusalaiḥwith clubs (musalas)m./n. instr. pl. of मुसल (‘pestle’, a heavy club); instrument
तथाtathāand likewiseindeclinable (connective)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • वृतो युद्धे vṛto yuddhe → वृतः + युद्धे
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced y becomes -o.
  • तत्राभून्महिषासुरः tatrābhūnmahiṣāsuraḥ → तत्र + अभूत् + महिषासुरः
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); consonant sandhi (nasalisation) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    a + a → ā; final t before the nasal m becomes n (optional rule, taken here).
  • तोमरैर्भिन्दिपालैश्च tomarairbhindipālaiśca → तोमरैः + भिन्दिपालैः + च
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga after ai before a voiced consonant appears as r; before c it becomes ś.
  • शक्तिभिर्मुसलैस्तथा śaktibhirmusalaistathā → शक्तिभिः + मुसलैः + तथा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga after i before m appears as r; before t it becomes s.

C. Morphology

  • अभूत् abhūt — Root aorist of √भू, ‘was’; with वृतः and the locative it means ‘stood (in the battle), surrounded’. As in 1.4, the aorist alternates with the perfect in narrative.
  • शक्तिभिः śaktibhiḥ — शक्ति here is a weapon, the spear or dart, as in the gifts of 2.21 (शक्तिं ददौ … हुताशनः); not the abstract ‘power’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(रथानां दन्तिनां तथा) हयानां च (कोटिकोटिसहस्रैः) वृतः महिषासुरः तत्र युद्धे अभूत्। तोमरैः भिन्दिपालैः च शक्तिभिः तथा मुसलैः (2.48: … महासुराः देव्या युयुधुः)।

(rathānāṃ dantināṃ tathā) hayānāṃ ca (koṭikoṭisahasraiḥ) vṛtaḥ mahiṣāsuraḥ tatra yuddhe abhūt | tomaraiḥ bhindipālaiḥ ca śaktibhiḥ tathā musalaiḥ (2.48: … mahāsurāḥ devyā yuyudhuḥ) |

The first line completes the sentence of 2.46b. The second line is a string of instrumentals for युयुधुः in 2.48.

6Translation

Literaland of horses, surrounded, Mahiṣāsura was there in the battle. With javelins and bhindipālas, with spears and likewise with clubs —

Readableand horses around him, Mahiṣāsura himself stood in the battle. With javelins and throwing-spears, with lances and clubs

The second line is completed in 2.48 (‘they fought with the Goddess …’).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMahiṣāsura himself is in the field, at the centre of an army counted in crores of crores. The weapon list that follows (javelins, bhindipālas, spears, clubs) begins the asuras’ assault, completed in 2.48.

Traditional interpretationThe bhindipāla, named here among the asuras’ weapons, is also carried by the Goddess’s own attendant hosts (भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः, 2.53) and is the weapon with which she strikes down Bāṣkala (3.18).

2.48Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

युयुधुः संयुगे देव्या खड्गैः परशुपट्टिशैः।
केचिच्च चिक्षिपुः शक्तीः केचित्पाशांस्तथापरे ॥ ४८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

युयुधुः | संयुगे | देव्या | खड्गैः | परशुपट्टिशैःकेचित् | च | चिक्षिपुः | शक्तीः | केचित् | पाशान् | तथा | अपरे

3IAST

As recitedyuyudhuḥ saṃyuge devyā khaḍgaiḥ paraśupaṭṭiśaiḥ |
kecicca cikṣipuḥ śaktīḥ kecitpāśāṃstathāpare ||

Separatedyuyudhuḥ | saṃyuge | devyā | khaḍgaiḥ | paraśupaṭṭiśaiḥ
kecit | ca | cikṣipuḥ | śaktīḥ | kecit | pāśān | tathā | apare

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
युयुधुःyuyudhuḥthey fought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to fight’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, with a parasmaipada ending (see Morphology); subject: the great asuras of 2.46–47
संयुगेsaṃyugein the battlen. loc. sg. of संयुग ‘conflict, battle’
देव्याdevyāwith the Devīf. instr. sg. of देवी; instrumental of the opponent with a verb of fighting (‘fought with’), सह being understood from 2.46
खड्गैःkhaḍgaiḥwith swordsm. instr. pl. of खड्ग; instrument
परशुपट्टिशैःparaśupaṭṭiśaiḥwith axes and paṭṭiśas (edged spears)m. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument
केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun (किम् + चित्), m. nom. pl. के + चित्; subject of चिक्षिपुः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
चिक्षिपुःcikṣipuḥhurled√क्षिप् (tudādi, 6U ‘to throw’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
शक्तीःśaktīḥspears (śakti, the throwing spear)f. acc. pl. of शक्ति (i-stem); object of चिक्षिपुः
केचित्kecitsome (others)indefinite pronoun, m. nom. pl.; subject, चिक्षिपुः understood
पाशान्pāśānnoosesm. acc. pl. of पाश; object of the understood चिक्षिपुः
तथाtathālikewise, and so tooindeclinable (adverb of manner)
अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject of the clause that runs on into 2.49

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • केचिच्च kecicca → केचित् + च
    consonant sandhi (palatal assimilation) · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Final t before the palatal c becomes the palatal c: केचित् च → केचिच्च.
  • केचित्पाशांस्तथापरे kecitpāśāṃstathāpare → केचित् + पाशान् + तथा + अपरे
    consonant and vowel sandhi · खरि च (8.4.55); नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    The t of केचित् stays unvoiced before p; final n before t becomes ṃs (पाशान् तथा → पाशांस्तथा); ā + a → ā (तथा अपरे → तथापरे).
  • युयुधुः संयुगे yuyudhuḥ saṃyuge → युयुधुः + संयुगे
    visarga before a sibilant · visarga kept before s (the optional alternative to विसर्जनीयस्य सः, 8.3.34)
    Before an initial sibilant the visarga may stand or be assimilated (युयुधुस्संयुगे); the Gita Press keeps it.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • परशुपट्टिशैः paraśupaṭṭiśaiḥ — itaretara dvandva
    Members: परशु + पट्टिश · Vigraha: परशवः च पट्टिशाः च, तैः paraśavaḥ ca paṭṭiśāḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally “with axes and paṭṭiśas”; in the verse: two kinds of edged weapon: the battle-axe and the paṭṭiśa, a spear or long blade with a sharp edge

C. Morphology

  • युयुधुः yuyudhuḥ — √युध् is ātmanepadin (युध्यते; perfect युयुधे, as in 2.41 and 2.43). The plural here takes the parasmaipada ending -उः instead of the classical युयुधिरे — the usual epic and Purāṇic form, used again in 2.53 and 2.63.
  • चिक्षिपुः cikṣipuḥ — Perfect with reduplication: क्षि → चि (a guttural in the reduplicated syllable becomes the corresponding palatal), then the weak stem क्षिप् with -उः.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(ते महासुराः तोमरैः भिन्दिपालैः च शक्तिभिः मुसलैः तथा) खड्गैः परशुपट्टिशैः (च) संयुगे देव्या (सह) युयुधुः। केचित् च शक्तीः चिक्षिपुः, केचित् पाशान् (चिक्षिपुः); तथा अपरे —

(te mahāsurāḥ tomaraiḥ bhindipālaiḥ ca śaktibhiḥ musalaiḥ tathā) khaḍgaiḥ paraśupaṭṭiśaiḥ (ca) saṃyuge devyā (saha) yuyudhuḥ | kecit ca śaktīḥ cikṣipuḥ, kecit pāśān (cikṣipuḥ); tathā apare —

The first line completes the sentence begun in 2.47b (तोमरैः … मुसलैः तथा): the list of instruments runs across the verse boundary. The second line distributes the action: ‘some … some … and others’; अपरे is taken as the subject of 2.49.

6Translation

Literal(With lances, javelins, spears and clubs,) with swords, with axes and paṭṭiśas they fought with the Devī in the battle. And some hurled spears, some nooses; and likewise others —

ReadableWith lances and javelins, with spears and maces, with swords, axes and halberds, they fought the Goddess in that battle. Some hurled spears, others nooses; and still others —

The opening phrase is carried over from 2.47b. ‘Halberds’ renders पट्टिश loosely. The verse ends on अपरे, whose action follows in 2.49.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe asura host, mustered in 2.40–47, attacks all at once. The verse names a whole armoury — swords, axes, paṭṭiśas, spears, nooses — to show the full weight of the assault the Devī is about to cut down.

Traditional interpretationThe weapons the asuras use are largely those the gods have just given the Devī (2.20–31: spear, noose, axe, sword). The battle is fought weapon against weapon, and the text will show hers prevailing.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: अपरे can also be read within this verse as an elliptical third member (‘and others likewise [hurled other weapons]’). Here it is taken with 2.49, on the pattern of तथापरे in 2.57–58, where the clause clearly runs on.

2.49Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

देवीं खड्गप्रहारैस्तु ते तां हन्तुं प्रचक्रमुः।
सापि देवी ततस्तानि शस्त्राण्यस्त्राणि चण्डिका ॥ ४९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देवीम् | खड्गप्रहारैः | तु | ते | ताम् | हन्तुम् | प्रचक्रमुःसा | अपि | देवी | ततः | तानि | शस्त्राणि | अस्त्राणि | चण्डिका

3IAST

As reciteddevīṃ khaḍgaprahāraistu te tāṃ hantuṃ pracakramuḥ |
sāpi devī tatastāni śastrāṇyastrāṇi caṇḍikā ||

Separateddevīm | khaḍgaprahāraiḥ | tu | te | tām | hantum | pracakramuḥ
sā | api | devī | tataḥ | tāni | śastrāṇi | astrāṇi | caṇḍikā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
देवीम्devīmthe Devīf. acc. sg. of देवी; object of हन्तुम्
खड्गप्रहारैःkhaḍgaprahāraiḥwith sword-strokesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument
तुtubut, andindeclinable (particle of contrast or continuation)
तेtetheypronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; resumes अपरे of 2.48; subject of प्रचक्रमुः
ताम्tāmherpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; in apposition to देवीम्
हन्तुम्hantumto killinfinitive (tumun) of √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’)
प्रचक्रमुःpracakramuḥset about, beganप्र + √क्रम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to step’; with प्र ‘to begin’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject of प्रचिच्छेद (2.50)
अपिapi(she) for her part, in turnindeclinable (particle: ‘also, in turn’)
देवीdevīthe Devīf. nom. sg. of देवी; in apposition to सा
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb, तद् + tasil)
तानिtānithosepronoun तद्, n. acc. pl.; qualifies शस्त्राणि and अस्त्राणि
शस्त्राणिśastrāṇiweapons (held in the hand)n. acc. pl. of शस्त्र; object of प्रचिच्छेद (2.50)
अस्त्राणिastrāṇimissiles (weapons thrown or shot)n. acc. pl. of अस्त्र; object of प्रचिच्छेद (2.50)
चण्डिकाcaṇḍikāCaṇḍikā, the fierce onef. nom. sg. of चण्डिका (चण्डी + क); in apposition to सा देवी

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • खड्गप्रहारैस्तु khaḍgaprahāraistu → खड्गप्रहारैः + तु
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s. So too ततः तानि → ततस्तानि.
  • सापि sāpi → सा + अपि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • शस्त्राण्यस्त्राणि śastrāṇyastrāṇi → शस्त्राणि + अस्त्राणि
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final i before a dissimilar vowel becomes y: -णि अ- → -ण्य-.
  • देवीं खड्ग devīṃ khaḍga → देवीम् + खड्गप्रहारैः
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra; likewise तां हन्तुं प्रचक्रमुः.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • खड्गप्रहारैः khaḍgaprahāraiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: खड्ग + प्रहार · Vigraha: खड्गस्य प्रहाराः, तैः khaḍgasya prahārāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “with blows of the sword”; in the verse: with sword-strokes
    Alternative: Also a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, खड्गेन प्रहाराः ‘blows (dealt) with the sword’; the sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • प्रचक्रमुः pracakramuḥ — Perfect of प्र + √क्रम् (reduplication च-क्रम्, क → च). With an infinitive it means ‘set about (doing)’. Classical grammar puts प्र + √क्रम् ‘to begin’ in the ātmanepada (प्रचक्रमिरे); the parasmaipada is the common epic usage.
  • हन्तुम् hantum — Infinitive in -तुम् (tumun) of √हन्, expressing purpose with a verb of beginning: ‘began to kill’.
  • चण्डिका caṇḍikā — चण्डी ‘the fierce (woman)’ with the suffix -क: Caṇḍikā, the name by which the Devī is most often called in the middle and last caritas.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(अपरे) ते तु खड्गप्रहारैः तां देवीं हन्तुं प्रचक्रमुः। ततः सा देवी चण्डिका अपि तानि शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि (च) —

(apare) te tu khaḍgaprahāraiḥ tāṃ devīṃ hantuṃ pracakramuḥ | tataḥ sā devī caṇḍikā api tāni śastrāṇi astrāṇi (ca) —

Two sentences. The first ends the asuras’ assault. The second has its subject (सा देवी चण्डिका) and objects (तानि शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि) here and its verb (प्रचिच्छेद) in 2.50.

6Translation

LiteralAnd they set about to kill her, the Devī, with sword-strokes. She too, the Devī Caṇḍikā, then — those weapons and missiles —

Readableset out to cut the Goddess down with blows of the sword. Then the Goddess Caṇḍikā, in her turn, took those weapons and missiles —

The second line has no verb of its own; ‘took’ in the readable version only bridges to ‘cut to pieces’ in 2.50, where the verb stands.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe attack reaches its height: the asuras close in with swords to kill her. The second line turns at once to the Devī’s answer, which 2.50 completes: she cuts every weapon down.

Traditional interpretationThe pair शस्त्र and अस्त्र is the standard division of weapons: śastra is held and wielded, astra is released — thrown, shot or discharged. Naming both says that no kind of weapon reached her.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also names her Caṇḍikā (name 755, चण्डिका). In the Devī Māhātmya the name belongs above all to the warrior Goddess of these battle chapters.

2.50Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

लीलयैव प्रचिच्छेद निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी।
अनायस्तानना देवी स्तूयमाना सुरर्षिभिः ॥ ५० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

लीलया | एव | प्रचिच्छेद | निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणीअनायस्तानना | देवी | स्तूयमाना | सुरर्षिभिः

3IAST

As recitedlīlayaiva praciccheda nijaśastrāstravarṣiṇī |
anāyastānanā devī stūyamānā surarṣibhiḥ ||

Separatedlīlayā | eva | praciccheda | nijaśastrāstravarṣiṇī
anāyastānanā | devī | stūyamānā | surarṣibhiḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
लीलयाlīlayāin play, effortlesslyf. instr. sg. of लीला ‘play, sport’; instrumental of manner
एवevamerely, justindeclinable (emphatic particle)
प्रचिच्छेदpracicchedacut to piecesप्र + √छिद् (rudhādi, 7U ‘to cut’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; its objects are तानि शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि (2.49)
निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणीnijaśastrāstravarṣiṇīraining her own weapons and missilesf. nom. sg. (stem -वर्षिन् + ङीप्); compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सा देवी (2.49)
अनायस्ताननाanāyastānanāher face unweariedf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवी
देवीdevīthe Devīf. nom. sg. of देवी; subject of मुमोच (2.51)
स्तूयमानाstūyamānābeing praisedpresent passive participle (śānac with yak) of √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies देवी
सुरर्षिभिःsurarṣibhiḥby the gods and seersm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of स्तूयमाना

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • लीलयैव līlayaiva → लीलया + एव
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai.
  • प्रचिच्छेद praciccheda → प्र + चि + छेद
    consonant sandhi inside the word (tuk before ch) · छे च (6.1.73)
    After the short i of the reduplicated syllable, ch is doubled as cch (चि + छेद → चिच्छेद).
  • अनायस्तानना anāyastānanā → अनायस्त + आनना
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā. The same rule joins शस्त्र + अस्त्र in निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी.
  • सुरर्षिभिः surarṣibhiḥ → सुर + ऋषिभिः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ṛ → ar.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी nijaśastrāstravarṣiṇī — upapada tatpuruṣa (the second member a kṛt form in -इन्, णिनि, ‘habitually doing’); its first member: शस्त्रास्त्र, itaretara dvandva (शस्त्राणि च अस्त्राणि च), qualified by निज (karmadhāraya)
    Members: निजशस्त्रास्त्र + वर्षिणी · Vigraha: निजानि शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि च वर्षति इति (तच्छीला) nijāni śastrāṇi astrāṇi ca varṣati iti (tacchīlā)
    Literally “she who rains her own weapons and missiles”; in the verse: pouring out her own arms against theirs as she cuts theirs down
  • अनायस्तानना anāyastānanā — bahuvrīhi (its first member अनायस्त is a nañ tatpuruṣa: न आयस्तम्)
    Members: अनायस्त + आनन · Vigraha: अनायस्तम् आननं यस्याः सा anāyastam ānanaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose face is not strained”; in the verse: showing no sign of effort
  • सुरर्षिभिः surarṣibhiḥ — itaretara dvandva
    Members: सुर + ऋषि · Vigraha: सुराः च ऋषयः च, तैः surāḥ ca ṛṣayaḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally “by gods and seers”; in the verse: the gods who hailed her (2.34) and the sages who praised her (2.35)
    Alternative: Also read as a karmadhāraya, सुराः च ते ऋषयः च ‘divine seers’ (like देवर्षि). The dvandva fits 2.34–35, where gods and sages praise her separately.

C. Morphology

  • प्रचिच्छेद praciccheda — Perfect of √छिद्: the reduplicated syllable छि becomes चि (an aspirate is reduplicated unaspirated), and the root vowel takes guṇa (छेद) in the singular.
  • स्तूयमाना stūyamānā — Passive present stem स्तूय- (√स्तु + yak, with the root vowel lengthened before -य-) and the participle ending -मान: ‘being praised’, with the praisers in the instrumental.
  • निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी nijaśastrāstravarṣiṇī — The agent suffix -इन् (णिनि) on √वृष् ‘to rain’ (guṇa वर्ष-) gives वर्षिन् ‘raining (by habit)’; the feminine adds -ई: वर्षिणी (n → ṇ after r).

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सा देवी चण्डिका तानि शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि) निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी लीलया एव प्रचिच्छेद। अनायस्तानना सुरर्षिभिः स्तूयमाना देवी —

(sā devī caṇḍikā tāni śastrāṇi astrāṇi) nijaśastrāstravarṣiṇī līlayā eva praciccheda | anāyastānanā surarṣibhiḥ stūyamānā devī —

The first line completes the sentence of 2.49b with its verb प्रचिच्छेद. The second line opens a new sentence whose verb, मुमोच, stands in 2.51.

6Translation

LiteralRaining her own weapons and missiles, she cut (them) to pieces merely in play. The Devī, her face unwearied, being praised by the gods and seers —

Readableand, raining down her own weapons and missiles, cut them to pieces as if in play. Unwearied of face, praised by gods and seers, the Goddess —

The verse divides between two sentences; the dash marks the subject of 2.51.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe point is effortlessness: लीलया एव ‘merely in play’ and अनायस्तानना ‘her face unstrained’. The whole armoury of 2.48 is undone without the Devī showing effort, while gods and seers look on and praise her.

Traditional interpretationThe same phrase returns in the third carita: Caṇḍikā cuts Śumbha’s weapons चिच्छेद लीलया (8.31). The tradition hears in लीला the Devī’s freedom: what she does in the world is play, not labour or need.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has a close image in its own battle with Bhaṇḍa: name 79, भण्डासुरेन्द्रनिर्मुक्तशस्त्रप्रत्यस्त्रवर्षिणी, ‘she who rains counter-missiles against the weapons released by Bhaṇḍāsura’. Both texts use the same agent noun -वर्षिणी for the warrior Goddess; the battles themselves are different stories.

2.51Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

मुमोचासुरदेहेषु शस्त्राण्यस्त्राणि चेश्वरी।
सोऽपि क्रुद्धो धुतसटो देव्या वाहनकेसरी ॥ ५१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मुमोच | असुरदेहेषु | शस्त्राणि | अस्त्राणि | च | ईश्वरीसः | अपि | क्रुद्धः | धुतसटः | देव्याः | वाहनकेसरी

3IAST

As recitedmumocāsuradeheṣu śastrāṇyastrāṇi ceśvarī |
so'pi kruddho dhutasaṭo devyā vāhanakesarī ||

Separatedmumoca | asuradeheṣu | śastrāṇi | astrāṇi | ca | īśvarī
saḥ | api | kruddhaḥ | dhutasaṭaḥ | devyāḥ | vāhanakesarī

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
मुमोचmumocareleased, let fly√मुच् (tudādi, 6U ‘to release’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
असुरदेहेषुasuradeheṣuon the bodies of the asurasm. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); locative of the target
शस्त्राणिśastrāṇiweaponsn. acc. pl. of शस्त्र; object of मुमोच
अस्त्राणिastrāṇimissilesn. acc. pl. of अस्त्र; object of मुमोच
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); joins शस्त्राणि and अस्त्राणि
ईश्वरीīśvarīthe Sovereign (Goddess)f. nom. sg. of ईश्वरी (fem. of ईश्वर ‘lord’); in apposition to देवी (2.50)
सःsaḥhe, that (lion)pronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; subject of चचार (2.52)
अपिapitoo, for his partindeclinable (particle)
क्रुद्धःkruddhaḥenragedpast participle (kta) of √क्रुध् (divādi, 4P ‘to be angry’), m. nom. sg.; active sense
धुतसटःdhutasaṭaḥwith mane shakingm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सः
देव्याःdevyāḥof the Devīf. gen. sg. of देवी; depends on वाहनकेसरी
वाहनकेसरीvāhanakesarīthe lion that was her mountm. nom. sg. (stem केसरिन्); compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to सः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मुमोचासुरदेहेषु mumocāsuradeheṣu → मुमोच + असुरदेहेषु
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • चेश्वरी ceśvarī → च + ईश्वरी
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e.
  • सोऽपि so'pi → सः + अपि
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    Before a vowel सः is treated like any -aḥ: before a it becomes so, and the a of अपि is elided (avagraha).
  • क्रुद्धो धुतसटो देव्या वाहन kruddho dhutasaṭo devyā vāhana → क्रुद्धः + धुतसटः + देव्याः + वाहनकेसरी
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o (twice). -āḥ before the voiced v loses its visarga (देव्याः → देव्या), which is why the genitive looks like the instrumental.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरदेहेषु asuradeheṣu — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: असुर + देह · Vigraha: असुराणां देहाः, तेषु asurāṇāṃ dehāḥ, teṣu
    Literally “on the bodies of the asuras”; in the verse: her weapons now strike the enemy themselves, not only their weapons
  • धुतसटः dhutasaṭaḥ — bahuvrīhi (the feminine सटा ‘mane’ is shortened to -सट as the last member of a bahuvrīhi qualifying a masculine)
    Members: धुत + सटा · Vigraha: धुताः सटाः यस्य सः dhutāḥ saṭāḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose mane-hairs are shaken”; in the verse: tossing his mane in fury
  • वाहनकेसरी vāhanakesarī — karmadhāraya
    Members: वाहन + केसरिन् · Vigraha: वाहनं च असौ केसरी च vāhanaṃ ca asau kesarī ca
    Literally “the mount-lion”; in the verse: the lion given by Himavat as her vehicle (2.29)

C. Morphology

  • मुमोच mumoca — Perfect of √मुच्: reduplication मु-, guṇa of the root vowel in the singular (मोच). Its ātmanepada counterpart मुमुचे follows in 2.52.
  • देव्याः devyāḥ — Genitive singular of देवी. The verse prints देव्या because -āḥ drops its visarga before a voiced sound; the sense (‘the Devī’s mount’) shows it is the genitive, not the instrumental देव्या.
  • क्रुद्धः kruddhaḥ — kta of √क्रुध्: dh + t → ddh (क्रुध् + त → क्रुद्ध). Intransitive, so active in sense: ‘angered’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(अनायस्तानना सुरर्षिभिः स्तूयमाना देवी) ईश्वरी असुरदेहेषु शस्त्राणि अस्त्राणि च मुमोच। देव्याः वाहनकेसरी सः अपि क्रुद्धः धुतसटः —

(anāyastānanā surarṣibhiḥ stūyamānā devī) īśvarī asuradeheṣu śastrāṇi astrāṇi ca mumoca | devyāḥ vāhanakesarī saḥ api kruddhaḥ dhutasaṭaḥ —

The first line completes the sentence opened in 2.50b. The second begins a new one: subject (सः … वाहनकेसरी) with two qualifiers; its verb, चचार, is in 2.52.

6Translation

Literalthe Sovereign released weapons and missiles on the bodies of the asuras. He too, enraged, his mane shaken, the lion that was the Devī’s mount —

Readableloosed her weapons and missiles upon the asuras’ bodies, sovereign that she is. And her lion too, the Goddess’s own mount, raging and tossing his mane —

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningDefence turns to attack: having destroyed the asuras’ weapons, the Devī now looses her own on their bodies. The lion given her by Himavat (2.29) enters the fight in his own right.

Traditional interpretationईश्वरी, ‘sovereign’, is chosen at the moment she goes on the offensive: the battle is the exercise of her lordship, not a contest between equals.

2.52Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

चचारासुरसैन्येषु वनेष्विव हुताशनः।
निःश्वासान् मुमुचे यांश्च युध्यमाना रणेऽम्बिका ॥ ५२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

चचार | असुरसैन्येषु | वनेषु | इव | हुताशनःनिःश्वासान् | मुमुचे | यान् | च | युध्यमाना | रणे | अम्बिका

3IAST

As recitedcacārāsurasainyeṣu vaneṣviva hutāśanaḥ |
niḥśvāsān mumuce yāṃśca yudhyamānā raṇe'mbikā ||

Separatedcacāra | asurasainyeṣu | vaneṣu | iva | hutāśanaḥ
niḥśvāsān | mumuce | yān | ca | yudhyamānā | raṇe | ambikā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
चचारcacāraranged, moved through√चर् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to move’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; subject: the lion (2.51)
असुरसैन्येषुasurasainyeṣuamong the asura armiesn. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
वनेषुvaneṣuin forestsn. loc. pl. of वन
इवivalike, asindeclinable (particle of comparison)
हुताशनःhutāśanaḥfire (lit. ‘the eater of oblations’)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); the standard of comparison
निःश्वासान्niḥśvāsānbreaths, exhalationsm. acc. pl. of निःश्वास (निस् + √श्वस् + घञ्); object of मुमुचे
मुमुचेmumucereleased, breathed out√मुच् (tudādi, 6U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
यान्yānwhichrelative pronoun यद्, m. acc. pl.; agrees with निःश्वासान्; correlative ते in 2.53
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); links the new sentence to the narrative
युध्यमानाyudhyamānāfightingpresent participle (śānac) of √युध् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to fight’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies अम्बिका
रणेraṇein battlem. loc. sg. of रण
अम्बिकाambikāAmbikā, the Motherf. nom. sg. of अम्बिका; subject of मुमुचे

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • चचारासुरसैन्येषु cacārāsurasainyeṣu → चचार + असुरसैन्येषु
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • वनेष्विव vaneṣviva → वनेषु + इव
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    u before the dissimilar i becomes v.
  • यांश्च yāṃśca → यान् + च
    consonant sandhi (n before c) · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Final n before c becomes anusvāra + s, and the s becomes the palatal ś before c: यांश्च.
  • रणेऽम्बिका raṇe'mbikā → रणे + अम्बिका
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरसैन्येषु asurasainyeṣu — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: असुर + सैन्य · Vigraha: असुराणां सैन्यानि, तेषु asurāṇāṃ sainyāni, teṣu
    Literally “among the armies of the asuras”; in the verse: through the ranks of the enemy
  • हुताशनः hutāśanaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: हुत + अशन · Vigraha: हुतम् अशनं यस्य सः hutam aśanaṃ yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose food is what is offered (into the fire)”; in the verse: Agni, fire
    Alternative: Also analysed as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa with अशन as an agent noun, हुतस्य अशनः ‘the eater of the oblation’; both analyses give the same name for fire.

C. Morphology

  • मुमुचे mumuce — The ātmanepada perfect of √मुच्, beside the parasmaipada मुमोच of 2.51: √मुच् is ubhayapadin. The ātmanepada suits an action whose fruit stays with the agent — here, her own breath.
  • युध्यमाना yudhyamānā — Present middle participle of √युध्: the divādi stem युध्य- + -मान (śānac). It is not passive; √युध् is itself ātmanepadin.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सः) वनेषु हुताशनः इव असुरसैन्येषु चचार। रणे युध्यमाना अम्बिका यान् च निःश्वासान् मुमुचे —

(saḥ) vaneṣu hutāśanaḥ iva asurasainyeṣu cacāra | raṇe yudhyamānā ambikā yān ca niḥśvāsān mumuce —

The first line completes the lion’s sentence from 2.51b, with a simile. The second line is a relative clause (यान् … मुमुचे) whose main clause (ते एव … गणाः) is in 2.53.

6Translation

Literalranged among the asura armies like fire in forests. And the breaths which Ambikā, fighting in battle, released —

Readableraged through the asura ranks like a fire through the forests. And every breath that Ambikā breathed out as she fought —

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningTwo images of the Devī’s power multiplying. The lion goes through the army like a forest fire; and the Devī’s very breathing becomes a weapon, as the next verse shows.

Traditional interpretationThe contrast with Mahiṣa is drawn by the text itself: in the next chapter Mahiṣāsura fells the Devī’s troops with the wind of his breath (निःश्वासपवनेन, 3.23). Her breath brings forth an army; his only destroys.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also names her Ambikā (name 295, अम्बिका), ‘the Mother’ — the name the Devī Māhātmya uses most often in its battle scenes.

2.53Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

त एव सद्यः सम्भूता गणाः शतसहस्रशः।
युयुधुस्ते परशुभिर्भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः ॥ ५३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ते | एव | सद्यः | सम्भूताः | गणाः | शतसहस्रशःयुयुधुः | ते | परशुभिः | भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः

3IAST

As recitedta eva sadyaḥ sambhūtā gaṇāḥ śatasahasraśaḥ |
yuyudhuste paraśubhirbhindipālāsipaṭṭiśaiḥ ||

Separatedte | eva | sadyaḥ | sambhūtāḥ | gaṇāḥ | śatasahasraśaḥ
yuyudhuḥ | te | paraśubhiḥ | bhindipālāsipaṭṭiśaiḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तेtethose (breaths)pronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; correlative of यान् (2.52), referring to निःश्वासाः; subject of सम्भूताः
एवevavery, themselvesindeclinable (emphatic particle)
सद्यःsadyaḥat once, instantlyindeclinable (adverb of time)
सम्भूताःsambhūtāḥcame into being, becamepast participle (kta) of सम् + √भू (bhvādi, 1P), m. nom. pl.; active sense, used as the finite verb
गणाःgaṇāḥtroops, hosts (gaṇas)m. nom. pl. of गण; predicate nominative with सम्भूताः
शतसहस्रशःśatasahasraśaḥby hundreds of thousandsindeclinable (शतसहस्र + taddhita -शस् ‘by …s’)
युयुधुःyuyudhuḥfought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada ending (as in 2.48)
तेtethey (the gaṇas)pronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; subject of युयुधुः
परशुभिःparaśubhiḥwith axesm. instr. pl. of परशु (u-stem); instrument
भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैःbhindipālāsipaṭṭiśaiḥwith javelins, swords and paṭṭiśasm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • त एव ta eva → ते + एव
    vowel sandhi (ayādi with y dropped) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    Final e before a vowel other than a becomes ay; the y is dropped and the hiatus remains (त एव).
  • युयुधुस्ते yuyudhuste → युयुधुः + ते
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • परशुभिर्भिन्दि paraśubhirbhindi → परशुभिः + भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After a vowel other than a/ā, the visarga (रु) appears as r before a voiced sound.
  • भिन्दिपालासि bhindipālāsi → भिन्दिपाल + असि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः bhindipālāsipaṭṭiśaiḥ — itaretara dvandva
    Members: भिन्दिपाल + असि + पट्टिश · Vigraha: भिन्दिपालाः च असयः च पट्टिशाः च, तैः bhindipālāḥ ca asayaḥ ca paṭṭiśāḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally “with javelins and swords and paṭṭiśas”; in the verse: three kinds of weapon: the bhindipāla (a short throwing javelin), the sword and the edged spear

C. Morphology

  • सम्भूताः sambhūtāḥ — kta of an intransitive verb, active in sense and standing for the finite verb: ‘(they) came into being’. The predicate गणाः says what they became.
  • शतसहस्रशः śatasahasraśaḥ — The numeral शतसहस्र ‘a hundred thousand’ (शत ‘hundred’ + सहस्र ‘thousand’) with the taddhita -शस्, which makes distributive adverbs from numbers and quantities (शतशः ‘by hundreds’, 2.56). It is indeclinable.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ते एव (निःश्वासाः) सद्यः शतसहस्रशः गणाः सम्भूताः। ते (गणाः) परशुभिः भिन्दिपालासिपट्टिशैः (च) युयुधुः।

te eva (niḥśvāsāḥ) sadyaḥ śatasahasraśaḥ gaṇāḥ sambhūtāḥ | te (gaṇāḥ) paraśubhiḥ bhindipālāsipaṭṭiśaiḥ (ca) yuyudhuḥ |

The main clause answers the relative clause of 2.52 (यान् … ते एव). The second line begins a new sentence whose participle (नाशयन्तः) runs into 2.54.

6Translation

Literalthose very (breaths) at once became gaṇas by hundreds of thousands. They fought with axes, with javelins, swords and paṭṭiśas,

Readablebecame at once her hosts, hundreds of thousands strong. They fought with axes, javelins, swords and halberds,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Devī’s breath becomes an army. She does not summon allies from outside: her own exhalations take form as gaṇas, hundreds of thousands of them, and go into battle with weapons of their own.

Traditional interpretationThe text itself later states the principle behind this: when Śumbha taunts her for relying on others, the Devī answers that she alone is there, and the powers he saw were hers, returning into her (10.5, एकैवाहं जगत्यत्र). The gaṇas here are an early instance: her helpers are her own power.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) likewise gives its Goddess an army that is her own power: name 65, भण्डासुरवधोद्युक्तशक्तिसेनासमन्विता, ‘accompanied by an army of śaktis intent on slaying Bhaṇḍāsura’. The idea is shared; the stories are separate.

2.54Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

नाशयन्तोऽसुरगणान् देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताः।
अवादयन्त पटहान् गणाः शङ्खांस्तथापरे ॥ ५४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

नाशयन्तः | असुरगणान् | देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताःअवादयन्त | पटहान् | गणाः | शङ्खान् | तथा | अपरे

3IAST

As recitednāśayanto'suragaṇān devīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥ |
avādayanta paṭahān gaṇāḥ śaṅkhāṃstathāpare ||

Separatednāśayantaḥ | asuragaṇān | devīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥ
avādayanta | paṭahān | gaṇāḥ | śaṅkhān | tathā | apare

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
नाशयन्तःnāśayantaḥdestroyingpresent participle (śatṛ) of the causative of √नश् (divādi, 4P ‘to perish’; causative नाशय- ‘to destroy’), m. nom. pl.; qualifies ते (the gaṇas, 2.53)

अवादयन्तavādayantasounded, playedcausative of √वद् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to speak’; causative वादय- ‘to make sound, to play’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, ātmanepada
पटहान्paṭahānwar-drumsm. acc. pl. of पटह; object of अवादयन्त
गणाःgaṇāḥ(some of the) gaṇasm. nom. pl. of गण; subject of अवादयन्त
शङ्खान्śaṅkhānconchesm. acc. pl. of शङ्ख; object of the understood अवादयन्त
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject of the understood अवादयन्त

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नाशयन्तोऽसुरगणान् nāśayanto'suragaṇān → नाशयन्तः + असुरगणान्
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a- becomes -o, and the following a is elided (avagraha).
  • शक्त्युप śaktyupa → शक्ति + उपबृंहिताः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i before the dissimilar u becomes y.
  • शङ्खांस्तथापरे śaṅkhāṃstathāpare → शङ्खान् + तथा + अपरे
    consonant and vowel sandhi · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Final n before t becomes ṃs; ā + a → ā. The same pattern as पाशांस्तथापरे in 2.48.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरगणान् asuragaṇān — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: असुर + गण · Vigraha: असुराणां गणाः, तान् asurāṇāṃ gaṇāḥ, tān
    Literally “the hosts of the asuras”; in the verse: the enemy troops — set against the Devī’s own गणाः
  • देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताः devīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥ — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (देवीशक्ति)
    Members: देवीशक्ति + उपबृंहित · Vigraha: देवीशक्त्या उपबृंहिताः; देवीशक्तिः = देव्याः शक्तिः devīśaktyā upabṛṃhitāḥ; devīśaktiḥ = devyāḥ śaktiḥ
    Literally “made strong by the power of the Devī”; in the verse: the gaṇas fight with the strength of the one who breathed them out

C. Morphology

  • नाशयन्तः nāśayantaḥ — Causative stem नाशय- (√नश् with vṛddhi + णिच्) and the present participle ending -अत् (śatṛ): ‘causing to perish’, i.e. destroying.
  • अवादयन्त avādayanta — Imperfect of the causative वादय- ‘to make speak’, the regular verb for playing an instrument. Causatives take either voice; the ātmanepada ending -अन्त is used here.
  • देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताः devīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥ — Its last member उपबृंहित is the kta of उप + √बृंह् ‘to make great, strengthen’: ‘reinforced’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(ते गणाः) देवीशक्त्युपबृंहिताः असुरगणान् नाशयन्तः (युयुधुः)। गणाः पटहान् अवादयन्त, तथा अपरे शङ्खान् (अवादयन्त),

(te gaṇāḥ) devīśaktyupabṛṃhitāḥ asuragaṇān nāśayantaḥ (yuyudhuḥ) | gaṇāḥ paṭahān avādayanta, tathā apare śaṅkhān (avādayanta),

The first line belongs with 2.53b: participle and qualifier of the fighting gaṇas. The second line starts the list of instruments, which continues in 2.55a.

6Translation

Literaldestroying the hosts of the asuras, strengthened by the Devī’s power. (Some) gaṇas sounded war-drums, and likewise others conches,

Readabledestroying the asura hosts, made mighty by the Goddess’s power. Some of the hosts beat the war-drums, others blew the conches,

‘Beat’ and ‘blew’ render the one verb अवादयन्त ‘sounded’ for each instrument.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gaṇas both fight and make music. Their strength is not their own: it is the Devī’s power (देवीशक्ति) that swells them. Drums and conches are the sounds of an army sure of victory.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the same beings destroy and celebrate in one breath. The text does not separate the battle from the festival — 2.55 will call it a ‘great festival of war’.

2.55Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

मृदङ्गांश्च तथैवान्ये तस्मिन् युद्धमहोत्सवे।
ततो देवी त्रिशूलेन गदया शक्तिवृष्टिभिः ॥ ५५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मृदङ्गान् | च | तथा | एव | अन्ये | तस्मिन् | युद्धमहोत्सवेततः | देवी | त्रिशूलेन | गदया | शक्तिवृष्टिभिः

3IAST

As recitedmṛdaṅgāṃśca tathaivānye tasmin yuddhamahotsave |
tato devī triśūlena gadayā śaktivṛṣṭibhiḥ ||

Separatedmṛdaṅgān | ca | tathā | eva | anye | tasmin | yuddhamahotsave
tataḥ | devī | triśūlena | gadayā | śaktivṛṣṭibhiḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
मृदङ्गान्mṛdaṅgānmṛdaṅga drumsm. acc. pl. of मृदङ्ग; object of the understood अवादयन्त (2.54)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
एवevajust soindeclinable (emphatic particle); तथा एव ‘in just the same way’
अन्येanyeotherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. pl.; subject of the understood अवादयन्त
तस्मिन्tasminin thatpronoun तद्, m. loc. sg.; qualifies युद्धमहोत्सवे
युद्धमहोत्सवेyuddhamahotsavegreat festival of battlem. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb, तद् + tasil)
देवीdevīthe Devīf. nom. sg. of देवी; subject of निजघान (2.56)
त्रिशूलेनtriśūlenawith the tridentn. instr. sg. of त्रिशूल; compound (see Samāsa); instrument
गदयाgadayāwith the macef. instr. sg. of गदा; instrument
शक्तिवृष्टिभिःśaktivṛṣṭibhiḥwith showers of spearsf. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मृदङ्गांश्च mṛdaṅgāṃśca → मृदङ्गान् + च
    consonant sandhi (n before c) · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    n before c becomes ṃś, as in यांश्च (2.52).
  • तथैवान्ये tathaivānye → तथा + एव + अन्ये
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi, then savarṇa-dīrgha) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + e → ai (तथैव); a + a → ā (एवान्ये).
  • युद्धमहोत्सवे yuddhamahotsave → युद्ध + महा + उत्सवे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + u → o (महा + उत्सव → महोत्सव).
  • ततो देवी tato devī → ततः + देवी
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • युद्धमहोत्सवे yuddhamahotsave — karmadhāraya (a metaphor, rūpaka: the battle is identified with a festival) containing a karmadhāraya (महोत्सव)
    Members: युद्ध + महोत्सव · Vigraha: युद्धम् एव महोत्सवः, तस्मिन्; महोत्सवः = महान् उत्सवः yuddham eva mahotsavaḥ, tasmin; mahotsavaḥ = mahān utsavaḥ
    Literally “in the battle that was a great festival”; in the verse: the war, celebrated with drums and conches, is a festival for the Devī’s side
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, युद्धस्य महोत्सवः ‘the great festival of battle’; the identifying karmadhāraya fits the drums and conches of 2.54–55.
  • त्रिशूलेन triśūlena — bahuvrīhi
    Members: त्रि + शूल · Vigraha: त्रीणि शूलानि यस्मिन् तत्, तेन trīṇi śūlāni yasmin tat, tena
    Literally “that which has three points”; in the verse: the trident, the gift of Śiva (2.20)
  • शक्तिवृष्टिभिः śaktivṛṣṭibhiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: शक्ति + वृष्टि · Vigraha: शक्तीनां वृष्टयः, ताभिः śaktīnāṃ vṛṣṭayaḥ, tābhiḥ
    Literally “with rains of spears”; in the verse: with volleys of thrown spears

C. Morphology

  • तस्मिन् tasmin — Locative singular of the pronoun तद् (masculine/neuter), with the pronominal ending -स्मिन्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अन्ये च तथा एव तस्मिन् युद्धमहोत्सवे मृदङ्गान् (अवादयन्त)। ततः देवी त्रिशूलेन गदया शक्तिवृष्टिभिः —

anye ca tathā eva tasmin yuddhamahotsave mṛdaṅgān (avādayanta) | tataḥ devī triśūlena gadayā śaktivṛṣṭibhiḥ —

The first line closes the sentence of 2.54b (the verb अवादयन्त is carried over). The second line opens a new sentence whose verb, निजघान, is in 2.56.

6Translation

Literaland others, just so, (sounded) mṛdaṅgas in that great festival of battle. Then the Devī, with the trident, with the mace, with showers of spears —

Readableand others the mṛdaṅga drums, in that great festival of war. Then the Goddess, with trident and mace and showers of spears,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe orchestra is completed — war-drums, conches, mṛdaṅgas — and the battle is named a ‘great festival’. Then the Devī herself takes up her weapons: the trident, the mace and the spear-showers begin the list that runs into 2.56.

Traditional interpretationSeveral of these weapons are the gods’ gifts of 2.20–24: Śiva’s trident, Agni’s śakti, and the sword (2.56) given by Kāla. The armed form the gods made is now at work.

2.56Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

खड्गादिभिश्च शतशो निजघान महासुरान्।
पातयामास चैवान्यान् घण्टास्वनविमोहितान् ॥ ५६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

खड्गादिभिः | च | शतशः | निजघान | महासुरान्पातयामास | च | एव | अन्यान् | घण्टास्वनविमोहितान्

3IAST

As recitedkhaḍgādibhiśca śataśo nijaghāna mahāsurān |
pātayāmāsa caivānyān ghaṇṭāsvanavimohitān ||

Separatedkhaḍgādibhiḥ | ca | śataśaḥ | nijaghāna | mahāsurān
pātayāmāsa | ca | eva | anyān | ghaṇṭāsvanavimohitān

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
खड्गादिभिःkhaḍgādibhiḥwith sword and the restm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
शतशःśataśaḥby hundredsindeclinable (शत + taddhita -शस्)
निजघानnijaghānastruck down, slewनि + √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; subject देवी (2.55)
महासुरान्mahāsurānthe great asurasm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of निजघान
पातयामासpātayāmāsafelled, made to fallcausative of √पत् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to fall’), periphrastic liṭ (perfect with आम्), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
एवevaindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
अन्यान्anyānotherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. acc. pl.; object of पातयामास
