5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • खड्गादिभिश्च khaḍgādibhiśca → खड्गादिभिः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c becomes s, which turns palatal ś.
  • शतशो निजघान śataśo nijaghāna → शतशः + निजघान
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced n becomes -o.
  • चैवान्यान् caivānyān → च + एव + अन्यान्
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi, then savarṇa-dīrgha) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + e → ai; a + a → ā.
  • खड्गादिभि khaḍgādibhi → खड्ग + आदि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • खड्गादिभिः khaḍgādibhiḥ — bahuvrīhi (tadguṇasaṃvijñāna: the sword is itself included)
    Members: खड्ग + आदि · Vigraha: खड्गः आदिः येषां तैः khaḍgaḥ ādiḥ yeṣāṃ taiḥ
    Literally “with those of which the sword is the first”; in the verse: with the sword and her other weapons
  • महासुरान् mahāsurān — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुर · Vigraha: महान्तः असुराः, तान् mahāntaḥ asurāḥ, tān
    Literally “great asuras”; in the verse: the asura champions (महत् takes the form महा- as the first member of a karmadhāraya)
  • घण्टास्वनविमोहितान् ghaṇṭāsvanavimohitān — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (घण्टास्वन)
    Members: घण्टास्वन + विमोहित · Vigraha: घण्टास्वनेन विमोहिताः, तान्; घण्टास्वनः = घण्टायाः स्वनः ghaṇṭāsvanena vimohitāḥ, tān; ghaṇṭāsvanaḥ = ghaṇṭāyāḥ svanaḥ
    Literally “stupefied by the sound of the bell”; in the verse: dazed by the ringing of the bell Indra gave her (2.22)

C. Morphology

  • निजघान nijaghāna — Perfect of √हन्: in the reduplicated form the h appears as j in the reduplicating syllable and as gh in the root (ज-घान), with vṛddhi of the root vowel in the 3rd singular.
  • पातयामास pātayāmāsa — Periphrastic perfect: the causative stem पातय- + -आम् + the perfect of √अस् (आस). Causatives form their perfect only this way.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(ततः देवी त्रिशूलेन गदया शक्तिवृष्टिभिः) खड्गादिभिः च शतशः महासुरान् निजघान। घण्टास्वनविमोहितान् अन्यान् च एव पातयामास।

(tataḥ devī triśūlena gadayā śaktivṛṣṭibhiḥ) khaḍgādibhiḥ ca śataśaḥ mahāsurān nijaghāna | ghaṇṭāsvanavimohitān anyān ca eva pātayāmāsa |

The first line completes the sentence of 2.55b with its object and verb. The second adds a parallel clause with the same subject.

6Translation

Literaland with the sword and the rest, struck down the great asuras by hundreds; and she felled others, stupefied by the sound of the bell.

Readablewith sword and every other weapon, struck down the great asuras by the hundred; and others, dazed by the ringing of her bell, she brought to the ground.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Devī’s own slaughter begins. Even her bell is a weapon: its sound stupefies the asuras before they are felled.

Traditional interpretationThe bell is Indra’s gift, taken from Airāvata (2.22). It returns in the gods’ hymn of the eleventh chapter, which asks that the bell that fills the world with its sound and destroys the daityas’ might protect them from sin, as one guards one’s children (11.27).

2.57Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

असुरान् भुवि पाशेन बद्ध्वा चान्यानकर्षयत्।
केचिद् द्विधा कृतास्तीक्ष्णैः खड्गपातैस्तथापरे ॥ ५७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

असुरान् | भुवि | पाशेन | बद्ध्वा | च | अन्यान् | अकर्षयत्केचित् | द्विधा | कृताः | तीक्ष्णैः | खड्गपातैः | तथा | अपरे

3IAST

As recitedasurān bhuvi pāśena baddhvā cānyānakarṣayat |
kecid dvidhā kṛtāstīkṣṇaiḥ khaḍgapātaistathāpare ||

Separatedasurān | bhuvi | pāśena | baddhvā | ca | anyān | akarṣayat
kecit | dvidhā | kṛtāḥ | tīkṣṇaiḥ | khaḍgapātaiḥ | tathā | apare

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
असुरान्asurānasurasm. acc. pl. of असुर; object of बद्ध्वा and अकर्षयत्, with अन्यान्
भुविbhuvion the groundf. loc. sg. of भू ‘earth’
पाशेनpāśenawith the noosem. instr. sg. of पाश; instrument
बद्ध्वाbaddhvāhaving boundabsolutive (ktvā) of √बन्ध् (kryādi, 9P ‘to bind’)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
अन्यान्anyānotherpronominal adjective अन्य, m. acc. pl.; qualifies असुरान्
अकर्षयत्akarṣayatdraggedcausative stem of √कृष् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to draw, drag’), used in the simple sense; laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun (किम् + चित्), m. nom. pl.; subject
द्विधाdvidhāin twoindeclinable (द्वि + taddhita -धा ‘in … ways/parts’)
कृताःkṛtāḥwere made, were cutpast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do, make’), m. nom. pl.; used as the finite verb
तीक्ष्णैःtīkṣṇaiḥby sharpadjective तीक्ष्ण, m. instr. pl.; qualifies खड्गपातैः
खड्गपातैःkhaḍgapātaiḥby sword-strokesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument with कृताः
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject of शेरते in 2.58

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • चान्यानकर्षयत् cānyānakarṣayat → च + अन्यान् + अकर्षयत्
    vowel sandhi and a consonant joined to a vowel · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā (चान्यान्); the final n of अन्यान् simply joins the following vowel (no doubling after the long ā).
  • केचिद् द्विधा kecid dvidhā → केचित् + द्विधा
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced d; the Gita Press writes it with a virāma and a space.
  • कृतास्तीक्ष्णैः kṛtāstīkṣṇaiḥ → कृताः + तीक्ष्णैः
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s; so too खड्गपातैः तथा → खड्गपातैस्तथा.
  • खड्गपातैस्तथापरे khaḍgapātaistathāpare → खड्गपातैः + तथा + अपरे
    visarga and vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Visarga → s before t; ā + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • खड्गपातैः khaḍgapātaiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: खड्ग + पात · Vigraha: खड्गस्य पाताः, तैः khaḍgasya pātāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “by the fallings of the sword”; in the verse: by sword-strokes coming down

C. Morphology

  • बद्ध्वा baddhvā — Absolutive of √बन्ध्: the nasal drops (बध्), and dh + t → ddh (बद्ध्वा). The binding precedes the dragging.
  • अकर्षयत् akarṣayat — A causative form (कर्षय-) with the meaning of the simple verb ‘dragged’; the causative suffix here adds no ‘make someone do’. The simple imperfect would be अकर्षत्.
  • द्विधा dvidhā — Numeral adverb with -धा: द्विधा ‘in two (parts)’, here with कृताः ‘were cut in two’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अन्यान् च असुरान् पाशेन बद्ध्वा भुवि अकर्षयत्। केचित् तीक्ष्णैः खड्गपातैः द्विधा कृताः। तथा अपरे —

anyān ca asurān pāśena baddhvā bhuvi akarṣayat | kecit tīkṣṇaiḥ khaḍgapātaiḥ dvidhā kṛtāḥ | tathā apare —

First line: absolutive + finite verb with the Devī as subject. Second: a passive participle as verb with an instrumental of means. अपरे opens the sentence completed in 2.58.

6Translation

LiteralAnd binding other asuras with the noose, she dragged (them) on the ground. Some were made in two by sharp sword-strokes; likewise others —

ReadableOthers she bound with her noose and dragged along the ground. Some were cut in two by the keen strokes of her sword; and others —

भुवि may also go with बद्ध्वा (‘bound on the ground’); the readable version joins it to the dragging.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningEach weapon has its own work: the noose binds and drags, the sword cuts in two. The verse begins the catalogue of the asuras’ deaths that runs to 2.64.

Traditional interpretationThe noose is Varuṇa’s gift (2.23). In iconography the pāśa is the binding power that the Goddess holds over those who oppose her.

2.58Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

विपोथिता निपातेन गदया भुवि शेरते।
वेमुश्च केचिद्रुधिरं मुसलेन भृशं हताः ॥ ५८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

विपोथिताः | निपातेन | गदया | भुवि | शेरतेवेमुः | च | केचित् | रुधिरम् | मुसलेन | भृशम् | हताः

3IAST

As recitedvipothitā nipātena gadayā bhuvi śerate |
vemuśca kecidrudhiraṃ musalena bhṛśaṃ hatāḥ ||

Separatedvipothitāḥ | nipātena | gadayā | bhuvi | śerate
vemuḥ | ca | kecit | rudhiram | musalena | bhṛśam | hatāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
विपोथिताःvipothitāḥcrushedpast passive participle (kta) of वि + √पुथ् (causative stem पोथय- ‘to crush’), m. nom. pl.; qualifies अपरे (2.57)
निपातेनnipātenaby the blow, by the downward strokem. instr. sg. of निपात (नि + √पत् + घञ्); instrument
गदयाgadayāwith the macef. instr. sg. of गदा; instrument, beside निपातेन
भुविbhuvion the groundf. loc. sg. of भू
शेरतेśeratelie√शी (adādi, 2Ā ‘to lie’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, ātmanepada
वेमुःvemuḥvomited√वम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to vomit, spew’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun (किम् + चित्), m. nom. pl.; subject of वेमुः
रुधिरम्rudhirambloodn. acc. sg. of रुधिर; object of वेमुः
मुसलेनmusalenaby the club (musala)m. instr. sg. of मुसल ‘pestle, club’; instrument with हताः
भृशम्bhṛśamviolently, hardindeclinable (adverbial acc. of भृश)
हताःhatāḥstruckpast passive participle (kta) of √हन् (adādi, 2P), m. nom. pl.; qualifies केचित्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • विपोथिता निपातेन vipothitā nipātena → विपोथिताः + निपातेन
    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant loses its visarga.
  • वेमुश्च vemuśca → वेमुः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c → s → ś.
  • केचिद्रुधिरं kecidrudhiraṃ → केचित् + रुधिरम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced r. The final m of रुधिरम् becomes anusvāra before m (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

C. Morphology

  • शेरते śerate — Present of √शी ‘to lie’: the root takes guṇa throughout (शे-), and the 3rd plural inserts r before the ending (शे-र-ते). The present here is the narrative (historic) present: ‘they lie’ amid perfects.
  • वेमुः vemuḥ — Perfect of √वम् in the form without reduplication, with e in the root (वेमुः, like पेचुः from √पच्); grammarians allow it for √वम् beside the reduplicated ववमुः.
  • विपोथिताः vipothitāḥ — kta from the causative stem पोथय- of √पुथ् (the causative suffix drops before -इत): ‘made to be crushed’, i.e. pounded flat.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(अपरे) गदया निपातेन विपोथिताः भुवि शेरते। केचित् च मुसलेन भृशं हताः रुधिरं वेमुः।

(apare) gadayā nipātena vipothitāḥ bhuvi śerate | kecit ca musalena bhṛśaṃ hatāḥ rudhiraṃ vemuḥ |

The first sentence has its subject, अपरे, at the end of 2.57. Each clause: participle(s) + instrumental of means + finite verb.

6Translation

Literal(others,) crushed by the blow, by the mace, lie on the ground. And some, struck hard by the club, vomited blood.

Readableothers lie on the ground, crushed by the blows of her mace; and some, struck hard by her club, spewed blood.

निपातेन गदया, two instrumentals side by side (‘by the blow, with the mace’), are rendered as one phrase.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningTwo more weapons, two more deaths: the mace crushes, the musala (club) makes the asuras spew blood. The verse moves into the present (शेरते ‘they lie’), as if the narrator were looking at the field.

Traditional interpretationThe mace (गदा) is among the weapons held by Mahālakṣmī in the Gita Press dhyāna for this carita (… गदेषुकुलिशं …); that the middle carita is presided over by Mahālakṣmī is the liturgical tradition of the pāṭha, not a statement of the Purāṇa.

2.59Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

केचिन्निपतिता भूमौ भिन्नाः शूलेन वक्षसि।
निरन्तराः शरौघेण कृताः केचिद्रणाजिरे ॥ ५९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

केचित् | निपतिताः | भूमौ | भिन्नाः | शूलेन | वक्षसिनिरन्तराः | शरौघेण | कृताः | केचित् | रणाजिरे

3IAST

As recitedkecinnipatitā bhūmau bhinnāḥ śūlena vakṣasi |
nirantarāḥ śaraugheṇa kṛtāḥ kecidraṇājire ||

Separatedkecit | nipatitāḥ | bhūmau | bhinnāḥ | śūlena | vakṣasi
nirantarāḥ | śaraugheṇa | kṛtāḥ | kecit | raṇājire

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun (किम् + चित्), m. nom. pl.; subject
निपतिताःnipatitāḥfell, lay fallenpast participle (kta) of नि + √पत् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to fall’), m. nom. pl.; active sense, used as the finite verb
भूमौbhūmauon the groundf. loc. sg. of भूमि (i-stem)
भिन्नाःbhinnāḥpiercedpast passive participle (kta) of √भिद् (rudhādi, 7U ‘to split, pierce’), m. nom. pl.
शूलेनśūlenaby the spear (śūla)m./n. instr. sg. of शूल; instrument
वक्षसिvakṣasiin the chestn. loc. sg. of वक्षस् (s-stem)
निरन्तराःnirantarāḥwithout a gap, riddled throughm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate with कृताः
शरौघेणśaraugheṇaby a flood of arrowsm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); agent/instrument
कृताःkṛtāḥwere madepast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U), m. nom. pl.; used as the finite verb
केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun, m. nom. pl.; subject
रणाजिरेraṇājireon the field of battlen. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • केचिन्निपतिता kecinnipatitā → केचित् + निपतिताः
    consonant sandhi (nasal before nasal) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t before the nasal n becomes the nasal n (optional; the text takes it). -āḥ then loses its visarga before the voiced bh.
  • केचिद्रणाजिरे kecidraṇājire → केचित् + रणाजिरे
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t → d before the voiced r.
  • शरौघेण śaraugheṇa → शर + ओघेण
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + o → au.
  • रणाजिरे raṇājire → रण + अजिरे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • निरन्तराः nirantarāḥ — bahuvrīhi (with the prefix निर् as first member)
    Members: निर् + अन्तर · Vigraha: निर्गतम् अन्तरं येभ्यः ते nirgatam antaraṃ yebhyaḥ te
    Literally “those from whom (every) gap has gone”; in the verse: so densely covered with arrows that no space was left on their bodies
  • शरौघेण śaraugheṇa — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: शर + ओघ · Vigraha: शराणाम् ओघः, तेन śarāṇām oghaḥ, tena
    Literally “by a flood of arrows”; in the verse: by her storm of arrows (from the bow and quiver Vāyu gave her, 2.21)
  • रणाजिरे raṇājire — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: रण + अजिर · Vigraha: रणस्य अजिरम्, तस्मिन् raṇasya ajiram, tasmin
    Literally “in the courtyard of battle”; in the verse: on the battlefield

C. Morphology

  • भिन्नाः bhinnāḥ — kta of √भिद् with -न instead of -त (d + n → nn): भिन्न ‘split, pierced’.
  • निपतिताः nipatitāḥ — kta of √पत् with the connecting vowel i (पतित). Intransitive, so active in sense: ‘fallen’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

केचित् वक्षसि शूलेन भिन्नाः भूमौ निपतिताः। केचित् रणाजिरे शरौघेण निरन्तराः कृताः।

kecit vakṣasi śūlena bhinnāḥ bhūmau nipatitāḥ | kecit raṇājire śaraugheṇa nirantarāḥ kṛtāḥ |

Two parallel sentences, each with केचित् and a participle for the verb; the second has a predicate adjective (निरन्तराः) with कृताः.

6Translation

LiteralSome fell on the ground, pierced in the chest by the spear. Some were made gapless by a flood of arrows on the battlefield.

ReadableSome, pierced through the chest by her spear, fell to the ground; some, on the field of battle, were riddled through and through by her storm of arrows.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe catalogue continues: the śūla pierces the chest, the arrows leave no space unwounded. निरन्तर, ‘without interval’, pictures bodies covered with arrows from end to end.

2.60Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

श्येनानुकारिणः प्राणान् मुमुचुस्त्रिदशार्दनाः।
केषांचिद् बाहवश्छिन्नाश्छिन्नग्रीवास्तथापरे ॥ ६० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

श्येनानुकारिणः | प्राणान् | मुमुचुः | त्रिदशार्दनाःकेषांचित् | बाहवः | छिन्नाः | छिन्नग्रीवाः | तथा | अपरे

3IAST

As recitedśyenānukāriṇaḥ prāṇān mumucustridaśārdanāḥ |
keṣāṃcid bāhavaśchinnāśchinnagrīvāstathāpare ||

Separatedśyenānukāriṇaḥ | prāṇān | mumucuḥ | tridaśārdanāḥ
keṣāṃcit | bāhavaḥ | chinnāḥ | chinnagrīvāḥ | tathā | apare

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
श्येनानुकारिणःśyenānukāriṇaḥimitating hawks, like hawksm. nom. pl. (stem -अनुकारिन्); compound (see Samāsa); taken with त्रिदशार्दनाः (the same form is acc. pl., see Samāsa alt)
प्राणान्prāṇāntheir life-breathsm. acc. pl. of प्राण (used in the plural for ‘life’); object of मुमुचुः
मुमुचुःmumucuḥgave up, released√मुच् (tudādi, 6U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
त्रिदशार्दनाःtridaśārdanāḥthe tormentors of the godsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of मुमुचुः
केषांचित्keṣāṃcitof someindefinite pronoun (केषाम्, gen. pl. of किम्, + चित्), m. gen. pl.; one word; possessor of बाहवः
बाहवःbāhavaḥarmsm. nom. pl. of बाहु (u-stem)
छिन्नाःchinnāḥ(were) cut offpast passive participle (kta) of √छिद् (rudhādi, 7U ‘to cut’), m. nom. pl.; predicate of बाहवः
छिन्नग्रीवाःchinnagrīvāḥwith severed necksm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of अपरे
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मुमुचुस्त्रिदशार्दनाः mumucustridaśārdanāḥ → मुमुचुः + त्रिदशार्दनाः
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • केषांचिद् बाहवश्छिन्नाश्छिन्नग्रीवास्तथापरे keṣāṃcid bāhavaśchinnāśchinnagrīvāstathāpare → केषांचित् + बाहवः + छिन्नाः + छिन्नग्रीवाः + तथा + अपरे
    consonant, visarga and vowel sandhi · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    t → d before b; visarga → s → ś before ch (twice); visarga → s before t; ā + a → ā.
  • श्येनानुकारिणः śyenānukāriṇaḥ → श्येन + अनुकारिणः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā; likewise त्रिदश + अर्दन → त्रिदशार्दन.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • श्येनानुकारिणः śyenānukāriṇaḥ — upapada tatpuruṣa (second member the agent noun अनुकारिन्, अनु + √कृ + णिनि)
    Members: श्येन + अनुकारिन् · Vigraha: श्येनम् अनुकुर्वन्ति इति (श्येनानुकारिणः) śyenam anukurvanti iti (śyenānukāriṇaḥ)
    Literally “those who imitate the hawk”; in the verse: falling as a hawk drops — said of the dying asuras
    Alternative: अनुकारिणः is also the accusative plural, so it can agree with प्राणान्: ‘they released their breaths, which (flew off) like hawks’. Both constructions are grammatical; this edition takes it as an epithet of the dying asuras and records the other.
  • त्रिदशार्दनाः tridaśārdanāḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (the second member the agent noun अर्दन ‘tormenting’, from √अर्द्)
    Members: त्रिदश + अर्दन · Vigraha: त्रिदशान् अर्दयन्ति इति (त्रिदशानाम् अर्दनाः) tridaśān ardayanti iti (tridaśānām ardanāḥ)
    Literally “tormentors of the Thirty”; in the verse: the asuras, oppressors of the gods (त्रिदश ‘the Thirty’ is a standard name for the gods, from the round number of the thirty-three)
  • छिन्नग्रीवाः chinnagrīvāḥ — bahuvrīhi (feminine ग्रीवा shortened to -ग्रीव at the end of a bahuvrīhi qualifying a masculine)
    Members: छिन्न + ग्रीवा · Vigraha: छिन्ना ग्रीवा येषां ते chinnā grīvā yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those whose necks are cut”; in the verse: beheaded

C. Morphology

  • केषांचित् keṣāṃcit — The indefinite pronoun is the interrogative किम् declined + the particle चित्: genitive plural केषाम् + चित् ‘of some’. The final m is written as anusvāra before c inside this one word.
  • मुमुचुः mumucuḥ — Perfect 3rd plural of √मुच् (weak stem मुमुच्), beside मुमोच (3rd sg., 2.51) and मुमुचे (ātmanepada, 2.52).

D. Anvaya — prose order

श्येनानुकारिणः त्रिदशार्दनाः प्राणान् मुमुचुः। केषांचित् बाहवः छिन्नाः, तथा अपरे छिन्नग्रीवाः (अभवन्)।

śyenānukāriṇaḥ tridaśārdanāḥ prāṇān mumucuḥ | keṣāṃcit bāhavaḥ chinnāḥ, tathā apare chinnagrīvāḥ (abhavan) |

The first line is a complete sentence (subject at the end). The second gives two short clauses with the verb ‘to be’ understood.

6Translation

LiteralThe tormentors of the gods, imitating hawks, released their life-breaths. Of some the arms were cut off; likewise others had their necks cut.

ReadableLike hawks dropping from the sky, the tormentors of the gods gave up their lives. Some had their arms hewn off, and others their heads struck from their necks.

‘Dropping from the sky’ interprets श्येनानुकारिणः ‘imitating hawks’, which the Sanskrit leaves unexplained; see Samāsa for the other construction.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe asuras are called त्रिदशार्दनाः, ‘tormentors of the Thirty’, at the moment they die: the epithet recalls why the battle is fought (2.6–8). The second line goes on cataloguing wounds — arms, necks.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The point of comparison in श्येनानुकारिणः is not stated: it may describe the asuras (falling as hawks drop) or, as an accusative, their departing breaths (flying off like hawks). Both constructions are recorded; the choice is interpretive.

2.61Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शिरांसि पेतुरन्येषामन्ये मध्ये विदारिताः।
विच्छिन्नजङ्घास्त्वपरे पेतुरुर्व्यां महासुराः ॥ ६१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शिरांसि | पेतुः | अन्येषाम् | अन्ये | मध्ये | विदारिताःविच्छिन्नजङ्घाः | तु | अपरे | पेतुः | उर्व्याम् | महासुराः

3IAST

As recitedśirāṃsi peturanyeṣāmanye madhye vidāritāḥ |
vicchinnajaṅghāstvapare petururvyāṃ mahāsurāḥ ||

Separatedśirāṃsi | petuḥ | anyeṣām | anye | madhye | vidāritāḥ
vicchinnajaṅghāḥ | tu | apare | petuḥ | urvyām | mahāsurāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
शिरांसिśirāṃsiheadsn. nom. pl. of शिरस् (s-stem); subject of पेतुः
पेतुःpetuḥfell√पत् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to fall’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
अन्येषाम्anyeṣāmof otherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. gen. pl.; possessor of शिरांसि
अन्येanyeotherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. pl.; subject
मध्येmadhyein the middle, at the waistn. loc. sg. of मध्य
विदारिताःvidāritāḥ(were) torn apartpast passive participle (kta) of the causative of वि + √दॄ (kryādi, 9P ‘to tear’; causative दारय-), m. nom. pl.; used as the finite verb
विच्छिन्नजङ्घाःvicchinnajaṅghāḥwith their legs cut offm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अपरे
तुtuand, butindeclinable (particle of contrast or continuation)
अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; qualifies महासुराः
पेतुःpetuḥfell√पत् (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
उर्व्याम्urvyāmon the earthf. loc. sg. of उर्वी ‘the broad one’, the earth
महासुराःmahāsurāḥgreat asurasm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of the second पेतुः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पेतुरन्येषामन्ये peturanyeṣāmanye → पेतुः + अन्येषाम् + अन्ये
    visarga sandhi; final m before a vowel · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After u the visarga (रु) appears as r before a vowel; the final m of अन्येषाम् simply joins the following vowel.
  • विच्छिन्नजङ्घास्त्वपरे vicchinnajaṅghāstvapare → विच्छिन्नजङ्घाः + तु + अपरे
    visarga and vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Visarga → s before t; u before a becomes v (तु अपरे → त्वपरे).
  • पेतुरुर्व्यां petururvyāṃ → पेतुः + उर्व्याम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    As above, -uḥ before a vowel → -ur.
  • विच्छिन्न vicchinna → वि + छिन्न
    consonant sandhi inside the word (tuk before ch) · छे च (6.1.73)
    After the short i of the prefix, ch becomes cch.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • विच्छिन्नजङ्घाः vicchinnajaṅghāḥ — bahuvrīhi (feminine जङ्घा shortened to -जङ्घ at the end of a bahuvrīhi qualifying a masculine)
    Members: विच्छिन्न + जङ्घा · Vigraha: विच्छिन्नाः जङ्घाः येषां ते vicchinnāḥ jaṅghāḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those whose shanks are severed”; in the verse: hamstrung, their legs cut from under them
  • महासुराः mahāsurāḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुर · Vigraha: महान्तः असुराः mahāntaḥ asurāḥ
    Literally “great asuras”; in the verse: the asura champions

C. Morphology

  • पेतुः petuḥ — Perfect 3rd plural of √पत् without reduplication: the root vowel becomes e (पेत्-), as regularly in roots of the shape C-a-C with an unchanged initial consonant (compare वेमुः, 2.58).
  • विदारिताः vidāritāḥ — kta from the causative दारय- of √दॄ ‘to burst, tear’: ‘torn open’. मध्ये ‘in the middle’ says where: cut through at the waist.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अन्येषां शिरांसि पेतुः। अन्ये मध्ये विदारिताः। अपरे तु महासुराः विच्छिन्नजङ्घाः उर्व्यां पेतुः।

anyeṣāṃ śirāṃsi petuḥ | anye madhye vidāritāḥ | apare tu mahāsurāḥ vicchinnajaṅghāḥ urvyāṃ petuḥ |

Three clauses, each with its own subject: heads fell; others were split; still others fell hamstrung.

6Translation

LiteralThe heads of others fell; others were torn apart in the middle; and other great asuras, their legs cut off, fell on the earth.

ReadableOthers’ heads fell; others were split through the middle; and still other great asuras, their legs hewn away, fell to the earth.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe anatomy of the slaughter continues — heads, waists, legs. The repetition of पेतुः ‘fell’ frames the verse.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the unflinching detail of these verses is not cruelty for its own sake. The text wants the defeat of what oppresses the gods to be total and visible, limb by limb.

2.62Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एकबाह्वक्षिचरणाः केचिद्देव्या द्विधा कृताः।
छिन्नेऽपि चान्ये शिरसि पतिताः पुनरुत्थिताः ॥ ६२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एकबाह्वक्षिचरणाः | केचित् | देव्या | द्विधा | कृताःछिन्ने | अपि | च | अन्ये | शिरसि | पतिताः | पुनः | उत्थिताः

3IAST

As recitedekabāhvakṣicaraṇāḥ keciddevyā dvidhā kṛtāḥ |
chinne'pi cānye śirasi patitāḥ punarutthitāḥ ||

Separatedekabāhvakṣicaraṇāḥ | kecit | devyā | dvidhā | kṛtāḥ
chinne | api | ca | anye | śirasi | patitāḥ | punaḥ | utthitāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एकबाह्वक्षिचरणाःekabāhvakṣicaraṇāḥwith one arm, one eye, one footm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies केचित्
केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun (किम् + चित्), m. nom. pl.; subject
देव्याdevyāby the Devīf. instr. sg. of देवी; agent with the passive कृताः
द्विधाdvidhāin twoindeclinable (द्वि + taddhita -धा)
कृताःkṛtāḥwere made, were cutpast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U), m. nom. pl.; used as the finite verb
छिन्नेchinnecut offpast passive participle (kta) of √छिद् (rudhādi, 7U), n. loc. sg.; with शिरसि in a locative absolute
अपिapieven, thoughindeclinable (concessive particle)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
अन्येanyeotherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. pl.; subject
शिरसिśirasithe headn. loc. sg. of शिरस्; locative absolute with छिन्ने
पतिताःpatitāḥhaving fallenpast participle (kta) of √पत् (bhvādi, 1P), m. nom. pl.; active sense
पुनःpunaḥagainindeclinable (adverb)
उत्थिताःutthitāḥrose uppast participle (kta) of उद् + √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’), m. nom. pl.; active sense, used as the finite verb

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • केचिद्देव्या keciddevyā → केचित् + देव्या
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t → d before the voiced d.
  • छिन्नेऽपि चान्ये chinne'pi cānye → छिन्ने + अपि + च + अन्ये
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa; savarṇa-dīrgha) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    -e + a- → -e’- (avagraha); a + a → ā.
  • पुनरुत्थिताः punarutthitāḥ → पुनः + उत्थिताः
    visarga sandhi (r-visarga) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The visarga of पुनः stands for r and reappears as r before a vowel.
  • बाह्वक्षि bāhvakṣi → बाहु + अक्षि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    u before the dissimilar a becomes v.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • एकबाह्वक्षिचरणाः ekabāhvakṣicaraṇāḥ — bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (बाह्वक्षिचरण)
    Members: एक + बाह्वक्षिचरण · Vigraha: एकः बाहुः एकम् अक्षि एकः चरणः च येषां ते; बाह्वक्षिचरणाः = बाहुः च अक्षि च चरणः च ekaḥ bāhuḥ ekam akṣi ekaḥ caraṇaḥ ca yeṣāṃ te; bāhvakṣicaraṇāḥ = bāhuḥ ca akṣi ca caraṇaḥ ca
    Literally “those whose arm, eye and foot are (each) one”; in the verse: split lengthwise, so that each half has a single arm, eye and foot

C. Morphology

  • छिन्ने chinne — Locative absolute (sati saptamī): छिन्ने शिरसि ‘the head having been cut off’, with अपि ‘even when …’. The participle agrees with शिरसि in neuter locative singular.
  • उत्थिताः utthitāḥ — kta of उद् + √स्था: स्था weakens to स्थि (स्थित), and the s is lost after the prefix उद् (उद् + स्थित → उत्थित).

D. Anvaya — prose order

केचित् देव्या द्विधा कृताः एकबाह्वक्षिचरणाः (अभवन्)। अन्ये च शिरसि छिन्ने अपि पतिताः पुनः उत्थिताः।

kecit devyā dvidhā kṛtāḥ ekabāhvakṣicaraṇāḥ (abhavan) | anye ca śirasi chinne api patitāḥ punaḥ utthitāḥ |

The first sentence explains the compound: cut in two, they were left with one arm, eye and foot. The second sentence continues into 2.63, where the risen trunks fight.

6Translation

LiteralSome, cut in two by the Devī, (were left) with one arm, one eye, one foot. And others, even when the head was cut off, having fallen rose up again.

ReadableSome the Goddess cleft in two, leaving each half one arm, one eye, one foot. And others, though their heads were struck off, fell and rose again —

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe battle turns grotesque: asuras split lengthwise, and headless bodies that fall and stand up again. The next two verses describe these headless trunks (कबन्ध) fighting on.

Traditional interpretationHeadless trunks (कबन्ध) rising and fighting on are a familiar image in epic battle descriptions. Here it marks the asuras’ ferocity: they resist even past death, and it is still not enough.

2.63Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कबन्धा युयुधुर्देव्या गृहीतपरमायुधाः।
ननृतुश्चापरे तत्र युद्धे तूर्यलयाश्रिताः ॥ ६३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

कबन्धाः | युयुधुः | देव्या | गृहीतपरमायुधाःननृतुः | च | अपरे | तत्र | युद्धे | तूर्यलयाश्रिताः

3IAST

As recitedkabandhā yuyudhurdevyā gṛhītaparamāyudhāḥ |
nanṛtuścāpare tatra yuddhe tūryalayāśritāḥ ||

Separatedkabandhāḥ | yuyudhuḥ | devyā | gṛhītaparamāyudhāḥ
nanṛtuḥ | ca | apare | tatra | yuddhe | tūryalayāśritāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
कबन्धाःkabandhāḥ(as) headless trunksm. nom. pl. of कबन्ध ‘a headless body’; in apposition to अन्ये (2.62), subject of युयुधुः
युयुधुःyuyudhuḥfought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada ending (as in 2.48)
देव्याdevyāwith the Devīf. instr. sg. of देवी; instrumental of the opponent with a verb of fighting
गृहीतपरमायुधाःgṛhītaparamāyudhāḥgrasping excellent weaponsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies कबन्धाः
ननृतुःnanṛtuḥdanced√नृत् (divādi, 4P ‘to dance’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject of ननृतुः
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place, तद् + त्रल्)
युद्धेyuddhein the battlen. loc. sg. of युद्ध
तूर्यलयाश्रिताःtūryalayāśritāḥkeeping to the rhythm of the instrumentsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अपरे

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कबन्धा युयुधुर्देव्या kabandhā yuyudhurdevyā → कबन्धाः + युयुधुः + देव्या
    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    -āḥ loses its visarga before the voiced y; -uḥ becomes -ur before the voiced d.
  • ननृतुश्चापरे nanṛtuścāpare → ननृतुः + च + अपरे
    visarga and vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Visarga before c → ś; a + a → ā.
  • परमायुधाः paramāyudhāḥ → परम + आयुधाः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā; likewise लय + आश्रित → लयाश्रित.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • गृहीतपरमायुधाः gṛhītaparamāyudhāḥ — bahuvrīhi containing a karmadhāraya (परमायुध)
    Members: गृहीत + परमायुध · Vigraha: गृहीतानि परमायुधानि यैः ते; परमायुधम् = परमम् आयुधम् gṛhītāni paramāyudhāni yaiḥ te; paramāyudham = paramam āyudham
    Literally “those by whom excellent weapons are grasped”; in the verse: still gripping their finest weapons
  • तूर्यलयाश्रिताः tūryalayāśritāḥ — dvitīyā tatpuruṣa (with the participle आश्रित ‘resorting to’) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (तूर्यलय)
    Members: तूर्यलय + आश्रित · Vigraha: तूर्यलयम् आश्रिताः; तूर्यलयः = तूर्याणां लयः tūryalayam āśritāḥ; tūryalayaḥ = tūryāṇāṃ layaḥ
    Literally “resorting to the rhythm of the instruments”; in the verse: dancing in time to the war-music of 2.54–55

C. Morphology

  • ननृतुः nanṛtuḥ — Perfect of √नृत् with reduplication न- (ṛ is reduplicated as a): न-नृत्-उः.
  • गृहीतपरमायुधाः gṛhītaparamāyudhāḥ — In a bahuvrīhi the participle stands first (गृहीत-, kta of √ग्रह् with i and samprasāraṇa: ग्रह् → गृह् + ई + त).

D. Anvaya — prose order

(ते) कबन्धाः गृहीतपरमायुधाः देव्या (सह) युयुधुः। अपरे च तत्र युद्धे तूर्यलयाश्रिताः ननृतुः।

(te) kabandhāḥ gṛhītaparamāyudhāḥ devyā (saha) yuyudhuḥ | apare ca tatra yuddhe tūryalayāśritāḥ nanṛtuḥ |

The first sentence continues 2.62b (अन्ये … पुनः उत्थिताः): the risen bodies, now कबन्धाः, fight. The second is parallel: others dance.

6Translation

LiteralAs headless trunks, grasping excellent weapons, they fought with the Devī; and others danced there in the battle, keeping to the rhythm of the instruments.

Readableand fought the Goddess as headless trunks, still gripping their finest weapons; and others danced there on the battlefield to the beat of the war-music.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe headless bodies of 2.62 fight on, and some even dance to the rhythm of the drums and conches the gaṇas are playing (2.54–55). The festival of battle (युद्धमहोत्सव) has its macabre dancers.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: what has lost its head can still move to the music of battle. Resistance can go on long after its reason is gone.

2.64Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कबन्धाश्छिन्नशिरसः खड्गशक्त्यृष्टिपाणयः।

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कबन्धाश्छिन्नशिरसः kabandhāśchinnaśirasaḥ → कबन्धाः + छिन्नशिरसः
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before ch → s → ś.
  • तिष्ठेति tiṣṭheti → तिष्ठ + इति
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + i → e.
  • भाषन्तो देवीमन्ये bhāṣanto devīmanye → भाषन्तः + देवीम् + अन्ये
    visarga sandhi; final m before a vowel · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced d → -o; the final m of देवीम् joins the following vowel.
  • शक्त्यृष्टि śaktyṛṣṭi → शक्ति + ऋष्टि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i before the dissimilar ṛ becomes y.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • छिन्नशिरसः chinnaśirasaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: छिन्न + शिरस् · Vigraha: छिन्नं शिरः येषां ते chinnaṃ śiraḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those whose head is cut off”; in the verse: beheaded — restating कबन्धाः
  • खड्गशक्त्यृष्टिपाणयः khaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭipāṇayaḥ — bahuvrīhi (vyadhikaraṇa: the members stand in different cases in the vigraha) containing an itaretara dvandva
    Members: खड्गशक्त्यृष्टि + पाणि · Vigraha: खड्गशक्त्यृष्टयः पाणिषु येषां ते; खड्गशक्त्यृष्टयः = खड्गः च शक्तिः च ऋष्टिः च khaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭayaḥ pāṇiṣu yeṣāṃ te; khaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭayaḥ = khaḍgaḥ ca śaktiḥ ca ṛṣṭiḥ ca
    Literally “those in whose hands are swords, spears and ṛṣṭis”; in the verse: fully armed though headless (ṛṣṭi: a double-edged sword or lance)

C. Morphology

  • भाषन्तः bhāṣantaḥ — √भाष् is ātmanepadin, and its classical participle is भाषमाणाः (śānac). The form with the parasmaipada -अत् (śatṛ) is an epic and Purāṇic usage, like युयुधुः (2.48).
  • तिष्ठ tiṣṭha — Imperative 2nd sg. of √स्था, whose present stem is the reduplicated तिष्ठ-. Doubled, it is the battlefield challenge ‘Stop! Stop!’ — ‘stand and fight’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अन्ये महासुराः छिन्नशिरसः कबन्धाः खड्गशक्त्यृष्टिपाणयः (सन्तः) देवीं ‘तिष्ठ तिष्ठ’ इति भाषन्तः (अभवन्)।

anye mahāsurāḥ chinnaśirasaḥ kabandhāḥ khaḍgaśaktyṛṣṭipāṇayaḥ (santaḥ) devīṃ ‘tiṣṭha tiṣṭha’ iti bhāṣantaḥ (abhavan) |

The verse has no finite verb: the participle भाषन्तः serves as predicate (‘were crying’), or युयुधुः is carried over from 2.63.

6Translation

LiteralOther great asuras — headless trunks, their heads severed, swords, spears and ṛṣṭis in their hands — (were) saying to the Devī, ‘Stop! Stop!’

ReadableOther great asuras, headless trunks with their heads struck off, swords and spears and lances still in their hands, kept crying to the Goddess, ‘Stand! Stand and fight!’

‘Kept crying’ supplies the missing finite verb. The headless shout although they have no heads — the text does not explain it, and the translation does not either.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe grotesque reaches its climax: headless asuras, still armed, challenge the Devī with the warrior’s cry तिष्ठ तिष्ठ. The image shows a defiance that outlives the body.

Traditional interpretationThe challenge ‘Stop! Stop!’ is the formula of epic combat, addressed to an opponent who is withdrawing or must stand and fight. Here it is spoken by the dead to the one who cannot be stopped.

2.65Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

पातितै रथनागाश्वैरसुरैश्च वसुन्धरा।
अगम्या साभवत्तत्र यत्राभूत्स महारणः ॥ ६५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

पातितैः | रथनागाश्वैः | असुरैः | च | वसुन्धराअगम्या | सा | अभवत् | तत्र | यत्र | अभूत् | सः | महारणः

3IAST

As recitedpātitai rathanāgāśvairasuraiśca vasundharā |
agamyā sābhavattatra yatrābhūtsa mahāraṇaḥ ||

Separatedpātitaiḥ | rathanāgāśvaiḥ | asuraiḥ | ca | vasundharā
agamyā | sā | abhavat | tatra | yatra | abhūt | saḥ | mahāraṇaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
पातितैःpātitaiḥfelledpast passive participle (kta) of the causative of √पत् (पातय- ‘to fell’), m. instr. pl.; qualifies रथनागाश्वैः and असुरैः
रथनागाश्वैःrathanāgāśvaiḥby chariots, elephants and horsesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of cause
असुरैःasuraiḥby asurasm. instr. pl. of असुर; instrumental of cause
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
वसुन्धराvasundharāthe earthf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of अभवत्
अगम्याagamyāimpassablef. nom. sg. of अगम्य (nañ + गम्य, the gerundive of √गम् with यत्); predicate with अभवत्
साsāthat (earth)pronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; resumes वसुन्धरा
अभवत्abhavatbecame√भू (bhvādi, 1P), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place); correlative of यत्र
यत्रyatrawhereindeclinable (relative adverb of place)
अभूत्abhūttook place, was√भू, luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
सःsaḥthatpronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies महारणः
महारणःmahāraṇaḥgreat battlem. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of अभूत्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पातितै रथ pātitai ratha → पातितैः + रथनागाश्वैः
    visarga sandhi before r · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), रो रि (8.3.14)
    After ai the visarga becomes r, and r before r is dropped; ai, already long, cannot lengthen further, so पातितै stands alone.
  • रथनागाश्वैरसुरैश्च rathanāgāśvairasuraiśca → रथनागाश्वैः + असुरैः + च
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    After ai the visarga appears as r before a vowel; before c it becomes ś.
  • साभवत्तत्र यत्राभूत्स महारणः sābhavattatra yatrābhūtsa mahāraṇaḥ → सा + अभवत् + तत्र + यत्र + अभूत् + सः + महारणः
    vowel, consonant and visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); खरि च (8.4.55); एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    ā + a → ā (twice); t stays t before t and s; सः loses its visarga before m.
  • नागाश्वै nāgāśvai → नाग + अश्व
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • रथनागाश्वैः rathanāgāśvaiḥ — itaretara dvandva
    Members: रथ + नाग + अश्व · Vigraha: रथाः च नागाः च अश्वाः च, तैः rathāḥ ca nāgāḥ ca aśvāḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally “by chariots and elephants and horses”; in the verse: the three wings of the fourfold army (the fourth is the asuras on foot, named next)
  • वसुन्धरा vasundharā — upapada tatpuruṣa (with the inserted m: वसुम्-धरा)
    Members: वसु + धरा · Vigraha: वसूनि धारयति इति vasūni dhārayati iti
    Literally “she who holds wealth”; in the verse: the earth
  • महारणः mahāraṇaḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + रण · Vigraha: महान् रणः mahān raṇaḥ
    Literally “a great battle”; in the verse: the great battle

C. Morphology

  • अगम्या agamyā — Gerundive (kṛtya) of √गम् with यत् (गम्य ‘to be gone over’), negated with अ-: ‘not to be traversed’, impassable.
  • अभवत् abhavat — Imperfect of √भू, beside the aorist अभूत् in the same line: the two pasts are used side by side in narrative.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यत्र सः महारणः अभूत्, तत्र सा वसुन्धरा पातितैः रथनागाश्वैः असुरैः च अगम्या अभवत्।

yatra saḥ mahāraṇaḥ abhūt, tatra sā vasundharā pātitaiḥ rathanāgāśvaiḥ asuraiḥ ca agamyā abhavat |

Main clause (वसुन्धरा … अगम्या सा अभवत् तत्र) with a relative clause of place (यत्र … महारणः). The instrumentals give the cause.

6Translation

LiteralBy felled chariots, elephants and horses, and by asuras, the earth — it became impassable there, where that great battle took place.

ReadableWhere that great battle was fought, the earth became impassable, strewn with fallen chariots, elephants, horses and asuras.

‘Strewn with’ renders the instrumental of cause (‘by …’).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe camera pulls back from single deaths to the whole field: so many chariots, elephants, horses and asuras lie fallen that the ground cannot be crossed.

Traditional interpretationGRETIL’s Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa carries further verse material near this point that the Gita Press text does not (corrections.json, recension notes). It belongs to the Purāṇa recension, not to the recited 700, and is not added here.

2.66Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शोणितौघा महानद्यः सद्यस्तत्र प्रसुस्रुवुः।
मध्ये चासुरसैन्यस्य वारणासुरवाजिनाम् ॥ ६६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शोणितौघाः | महानद्यः | सद्यः | तत्र | प्रसुस्रुवुःमध्ये | च | असुरसैन्यस्य | वारणासुरवाजिनाम्

3IAST

As recitedśoṇitaughā mahānadyaḥ sadyastatra prasusruvuḥ |
madhye cāsurasainyasya vāraṇāsuravājinām ||

Separatedśoṇitaughāḥ | mahānadyaḥ | sadyaḥ | tatra | prasusruvuḥ
madhye | ca | asurasainyasya | vāraṇāsuravājinām

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
शोणितौघाःśoṇitaughāḥtorrents of bloodm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of प्रसुस्रुवुः
महानद्यःmahānadyaḥ(as) great riversf. nom. pl. of महानदी; compound (see Samāsa); predicate in apposition
सद्यःsadyaḥat onceindeclinable (adverb of time)
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
प्रसुस्रुवुःprasusruvuḥflowed forthप्र + √स्रु (bhvādi, 1P ‘to flow’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
मध्येmadhyein the midstn. loc. sg. of मध्य; governs the genitives
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
असुरसैन्यस्यasurasainyasyaof the asura armyn. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); with मध्ये
वारणासुरवाजिनाम्vāraṇāsuravājināmof elephants, asuras and horsesm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); with मध्ये, specifying असुरसैन्यस्य

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शोणितौघा महानद्यः śoṇitaughā mahānadyaḥ → शोणितौघाः + महानद्यः
    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -āḥ before the voiced m loses its visarga.
  • सद्यस्तत्र sadyastatra → सद्यः + तत्र
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • चासुरसैन्यस्य cāsurasainyasya → च + असुरसैन्यस्य
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • शोणितौघा śoṇitaughā → शोणित + ओघाः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + o → au.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शोणितौघाः śoṇitaughāḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: शोणित + ओघ · Vigraha: शोणितस्य ओघाः śoṇitasya oghāḥ
    Literally “floods of blood”; in the verse: streams of blood that run like rivers
    Alternative: Also a bahuvrīhi qualifying महानद्यः, शोणितम् ओघः यासां ताः ‘great rivers whose flood is blood’ (feminine nominative plural, same spelling). The tatpuruṣa in apposition is the simpler construction; the sense is the same.
  • महानद्यः mahānadyaḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + नदी · Vigraha: महत्यः नद्यः mahatyaḥ nadyaḥ
    Literally “great rivers”; in the verse: rivers in flood
  • असुरसैन्यस्य asurasainyasya — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: असुर + सैन्य · Vigraha: असुराणां सैन्यम्, तस्य asurāṇāṃ sainyam, tasya
    Literally “of the army of the asuras”; in the verse: of the enemy host
  • वारणासुरवाजिनाम् vāraṇāsuravājinām — itaretara dvandva
    Members: वारण + असुर + वाजिन् · Vigraha: वारणाः च असुराः च वाजिनः च, तेषाम् vāraṇāḥ ca asurāḥ ca vājinaḥ ca, teṣām
    Literally “of elephants and asuras and horses”; in the verse: of the beasts and warriors of that army

C. Morphology

  • प्रसुस्रुवुः prasusruvuḥ — Perfect of √स्रु with reduplication सु- (the reduplicated syllable keeps only the first consonant and the vowel); before the vowel ending -उः the root’s u becomes uv: सु-स्रुव्-उः.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तत्र असुरसैन्यस्य वारणासुरवाजिनां च मध्ये शोणितौघाः महानद्यः (इव) सद्यः प्रसुस्रुवुः।

tatra asurasainyasya vāraṇāsuravājināṃ ca madhye śoṇitaughāḥ mahānadyaḥ (iva) sadyaḥ prasusruvuḥ |

One sentence: subject (शोणितौघाः) with a predicate noun (महानद्यः), place (तत्र … मध्ये with genitives), verb (प्रसुस्रुवुः).

6Translation

LiteralTorrents of blood, great rivers, at once flowed forth there, in the midst of the asura army, of elephants, asuras and horses.

ReadableAt once torrents of blood flowed there like great rivers, through the midst of the asura host, among its elephants, warriors and horses.

‘Like’ makes explicit the identification of the torrents with great rivers, which the Sanskrit states by apposition.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe scale of the slaughter is measured in rivers of blood running through the asura army itself — among its own elephants, warriors and horses.

2.67Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

क्षणेन तन्महासैन्यमसुराणां तथाम्बिका।
निन्ये क्षयं यथा वह्निस्तृणदारुमहाचयम् ॥ ६७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

क्षणेन | तत् | महासैन्यम् | असुराणाम् | तथा | अम्बिकानिन्ये | क्षयम् | यथा | वह्निः | तृणदारुमहाचयम्

3IAST

As recitedkṣaṇena tanmahāsainyamasurāṇāṃ tathāmbikā |
ninye kṣayaṃ yathā vahnistṛṇadārumahācayam ||

Separatedkṣaṇena | tat | mahāsainyam | asurāṇām | tathā | ambikā
ninye | kṣayam | yathā | vahniḥ | tṛṇadārumahācayam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
क्षणेनkṣaṇenain a momentm. instr. sg. of क्षण; instrumental of time within which
तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. acc. sg.; qualifies महासैन्यम्
महासैन्यम्mahāsainyamgreat armyn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of निन्ये
असुराणाम्asurāṇāmof the asurasm. gen. pl. of असुर; depends on महासैन्यम्
तथाtathāso, in the same wayindeclinable (correlative adverb of manner, answering यथा)
अम्बिकाambikāAmbikāf. nom. sg. of अम्बिका; subject of निन्ये
निन्येninyeled, brought√नी (bhvādi, 1U ‘to lead’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
क्षयम्kṣayamto destructionm. acc. sg. of क्षय; the second object of निन्ये (‘lead X to Y’)
यथाyathāasindeclinable (relative adverb of manner)
वह्निःvahniḥfirem. nom. sg. of वह्नि (i-stem); subject of the simile (निनयति understood)
तृणदारुमहाचयम्tṛṇadārumahācayama great heap of grass and woodm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object in the simile

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तन्महासैन्यमसुराणां tanmahāsainyamasurāṇāṃ → तत् + महासैन्यम् + असुराणाम्
    consonant sandhi (nasal before nasal); final m before a vowel · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t before the nasal m becomes the nasal n; the final m of महासैन्यम् joins the next vowel; असुराणाम् ends in anusvāra before t (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • तथाम्बिका tathāmbikā → तथा + अम्बिका
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • वह्निस्तृण vahnistṛṇa → वह्निः + तृणदारुमहाचयम्
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महासैन्यम् mahāsainyam — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + सैन्य · Vigraha: महत् सैन्यम् mahat sainyam
    Literally “a great army”; in the verse: the whole vast host mustered in 2.40–47
  • तृणदारुमहाचयम् tṛṇadārumahācayam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a dvandva (तृणदारु) and a karmadhāraya (महाचय)
    Members: तृणदारु + महाचय · Vigraha: तृणदारूणां महाचयः, तम्; तृणदारु = तृणानि च दारूणि च; महाचयः = महान् चयः tṛṇadārūṇāṃ mahācayaḥ, tam; tṛṇadāru = tṛṇāni ca dārūṇi ca; mahācayaḥ = mahān cayaḥ
    Literally “a great pile of grass and wood”; in the verse: dry fuel that fire consumes at once
    Alternative: The dvandva तृणदारु may be taken as samāhāra (a collective, ‘grass-and-wood’) or itaretara (‘grasses and woods’); as the first member of a compound the two cannot be told apart, and the sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • निन्ये ninye — Perfect ātmanepada of √नी: reduplication नि-, and the root ī becomes y before the vowel ending (नि-न्य्-ए). √नी takes two accusatives: the thing led (महासैन्यम्) and the goal (क्षयम्); क्षयं नी ‘to lead to destruction’ means ‘to destroy’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यथा वह्निः तृणदारुमहाचयं (क्षयं नयति), तथा अम्बिका असुराणां तत् महासैन्यं क्षणेन क्षयं निन्ये।

yathā vahniḥ tṛṇadārumahācayaṃ (kṣayaṃ nayati), tathā ambikā asurāṇāṃ tat mahāsainyaṃ kṣaṇena kṣayaṃ ninye |

A correlative simile: यथा (the fire) … तथा (Ambikā). The verb of the simile is supplied from निन्ये.

6Translation

LiteralIn a moment Ambikā so led that great army of the asuras to destruction, as fire (does) a great heap of grass and wood.

ReadableIn a moment Ambikā brought that vast army of the asuras to ruin, as fire consumes a great heap of straw and timber.

‘Consumes’ renders the verb supplied in the simile (‘leads to destruction’).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe summary verse of the battle: the whole army is gone in a moment (क्षणेन), as easily as fire takes dry fuel. The simile returns to the fire-image used of the lion in 2.52.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the asuras’ vast numbers — tens of thousands of chariots, crores of elephants — are only fuel. Size is no defence against what the text presents as the power of the Goddess.

2.68Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

स च सिंहो महानादमुत्सृजन्धुतकेसरः।
शरीरेभ्योऽमरारीणामसूनिव विचिन्वति ॥ ६८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सः | च | सिंहः | महानादम् | उत्सृजन् | धुतकेसरःशरीरेभ्यः | अमरारीणाम् | असून् | इव | विचिन्वति

3IAST

As recitedsa ca siṃho mahānādamutsṛjandhutakesaraḥ |
śarīrebhyo'marārīṇāmasūniva vicinvati ||

Separatedsaḥ | ca | siṃhaḥ | mahānādam | utsṛjan | dhutakesaraḥ
śarīrebhyaḥ | amarārīṇām | asūn | iva | vicinvati

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सःsaḥthatpronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies सिंहः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
सिंहःsiṃhaḥlionm. nom. sg. of सिंह; subject of विचिन्वति
महानादम्mahānādama great roarm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of उत्सृजन्
उत्सृजन्utsṛjanletting outpresent participle (śatṛ) of उद् + √सृज् (tudādi, 6P ‘to let go, emit’), m. nom. sg.; qualifies सिंहः
धुतकेसरःdhutakesaraḥhis mane shakingm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सिंहः
शरीरेभ्यःśarīrebhyaḥfrom the bodiesn. abl. pl. of शरीर; source with विचिन्वति
अमरारीणाम्amarārīṇāmof the enemies of the immortalsm. gen. pl. (stem -अरि, i-stem); compound (see Samāsa); depends on शरीरेभ्यः
असून्asūnthe life-breathsm. acc. pl. of असु ‘vital breath’ (used in the plural for ‘life’); object of विचिन्वति
इवivaas if, as it wereindeclinable (particle of comparison, here of seeming)
विचिन्वतिvicinvatisearches out, picks outवि + √चि (svādi, 5U ‘to gather, pick’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada, from a thematic stem चिन्व- (see Morphology)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • स च सिंहो महानादमुत्सृजन्धुतकेसरः sa ca siṃho mahānādamutsṛjandhutakesaraḥ → सः + च + सिंहः + महानादम् + उत्सृजन् + धुतकेसरः
    visarga sandhi; final m and n · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    सः loses its visarga before c; सिंहः becomes सिंहो before the voiced m; the final m of महानादम् joins the vowel; the final n of उत्सृजन् stands unchanged before dh and is only written together with it.
  • शरीरेभ्योऽमरारीणामसूनिव śarīrebhyo'marārīṇāmasūniva → शरीरेभ्यः + अमरारीणाम् + असून् + इव
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa; final consonants before vowels · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a- becomes -o and the a is elided (avagraha); final m and n (after the long ū, so not doubled) join the following vowels. Inside the compound, अमर + अरि → अमरारि by अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महानादम् mahānādam — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + नाद · Vigraha: महान्तं नादम् mahāntaṃ nādam
    Literally “a great roar”; in the verse: a mighty roar
  • धुतकेसरः dhutakesaraḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: धुत + केसर · Vigraha: धुताः केसराः यस्य सः dhutāḥ kesarāḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose mane-hairs are shaken”; in the verse: tossing his mane (compare धुतसटः, 2.51)
  • अमरारीणाम् amarārīṇām — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: अमर + अरि · Vigraha: अमराणाम् अरयः, तेषाम् amarāṇām arayaḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of the enemies of the immortals”; in the verse: of the asuras

C. Morphology

  • विचिन्वति vicinvati — The classical present of √चि is चिनोति (svādi, stem चिनु-/चिनो-); विचिन्वति is formed as if from a thematic stem चिन्व- (abstracted from forms like चिन्वन्ति ‘they gather’). Such thematized forms are an epic and Purāṇic (ārṣa) usage. The present tense is the historic present.
  • उत्सृजन् utsṛjan — Present participle of उद् + √सृज्: the tudādi stem सृज- + -अत्, nom. sg. m. -अन्; the d of the prefix becomes t before s (खरि च, 8.4.55).

D. Anvaya — prose order

सः च सिंहः धुतकेसरः महानादम् उत्सृजन् अमरारीणां शरीरेभ्यः असून् विचिन्वति इव।

saḥ ca siṃhaḥ dhutakesaraḥ mahānādam utsṛjan amarārīṇāṃ śarīrebhyaḥ asūn vicinvati iva |

Subject with two qualifiers (one a participial phrase), then the verb; इव makes the whole action an ‘as it were’.

6Translation

LiteralAnd that lion, letting out a great roar, his mane shaking, as it were picks out the life-breaths from the bodies of the enemies of the immortals.

ReadableAnd the lion, shaking his mane and roaring mightily, ranged as if he were plucking the very lives out of the bodies of the gods’ enemies.

The readable version puts the historic present into the past and adds ‘ranged’ to carry the image; the Sanskrit has only ‘as it were picks out’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe lion returns (2.51–52). He roars and tosses his mane, and moves among the fallen as if searching out any life still left in their bodies — the last stroke of the battle.

Traditional interpretationThe Devī’s lion is more than a mount in the Devī Māhātmya: it fights in its own right here (2.51–52, 2.68), against Mahiṣa’s generals (3.14), against Dhūmralocana’s army (6.15–19) and against Niśumbha’s forces (9.37). It shares in the work of the Goddess who rides it.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: विचिन्वति is taken as a finite verb (3rd sg. present, thematic ārṣa form). Grammatically it could also be the locative singular of the participle विचिन्वत्, but nothing in the verse agrees with a locative, so that reading does not construe.

2.69Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

देव्या गणैश्च तैस्तत्र कृतं युद्धं महासुरैः।
यथैषां तुतुषुर्देवाः पुष्पवृष्टिमुचो दिवि ॥ ६९ ॥ ॐ

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देव्या | गणैः | च | तैः | तत्र | कृतम् | युद्धम् | महासुरैःयथा | एषाम् | तुतुषुः | देवाः | पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः | दिवि

3IAST

As reciteddevyā gaṇaiśca taistatra kṛtaṃ yuddhaṃ mahāsuraiḥ |
yathaiṣāṃ tutuṣurdevāḥ puṣpavṛṣṭimuco divi ||

Separateddevyā | gaṇaiḥ | ca | taiḥ | tatra | kṛtam | yuddham | mahāsuraiḥ
yathā | eṣām | tutuṣuḥ | devāḥ | puṣpavṛṣṭimucaḥ | divi

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
देव्याdevyāby the Devīf. instr. sg. of देवी; agent of the passive कृतम्
गणैःgaṇaiḥby the gaṇasm. instr. pl. of गण; agent of कृतम्
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); joins देव्या and गणैः
तैःtaiḥthosepronoun तद्, m. instr. pl.; qualifies गणैः (the gaṇas born of her breath, 2.53)
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
कृतम्kṛtamwas made, was wagedpast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U), n. nom. sg.; agrees with युद्धम्; used as the finite verb
युद्धम्yuddhambattlen. nom. sg. of युद्ध; subject of the passive कृतम्
महासुरैःmahāsuraiḥwith the great asurasm. instr. pl.; compound (karmadhāraya, as in 2.61); instrumental of the opponent
यथाyathāso that, in such a way thatindeclinable (relative adverb introducing a clause of result)
एषाम्eṣāmwith them, at thempronoun इदम्, m. gen. pl.; genitive of reference (see anvaya)
तुतुषुःtutuṣuḥwere pleased, rejoiced√तुष् (divādi, 4P ‘to be pleased’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of तुतुषुः
पुष्पवृष्टिमुचःpuṣpavṛṣṭimucaḥshowering rains of flowersm. nom. pl. (stem -मुच्, a root-noun); compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवाः
दिविdiviin heavenf. loc. sg. of दिव् ‘sky, heaven’

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • गणैश्च तैस्तत्र gaṇaiśca taistatra → गणैः + च + तैः + तत्र
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga → ś before c; visarga → s before t.
  • यथैषां yathaiṣāṃ → यथा + एषाम्
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai. The final m becomes anusvāra before t (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • तुतुषुर्देवाः tutuṣurdevāḥ → तुतुषुः + देवाः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After u the visarga appears as r before the voiced d.
  • पुष्पवृष्टिमुचो दिवि puṣpavṛṣṭimuco divi → पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः + दिवि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः puṣpavṛṣṭimucaḥ — upapada tatpuruṣa (second member the root-noun -मुच्, क्विप्) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (पुष्पवृष्टि)
    Members: पुष्पवृष्टि + मुच् · Vigraha: पुष्पवृष्टिं मुञ्चन्ति इति; पुष्पवृष्टिः = पुष्पाणां वृष्टिः puṣpavṛṣṭiṃ muñcanti iti; puṣpavṛṣṭiḥ = puṣpāṇāṃ vṛṣṭiḥ
    Literally “releasers of a rain of flowers”; in the verse: the gods scattering flowers from the sky in celebration
  • महासुरैः mahāsuraiḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुर · Vigraha: महान्तः असुराः, तैः mahāntaḥ asurāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “with great asuras”; in the verse: with the asura champions (the variant तथासुरैः reads ‘and with the asuras’)

C. Morphology

  • कृतम् kṛtam — Impersonal-looking passive: युद्धं कृतम् ‘battle was made’ with the agents in the instrumental (देव्या गणैः च) and the opponents also in the instrumental (महासुरैः). The two instrumentals are told apart by sense.
  • तुतुषुः tutuṣuḥ — Perfect of √तुष् with reduplication तु-: ‘they were gratified’.
  • पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः puṣpavṛṣṭimucaḥ — A root-noun (the bare root √मुच् with the zero suffix क्विप्) as the last member of a compound: ‘-releasing, releaser of …’. Nominative plural -मुचः.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तत्र देव्या तैः गणैः च महासुरैः (सह) युद्धं (तथा) कृतं, यथा दिवि पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः देवाः एषां तुतुषुः।

tatra devyā taiḥ gaṇaiḥ ca mahāsuraiḥ (saha) yuddhaṃ (tathā) kṛtaṃ, yathā divi puṣpavṛṣṭimucaḥ devāḥ eṣāṃ tutuṣuḥ |

Main clause in the passive (युद्धं कृतम् with instrumental agents), followed by a यथा-clause of result. एषाम् ‘of them’ is a loose genitive of reference: the gods were pleased with them (with the Devī and her gaṇas); it can also be taken with पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः, ‘showering flowers on them’.

6Translation

LiteralThere the battle was waged by the Devī and by those gaṇas with the great asuras in such a way that the gods, releasing rains of flowers in heaven, were pleased with them.

ReadableSo the Goddess and her hosts fought the great asuras there, and fought so well that the gods in heaven rejoiced in them and rained down flowers.

‘Fought so well’ makes explicit the result-clause यथा (‘in such a way that’).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe chapter closes on the gods’ response: the battle of the Devī and her gaṇas against the asura champions is such that the gods, watching from heaven, shower flowers. The asura army is destroyed; Mahiṣāsura himself remains for the next chapter (महिषासुरवधः).

Traditional interpretationThe text has a variant here: the Gita Press reads कृतं युद्धं महासुरैः ‘with the great asuras’; GRETIL and Freund read कृतं युद्धं तथासुरैः ‘and likewise with the asuras’ (corrections.json, variants). The sense of the verse is the same. In the Gita Press pāṭha the chapter’s last mantra closes with the bīja ॐ, which belongs to the recitation, not to the Purāṇa.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the chapter of slaughter ends not with corpses but with flowers falling from the sky — the gods’ gratitude. The violence is told as the clearing of the way for what the gods could not do for themselves.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: Variant reading (Textual Verification Required): महासुरैः (Gita Press, kept) against तथासुरैः (GRETIL, Freund). With तथासुरैः the line reads ‘battle was waged by the Devī and her gaṇas, and likewise by the asuras’ or ‘with the asuras likewise’. The construction of एषाम् (with तुतुषुः or with पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः) is also open.

Colophon

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयपुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये महिषासुरसैन्यवधो नाम द्वितीयोऽध्यायः॥

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye mahiṣāsurasainyavadho nāma dvitīyo'dhyāyaḥ||

Chapter conclusion

Narrative summary

The gods have lost heaven to Mahiṣāsura and wander the earth. Their report rouses Viṣṇu and Śiva to anger, and from that anger and from every god the radiance pours out that becomes a woman, the auspicious Śivā. The gods arm and adorn her from their own weapons and treasures; her roar shakes the worlds, and Mahiṣa, rushing to the sound, sees her as wide as the cosmos. His generals and their uncountable armies attack; she cuts down their weapons as if in play, her breath becomes an army, and in a moment the whole host is consumed. The chapter ends with flowers falling from heaven and Mahiṣāsura himself still to be faced (2.69).

Principal vocabulary

SanskritIASTMeaning
तेजःtejaḥradiance, fiery power: what each god gives to form the Goddess
शरणम्śaraṇamrefuge; शरणं प्रपद् ‘to go for refuge’
अधिकारःadhikāraḥoffice, function: the posts of the gods that Mahiṣa seizes
नारीnārīwoman: what the gathered radiance became
शिवाśivāthe auspicious one: the Goddess named as her body is completed
आयुधम्āyudhamweapon
शस्त्रम्śastrama weapon held and wielded
अस्त्रम्astrama missile, a weapon released
सिंहवाहिनीsiṃhavāhinīshe whose mount is the lion
चण्डिकाcaṇḍikāthe fierce one: the Goddess as warrior
अम्बिकाambikāthe Mother: the name used most in the battle
गणःgaṇaḥa host, a troop: the Goddess’s attendant army
लीलाlīlāplay, ease: लीलयैव ‘as if in play’
कबन्धःkabandhaḥa headless trunk still fighting

Important grammatical constructions

  • The perfect as the narrative past, with its reduplications (ददौ, चुक्षुभुः, तुष्टुवुः, निजघान) and the unreduplicated e-perfects (चेलुः, पेतुः, वेमुः).
  • The periphrastic perfect of derived stems: कथयामासुः (2.5), पातयामास (2.56).
  • Participles for finite verbs: पराजितम्, गताः, सम्भूताः; and दत्तवान् ‘he gave’ beside ददौ in the gift list (2.20–31).
  • The instrumental of source: याम्येन … केशाः, विष्णुतेजसा बाहवः (2.14), changing to a predicate nominative in 2.18 (भ्रुवौ … संध्ययोः तेजः).
  • The locative absolute: महिषे असुराणाम् अधिपे (2.2), छिन्ने शिरसि (2.62).
  • The passive imperative as a request to superiors: वधः तस्य विचिन्त्यताम् (2.8).
  • Absolutives in -त्वा without a prefix (जित्वा, दृष्ट्वा, बद्ध्वा) and in -य with one (पुरस्कृत्य, निशम्य, समुत्पाद्य, आभाष्य).
  • Epic (ārṣa) forms beside the classical: parasmaipada युयुधुः (2.46, 2.48) and भाषन्तः (2.64), अददत् (2.29), विचिन्वति (2.68), the genitive of the recipient (तस्याः, 2.24).

Śākta insights

  • The Goddess appears from the radiance of all the gods made one (2.10–13), yet the text has already said she is eternal and only manifests for the gods’ purpose (1.64–66): their powers give her a visible form; they do not bring her into being.
  • Her weapons are drawn out of the gods’ own weapons (2.20, 2.22, 2.23), as her body was drawn out of their light (2.14–18): she bears every god’s power at once.
  • Her helpers are her own power: her breaths become the hosts who fight for her, made mighty by the Devī’s śakti (2.52–54).
  • Her action is play: she cuts down the whole armoury ‘as if in play’, her face unwearied (2.50), and consumes a vast army in a moment (2.67).
  • One manifestation, two responses: the sages bow and praise, the asuras see the worlds in turmoil and arm themselves (2.35–36).

Śrīvidyā connections

There are real, specific parallels with the Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa), noted on the verses: the emergence of the Goddess for the gods’ purpose (4, चिदग्निकुण्डसम्भूता; 5, देवकार्यसमुद्यता; with 2.13 — there from a fire-pit, here from the gods’ tejas); the name Śivā (53, शिवा; 998, श्रीशिवा; with 2.18); the bearer of all weapons (530, सर्वायुधधरा; with 2.20–31); the Goddess raining weapons in battle (79, भण्डासुरेन्द्रनिर्मुक्तशस्त्रप्रत्यस्त्रवर्षिणी; with निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी, 2.50); her army of śaktis (65, भण्डासुरवधोद्युक्तशक्तिसेनासमन्विता; with the gaṇas of 2.53–54); and the names Ambikā (295) and Caṇḍikā (755). The Lalitā Sahasranāma also has the name महालक्ष्मीः (210), the presiding form of this carita in the Gita Press liturgy, though the chapter’s text never uses it. The battles are different stories (Bhaṇḍāsura there, Mahiṣa’s armies here), and these are shared Śākta images and names, not evidence that one text depends on the other; no specifically Śrīvidyā doctrine is stated in this chapter.

Verses for study and memorisation

  • 2.8 — The gods end their complaint: they have come for refuge and ask that Mahiṣa’s death be thought out.
  • 2.13 — The matchless radiance born from all the gods’ bodies gathers in one place and becomes a woman, filling the three worlds with its light.
  • 2.18 — The forming of her body is completed, and the auspicious one, Śivā, arises from the radiance of all the gods.
  • 2.39 — She stands with a thousand arms spread through every quarter, and the battle with the enemies of the gods begins.
  • 2.50 — Raining her own weapons, she cuts theirs down as if in play, her face unwearied, praised by gods and seers.
  • 2.53 — Her breaths become hosts by the hundred thousand and fight with axes, bhindipālas, swords and paṭṭiśas.
  • 2.67 — In a moment Ambikā consumes the great asura army, as fire consumes a heap of straw and timber.

Chapter completion report

Chapter2 — The Slaughter of Mahiṣāsura's Armies (महिषासुरसैन्यवधः)
Source editionŚrīdurgāsaptaśatī, Gita Press, Gorakhpur (Hindi translation by Paṇḍit Rāmanārāyaṇadatta Śāstrī “Rāma”)
Verse range2.1–2.69 (Gita Press mantra numbers)
Verse entries69 entries for 69 mantras — uvāca 1, half-ślokas 0, ślokas 68; the counts equal the chapter's colophon
Analysed69 of 69 entries
NumberingGita Press counts every uvāca line and half-śloka as a mantra; the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa (GRETIL, adhyāya 82) numbers by full ślokas and does not number uvāca lines, so its numbers differ.
Corrections to the digital textNone
Variant readings (kept as printed)2.69: कृतं युद्धं महासुरैः / कृतं युद्धं तथासुरैः (Textual Verification Required)
Uncertain readings or interpretations2.10, 2.17, 2.20, 2.23, 2.24, 2.25, 2.33, 2.42, 2.43, 2.44, 2.45, 2.48, 2.60, 2.68, 2.69
Sandhi patterns
  • Visarga sandhi is by far the commonest: -aḥ → -o before voiced sounds, -aḥ/-āḥ losing the visarga before vowels and voiced consonants, visarga → ś/s before c/t (तस्यास्तु, शम्भुश्च), r-visarga (पुनरुत्थिताः, 2.62), and the special rule for सः (स स्वयम्, 2.6; स ददर्श, 2.37).
  • Savarṇa-dīrgha across words and inside compounds (तत्रासुरैः, देवासुरम्); guṇa, vṛddhi and yaṇ less often; ayādi in ददावशून्यम् (2.30) and, with y dropped, त एव (2.53); āv in श्रवणावनिलस्य (2.18).
  • Pūrvarūpa with avagraha (महिषेऽसुराणाम्, 2.2; सोऽपि, 2.51; रणेऽम्बिका, 2.52).
  • Consonant junctions: voicing of finals, t + ś → cch (यदभूच्छाम्भवम्, 2.14), t + c/j → palatal (केचिच्च, अददज्जलधिः), t + n/m → n (केचिन्निपतिताः, तन्महासैन्यम्), n + c → ṃś (यांश्च, मृदङ्गांश्च), doubling of n (तस्मिन्नयुध्यत, 2.44), and tuk before ch inside words (प्रचिच्छेद, विच्छिन्न).
  • Final m before a vowel is written joined but unchanged (हारमजरे, तामूचुः, त्रैलोक्यममरारयः). No sandhi exceptions are declared in this chapter.
Samāsa constructions
  • Ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas are the commonest (देवसैन्यम्, महिषासुरचेष्टितम्, घण्टास्वन), often nested inside larger compounds.
  • Bahuvrīhis of description, many with a feminine last member shortened before a masculine (धुतसटः, छिन्नग्रीवाः, विच्छिन्नजङ्घाः) and many containing a tatpuruṣa (व्याप्तलोकत्रयम्, किरीटोल्लिखिताम्बराम्, क्षोभिताशेषपातालाम्).
  • Karmadhārayas, including a rūpaka (युद्धमहोत्सवे, the battle as a festival, 2.55).
  • Itaretara dvandvas in the lists of weapons, limbs and forces (शस्त्रास्त्र, जङ्घोरू, रथनागहयैः).
  • Upapada tatpuruṣas with agent nouns and root-nouns (पिनाकधृक्, सिंहवाहिनी, निजशस्त्रास्त्रवर्षिणी, सुरद्विषाम्, पुष्पवृष्टिमुचः); tṛtīyā and nañ tatpuruṣas (घण्टास्वनविमोहितान्, अनायस्त); one avyayībhāva.
Grammatical observations
  • The hypermetric pāda of 2.2 (महिषेऽसुराणामधिपे, nine syllables) is kept as every witness has it.
  • In 2.13 the participles and adjectives stay neuter, agreeing with तेजः, until the predicate नारी: it is the radiance that became a woman.
  • The gift list and the muster-roll run across verse boundaries (2.24–32, 2.40–49); several verses have no verb of their own (2.16, 2.24a, 2.25–26), and the anvaya supplies it.
  • Epic (ārṣa) forms are kept and explained, not corrected: युयुधुः (2.46, 2.48), भाषन्तः (2.64), अददत् (2.29), विचिन्वति (2.68), the genitive of the recipient (2.24).
  • परिवारितः is read as a general’s name in 2.43 but as the participle ‘surrounded’ in 2.45; the numbers अयुत, नियुत and कोटि are explained where they occur (2.41–46).
Philosophical themes
  • Refuge and the gods’ dispossession
  • Tejas made one: the manifestation of the Goddess
  • The arming of the Devī
  • Līlā and the effortless battle
  • The Goddess’s own hosts
Open questions
  • 2.69: महासुरैः (Gita Press, kept) / तथासुरैः (GRETIL, Freund) — Textual Verification Required.
  • Interpretive doubts (not about the reading) are flagged on 2.10, 2.17, 2.20, 2.23, 2.24, 2.25, 2.33, 2.42, 2.43, 2.44, 2.48, 2.60 and 2.68.
  • 2.45 carries the recension note: GRETIL’s Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa has additional verses there (and in 2.20 and 2.65) that the Gita Press 700 does not; nothing was added.
